ChromaCommand Platform — RGB + Menu Streaming IoT (Enhanced)

29 features 6 P0 30 implemented 6 partial 4 absent 0 decisions

ChromaCommand Platform — RGB + Menu Streaming IoT (Enhanced PRD)

1. What this version fixes

  • PRD v1.2-draft §4.1 claimed "Auth / RBAC … ⚠️ Bypassed" and "E2E Tests (15 tests)". The code at 804ccb3 no longer bypasses auth: apps/dashboard/app/components/AuthGate.tsx checks the cc_access_token cookie and redirects to /login; apps/api/src/auth.ts fails fast on a missing JWT_SECRET and gates loginWithEmail() behind NODE_ENV === "development" with password freakazoid. The test suite contains 42 it() cases across 3 files, not 15.
  • PRD v1.2-draft claimed "46 any types" and "84 console.* calls". A fresh grep returns 142 any (40 in apps/api/src, 102 in apps/dashboard/app) and 44 console.* (7 api, 2 dashboard, 35 edge). The old totals were never recomputed.
  • PRD v1.2-draft tech stack said "pnpm workspaces". The repo ships package-lock.json and declares "packageManager": "npm@10.7.0"; pnpm is not used by this repo.
  • SECURITY.md claims the refresh token moved to an HttpOnly cookie named __Host-refresh. The code returns refreshToken in the login JSON and stores only the access token in a JavaScript-readable cookie cc_access_token; the dashboard never persists or sends the refresh token. The doc claim is unverified and contradicted by code.
  • PRD v1.2-draft claimed a plaintext jwt-secret: "dev-secret-change-me" in k8s/secrets.yaml. At 804ccb3 that file holds only the placeholder ${JWT_SECRET} and instructs use of an External Secrets Operator. The committed plaintext secret is no longer present; the remaining leak is the compose fallback ${JWT_SECRET:-dev-secret-change-me} in docker-compose.yml.
  • Depth added in this version: a status ledger with evidence on every row, 32-table domain model with invariants, a sync state machine with arithmetic fan-out, 28 features each with an expected-value acceptance test, a failure-mode table, a role/action matrix, dependency-ordered build stages with boolean exit tests, and an evidence appendix quoting every number.

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a full-stack IoT control platform (Fastify/tRPC API, Next.js 14 dashboard, Node edge gateway, ESP32-S3/Pi5 firmware) that unifies RGB LED zones, digital menu screens, audio zones, sensor telemetry, and sponsor "TakeOver" campaigns behind one command.
  • Who uses it: HQ admins, regional managers, franchisees, technicians, and read-only sponsor viewers (MTN/FNB-style partners).
  • Decision or action it enables: one operator presses "Sync" and every LED zone, screen, and speaker in the targeted stores switches brand mode in one pass, with rollback to the previous preset.
  • Deliberately not: a POS, a generic CMS, a mobile companion app (deferred), and not multi-country in v1 (geo hierarchy exists but only SA data is seeded).
  • The single number that says it works: one sync.transform call returns affectedStores and dispatches to that many stores; the case study records 3 stores transformed in 14 hours versus 3–5 days of manual technician visits (docs/CASE_STUDY.md).

4. Domain model

Entities (one-line purpose)

  • Country / Province / City — geographic hierarchy that geo scopes expand through.
  • Org — tenant boundary for users and presets.
  • User — authenticated principal with role + scope claims.
  • Store — a physical franchise location with geo links and status.
  • LedZone — one addressable LED strip inside a store.
  • Screen — a digital menu display with auto-discovery.
  • AudioZone — a named audio output with volume and status.
  • RgbPreset — a reusable colour/mode/brightness configuration.
  • ContentAsset / ContentTemplate / Playlist — digital menu content and its scheduling.
  • AudioPlaylist — a list of tracks for an audio zone.
  • SyncTransaction — one One-Button Sync invocation with before/after preset.
  • SensorTelemetry — time-series sensor samples.
  • DeviceHeartbeat / Device — unified device inventory and liveness.
  • RefreshToken — server-side refresh token record for revocation.
  • SponsorActivation — billable sponsor TakeOver window.
  • FirmwareRelease / FirmwareDeployment — OTA registry and rollout status.
  • AlertRule / AlertEvent — threshold rule and its fired history.
  • SpotifyAccount / SpotifyAuthState — OAuth credentials and flow nonce.
  • Command — unified command ledger entry with per-device ack state.
  • Scene — named multi-component preset (RGB + content + audio).
  • ActivityLog — audit trail of operator actions.

Tables that carry them (columns that matter)

Table Key columns
users id uuid PK, email varchar(256) unique, role varchar(32), orgId uuid FK, scope jsonb not null default '[]'
stores id varchar(32) PK, regionId varchar(32), countryId/provinceId/cityId FK, status varchar(16) default 'setup'
ledZones id varchar(64) PK, storeId FK, group varchar(32), ledCount int, currentColour varchar(7), currentMode varchar(32), maxBrightness double, status varchar(16)
screens id varchar(64) PK, storeId FK, macAddress varchar(17) unique, status varchar(16) default 'offline', firmwareVersion varchar(32)
audioZones id varchar(64) PK, storeId FK, zoneType varchar(32), volume double default 0.5, status varchar(16)
rgbPresets id uuid PK, colours jsonb not null, mode varchar(32), brightness double, speed double, isGlobal bool
contentAssets id uuid PK, type varchar(32), variables jsonb, priority int default 100, validFrom/validUntil timestamptz
syncTransactions commandId varchar(64) PK, scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(32), presetIdBefore/After uuid, startedAt/completedAt timestamptz, ackState jsonb
sensorTelemetry id bigserial PK, storeId FK, sensorId varchar(64), metric varchar(32), value double, recordedAt timestamptz
refreshTokens jti varchar(64) PK, userId FK, expiresAt timestamptz, revokedAt timestamptz, replacedByJti varchar(64)
sponsorActivations id uuid PK, sponsorName varchar(64), presetId FK, scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(32), startedAt/endedAt timestamptz, affectedStores int, ratePerImpressionCents int default 5
firmwareDeployments id uuid PK, releaseId FK, scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(32), status varchar(16) default 'pending', totalDevices/successCount/failureCount int
alertRules id uuid PK, metric varchar(32), comparator varchar(8), threshold double, sustainedMinutes int, scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(32), severity varchar(16), active bool
commands commandId varchar(96) PK, kind varchar(48), scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(64), targets jsonb, ackState jsonb, status varchar(16) default 'dispatched', retries int default 0
scenes id uuid PK, name varchar(64), presetId FK, contentPlaylistId FK, audioPlaylistId FK, transitionMs int default 3000, isGlobal bool default true
spotifyAccounts id uuid PK, scope varchar(16), targetId varchar(32), accessToken text not null, refreshToken text not null, expiresAt timestamptz, active bool

Indexes that decide performance

  • sensorTelemetry: idx_telemetry_store_metric_time (storeId, metric, recordedAt) and idx_telemetry_recorded_at (recordedAt).
  • devices: idx_devices_store (storeId), idx_devices_status (status).
  • commands: idx_commands_created (createdAt).

Invariants

  • I1 Scope claims are strings of form level:id (or *); every mutating router must call requireScope with scopeFromRequest and never trust a raw target id alone.
  • I2 A commandId is globally unique (primary key on commands and syncTransactions); dispatch is idempotent by construction because each call mints a new id.
  • I3 Refresh tokens are single-use: refresh marks the presented jti revokedAt and inserts a replacement; a revoked jti triggers full-chain revocation for that user.
  • I4 resolveStoreTargets is the only fan-out primitive; global expands to every store, country/province/region/city expand via FK columns, store returns one.
  • I5 syncTransactions.presetIdBefore is the most recent prior presetIdAfter for the same targetId; rollback is only possible when it is non-null.
  • I6 Sponsor billing: a sponsor preset opens a sponsorActivations row and closes the prior open row for the same target; endedAt is set on replacement.
  • I7 Firmware deployment status rolls forward only when successCount + failureCount >= totalDevices; partial success is partial, not success.
  • I8 rgbPresets.colours is jsonb not null; ledZones.currentColour is varchar(7) and must hold a #RRGGBB value.

5. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Engine: sync.transform in apps/api/src/routers/sync.ts, fanned out by resolveStoreTargets in apps/api/src/targets.ts and dispatched by publishCommand in apps/api/src/mqtt.ts.

States

requested → authorised (requireScope) → resolved (target expansion) → recorded (syncTransactions + commands rows) → dispatched (MQTT publish per store) → acked (per-device command/ack) → complete | partial | failed. Optional terminal: rolled_back (new syncTransactions row, original untouched).

Pure function at the core

resolveStoreTargets({ scope, targetId }) → { scope, targetId, storeIds: string[] }

  • store: [targetId]
  • global: all rows in stores
  • country: rows where countryId === targetId
  • province: rows where provinceId === targetId
  • region/city: rows where cityId === targetId OR regionId === targetId

Arithmetic of a sync: affectedStores = storeIds.length; estimatedCompleteAt = now + fadeDurationMs; MQTT messages per store = (rgb ? 1 : 0) + (content ? 1 : 0) + (audio ? 1 : 0), so a full TakeOver of 3 stores emits 9 messages.

Edge cases enumerated

Edge case Behaviour Test
Empty store set (target matches no rows) storeIds = []; affectedStores = 0; command row still inserted; no MQTT publish targets.test.ts covers resolveStoreTargets for unknown scope
Unknown scope level resolveStoreTargets throws Unknown scope level: … targets.test.ts
Concurrency (two transforms same target) both insert syncTransactions; presetIdBefore reads the latest prior row; last writer wins on ledZones not tested — race accepted, no lock
Stale version (firmware) OTA acks accumulate; status rolls forward only at successCount + failureCount >= totalDevices firmware ack path in mqtt.ts
Withdrawal (sponsor) a new sponsor preset closes the prior open sponsorActivations row for the same targetId and opens a new one not tested
Exhaustion (queue) pendingQueue capped at 5000 entries during broker outage; oldest dropped mqtt.ts pendingQueue.length > 5000
Self-approval identify checks satisfiesScopes(user.scope, [store:${zone.storeId}]) before dispatch; non-owner gets FORBIDDEN e2e.test.ts franchisee scope test
Duplicate command commandId PK prevents duplicate ledger rows; MQTT duplicate handled by edge dedup refresh-rollback.test.ts covers ack state
Rollback with no prior preset throws "No prior preset to roll back to (this was the first sync for this target)." not tested
Empty components (all false) transform still records and returns affectedStores; zero MQTT messages not tested
Region scope that is actually a city slug sync.transform filters stores.regionId === targetId only; city-scoped stores with regionId != targetId are missed known gap (see §7 F9)

F6 Refresh-token rotation + reuse detection P0 Stolen refresh token must not persist Reusing a revoked refresh token returns "Refresh token reuse detected — all sessions revoked" and revokes the chain
F7 Command ledger with per-device ack tracking P1 Closes dispatch→ack loop commands.get after ack returns ackState[deviceId].status === "acked" and status === "complete" when all devices ack
F8 Geo fan-out for RGB commands P1 One command must reach a whole region rgb.set with scope=region, targetId=cape-town returns targets equal to the count of stores with cityId='cape-town' OR regionId='cape-town'

F13 OTA firmware releases + deployments P1 Field updates without site visits Creating a firmwareReleases row and deploying returns status="pending"; acks roll it to success/partial/failed
F14 Edge gateway offline resilience P1 Stores must survive broker outage With cloud broker down, gateway queues QoS1 commands and flushes on reconnect; sensor samples buffer in SQLite
F15 WebSocket live updates P1 Dashboard must reflect state without refresh A rgb.set triggers a rgb_update WS message to connected clients for each affected store
F16 Sponsor billing hooks P1 Sponsor revenue is the GTM case A sync using a preset whose name matches `mtn

7. Architecture and API surface

Package layout and import rule

chromacommand-platform/
  apps/
    api/          # Fastify + tRPC; only writer to the database
    dashboard/    # Next.js 14; calls API via /api/trpc rewrite
    edge-gateway/ # Node; MQTT bridge + SQLite cache
  packages/
    database/     # Drizzle schema + migrations + seed
    shared/       # tRPC router type (AppRouter) + zod schemas
  firmware/       # ESP32-S3 / Pi5 / audio node
  docker/         # Dockerfiles + mosquitto.conf
  k8s/            # manifests

Import rule that keeps it honest: apps/* may import packages/*; packages/* must never import apps/*; apps/dashboard must never import packages/database directly; only apps/api (and seed/migrate scripts) touch the database.

Endpoint / command table

Surface Count Auth
tRPC routers in appRouter 16 + health mixed (see docs/API.md)
tRPC procedures 106 (46 query, 60 mutation) per procedure
auth 5 login/refresh public; rest protected
stores 2 protected
rgb 9 protected + requireScope/requireRole
content 12 protected
audio 7 protected
sync 3 requireScope on transform/rollback
analytics 3 protected
sponsor 4 protected
telemetry 5 protected
schedules 5 protected
firmware 5 protected
alerts 7 protected
spotify 10 protected
hierarchy 7 protected
scenes 4 protected
commands 2 protected
REST: /healthz, /readyz, /metrics, /live/ws, /provision/*, /spotify/callback 6 groups /healthz and health.ping public; rest gated

Conventions

  • Pagination: limit clamped to 1–200 (listCommands uses Math.min(limit ?? 50, 200)); recent syncs clamp 1–100.
  • Idempotency: every mutating command mints a commandId (prefix_timestamp_random) and records it as a primary key; retries that reuse an id are rejected by the database.
  • Versioning: health.ping returns { status: "ok", version: "1.2.0" }; no API version prefix beyond /api/trpc.
  • Errors: tRPC error codes (UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, BAD_REQUEST) with messages; REST provisioning returns HTTP 4xx with { error }.

8. Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Missing JWT_SECRET at boot API exits on startup Fail-fast throw in auth.ts:25-27 operator / CI
Dev password used in production Anyone with a seeded email logs in loginWithEmail returns null unless NODE_ENV === "development" (auth.ts:79-84) code review + e2e test F3
Broker down at API start Commands queue; "offline" in logs initMqtt wrapped in try/catch (apps/api/src/index.ts:72-77); pendingQueue (cap 5000, mqtt.ts:14,92-93) operator
Broker down at edge Local cache serves last state; sensors buffer in SQLite clean:false session + SQLite buffer (gateway.js:111,171) edge healthcheck
Transform of unknown target affectedStores = 0, command row still inserted none — accepted; no validation that target exists accepted risk
Concurrent transforms same target last writer wins on ledZones; presetIdBefore may be stale none — no lock accepted risk
OTA partial failure deployment status = "partial" ack accumulation in mqtt.ts:285-307 firmware path operator via dashboard
Refresh-token reuse all user sessions revoked, 401 reuse detection in routers/auth.ts:140-149 refresh security event log
Scope bypass attempt FORBIDDEN requireScope (auth.ts:118) + satisfiesScopes (targets.ts:111) with geo expansion e2e test F5
Settings toggles not persisted UI shows static values none — no mutation wired accepted gap (F21)
K8s API manifest missing cannot deploy API via the provided manifests none — manifest mislabeled accepted risk (see F9 build stage)
No Postgres backup data loss on volume failure none accepted risk until Stage D

9. Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals

Role Scope claim Typical principal
hq_admin ["*"] Ricardo / Infinity Brands
regional_manager ["region:cape-town"] (geo) regional ops
franchisee ["store:pp-a01"] store owner
sponsor_viewer ["*"] read-only MTN/FNB marketing
technician ["*"] field tech

Role / action matrix (selected)

Action hq_admin regional_manager franchisee sponsor_viewer technician
rgb.set any scope yes own geo own store no yes
rgb.blackout yes own geo own store no yes
sync.transform yes own geo own store no no
sync.rollback yes own geo own store no no
create/delete preset yes create only no no no
firmware deploy yes no no no yes
alerts rules yes own geo no no read
stores.list/get yes own geo own store no yes
sponsor read yes yes no yes no

What is stored

  • Users, stores, zones, content, schedules, telemetry, activity log, refresh tokens, sponsor activations, firmware releases/deployments, alerts, Spotify accounts (plaintext tokens), commands, scenes, geo hierarchy.
  • Access token: JavaScript-readable cookie cc_access_token (max-age 86400s, SameSite=Strict). Refresh token: returned in JSON and not persisted by the dashboard client.

Anchored outside the system

  • activityLog rows are written on every mutating operator action (sync, rgb set/blackout, screen discovery, edge provisioning).
  • syncTransactions records before/after preset per transform.
  • commands records dispatch + per-device ack state.
  • sponsorActivations records billable windows.
  • refreshTokens records issued/revoked tokens.

Honest statement of signature / approval strength

  • Authentication uses symmetric JWT (HS256) signed with process.env.JWT_SECRET; strength depends entirely on the secret's entropy and rotation, which is manual.
  • There is no asymmetric signing, no mTLS on the API, and no external identity provider despite supertokens-node being declared as a dependency.
  • loginWithEmail is a dev-mode shared password (freakazoid), not a production credential store; production must replace it.
  • Edge provisioning issues a staging "cert envelope" (buildSelfSignedClientCert), not a real X.509 certificate; production mTLS is not implemented.
  • The frontend stores the access token in a JavaScript-readable cookie, so any XSS can steal it; the SECURITY.md claim of an HttpOnly refresh cookie is not implemented in code.
  • @fastify/helmet is installed and configured with CSP/HSTS, which is the one production-grade control present.

10. Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — Verify. Write the facts file and status ledger; confirm the PRD's claims against the code. Exit test: facts/chromacommand-platform.md exists and every ledger row cites file:line or a command output.
  • Stage B — Security core (parallel: F1, F2, F3, F4, F6). Remove dev password from production path, fail-fast JWT, wire rate limiting, implement refresh-token persistence on the client. Exit test: F1–F6 acceptance tests pass and grep -rn "dev-secret-change-me" . returns only the compose fallback line.
  • Stage C — Data integrity and audit (parallel: F7, F9, F16, F27). Fix sync scope coverage, close sponsor billing impressions, add ack timeout sweeper. Exit test: F7, F9, F16, F27 acceptance tests pass.
  • Stage D — Production infrastructure (parallel: K8s manifests, HPA, Postgres backup, MQTT auth, image digests). Exit test: kubectl apply --dry-run=client succeeds for all manifests in k8s/ and an API Deployment exists.
  • Stage E — UX and accessibility (parallel: F18, F19, F20, F21). Exit test: F18–F21 acceptance tests pass and settings toggles are keyboard-operable.
  • Stage F — GTM and documentation (parallel: F22–F25, F29). Exit test: landing page, case study, runbook, API docs and security policy all present and linked from README.

11. Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Should the dev-mode shared password be removed entirely or kept behind an explicit env flag? Keep behind ALLOW_DEV_AUTH=1 and default off; remove the freakazoid literal. Removing entirely breaks local dev ergonomics; keeping it risks a prod leak if the flag is mis-set.
2 Should the access token move to an HttpOnly cookie now, or stay in the JS cookie until the refresh flow is wired? Move to HttpOnly Secure SameSite=Strict cookie served by the API and wire the refresh token at the same time (Stage B). Keeping JS cookie leaves XSS account-takeover open; moving only the access token without refresh breaks 15-minute sessions.
3 Should sponsor impressions be computed at invoice time from sensorTelemetry, or tracked live? Compute at invoice time from telemetry + sponsorActivations windows. Live tracking adds write load and drift; invoice-time is simpler and auditable.
4 Should firmware OTA be a hard gate before any GTM work? Yes — ship no sales demo without a verified OTA rollback. Skipping risks bricking field devices; doing it first delays revenue by one stage.

Constellation — deep enhancement programme (enhanced)

1. What this version fixes

  • The source PRD (docs/PRD-Constellation-enhancement-2026-10-30.md) cites commit e9aff0d (29 Oct 2026); the repo I read is at 357303d (31 Oct 2026, +8 commits). based_on_commit here is the commit actually read. Any claim about files that moved in those 8 commits is flagged UNVERIFIED.
  • The source's esoteric read-path census counts 13 modules (E32); a fresh grep -rln over app lib components returns 16 (list in the fact file and §5). The census test (WS-1) must be built against the 16, not the 13.
  • The source counts 151 unit test files; the filesystem holds 155. The source counts 983 tracked files; git ls-files returns 994. Counts in this document use the verified values.
  • The source's "33 route groups" is not reproducible by a top-level directory count (ls app/ = 18 directories). Left as the source's claim; marked UNVERIFIED in the fact file. Route-level evidence in §8 uses the actual find app/api -name route.ts = 5.
  • The source's phase timings ("2–3 weeks") are converted to dependency stages with boolean exit tests; no dates appear anywhere in this document (standard §0.2).
  • Depth added where the source was thin: a domain model with column types and indexes (§5), the scoring/fit engine as a state machine with arithmetic and edge cases (§6), a failure-mode table (§9), a role/action matrix and audit record (§10), and an evidence appendix listing every command run (§13). The source had none of these as structured tables.

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a self-hosted people-assessment platform (Next.js 15 + Prisma + PostgreSQL 16) that runs sectioned psychometric batteries, computes deterministic position-fit, team coverage/synergy and constellation maps, and keeps an opt-in esoteric/reflective layer structurally isolated from every decision path.
  • Who uses it: staff (self-view of their own results), managers (team/placement surfaces, scoped to their departments), HR admins (campaigns, data rights, erasure, runs), platform owners (tenants, blueprints).
  • What decision/action it exists to enable: defensible staffing and development conversations — where a person fits, how a team is composed, what a company's shape looks like, and whether a number can be traced to its inputs.
  • What it is deliberately not: not a payroll or performance-management system; not a hiring portal; not an AI-narrative generator; not a multi-language product (instrument validity is English-only).
  • The single number that says it works: MIN_GROUP = 0 is already shipped and every ranked surface renders at every group size, with consent, tenant and role rules enforced independently of headcount — i.e. the privacy floor no longer hides results; the remaining work is provenance, encryption and scheduling (this programme).

Invariants (numbered, so a future feature cannot quietly break them)

  • I1. No module under lib/esoteric/ may be imported by lib/scoring/ or any fit/report-decision path. Enforced by tests/esoteric/isolation.test.ts and tests/fit/isolation.test.ts (fit outputs byte-identical with/without esoteric data). A new reader without consent/policy/audit wiring is a regression.
  • I2. Score rows are append-only: a recompute never overwrites; it appends a new row with isCurrent = true and retires the previous (isCurrent = false) with trigger and operator recorded. Enforced by schema comment at prisma/schema.prisma:288–310 and the recompute audit script.
  • I3. AuditLog is never deleted, even during erasure (WS-5). Erasure deletes person-derived rows only; audit rows for the person remain.
  • I4. Response has a unique constraint on (sessionId, itemId); this is the idempotency key for answer saves (WS-8). No second write to the same (sessionId, itemId) may create a duplicate.
  • I5. Tenant scoping: every read and write resolves through the request-scoped client; raw Prisma client access is banned by ESLint (zero exceptions). Cross-org writes are rejected with the existing "not in your company" refusal (no existence oracle).
  • I6. Consent is enforced at the write path, not only the read path (A02, v1.1). A save against an area with no consent grant is refused and writes nothing.
  • I7. The esoteric framing sentence is single-sourced in lib/ui/terms.ts and rendered verbatim on every reflective surface: "Reflective layer — for conversation and coaching. Not used in any placement, promotion or hiring decision."
  • I8. MIN_GROUP = 0 (owner decision 28 Oct 2026). Reports render at every group size; access, tenant and consent rules apply independently of headcount. Do not re-introduce a floor without a recorded owner decision.
  • I9. db:reset refuses to run against a non-disposable database (E44). No verification script resets anything.
  • I10. Every migration has a down direction rehearsed on a fixture; destructive migrations (column drops) ship separately from dual-write migrations.

5. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Constellation has two pure engines: scoring (raw → z → T) and fit (band/tolerance/weights → fit score, coverage, synergy, axes). Both live in lib/scoring/ and lib/fit/ and are pure functions with unit tests.

Scoring state machine

States of a Session: IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETE (terminal). A session is resumable: cursor records position; answers are upserted by (sessionId, itemId).

Transitions:

  • start(staffId, batteryVersion) → creates Session with status = IN_PROGRESS, cursor = { section: first, index: 0 }.
  • answer(sessionId, itemId, value) → upserts Response; updates cursor; refuses if status = COMPLETE (A04).
  • complete(sessionId) → sets status = COMPLETE, completedAt = now(); triggers Score computation (raw subscale → z → T clamp [20,80], normVersion recorded).
  • recompute(sessionId, trigger, operator) → appends new Score rows with isCurrent = true, retires prior with isCurrent = false (I2). Never deletes.

Pure function computeScore(raw, normSet):

  • Input: raw: number (subscale sum), normSet: { mean, sd, version }.
  • Output: { z: number, t: number (clamped [20,80]), band: string, normVersion: string }.
  • z = (raw - mean) / sd; t = 50 + 10*z; clamp to [20,80].

Edge cases:

  • Empty set (no responses): complete refuses; no Score rows written; session stays IN_PROGRESS.
  • Stale version (norm set changed mid-sitting): scores carry the normVersion actually used; recompute appends a revision; history preserved.
  • Withdrawal (consent revoked mid-sitting): answer save refused at write path (I6); existing answers remain; no silent loss (WS-8 outbox).
  • Exhaustion (all items answered): complete allowed; idempotent — re-calling complete on an already-complete session is a no-op (returns existing).
  • Self-approval: not applicable to scoring; applies to data requests (single-use settlement, different actor).
  • Duplicate (re-POST of same answer batch): deduped on (sessionId, itemId); returns 200 with duplicates: n (WS-8).
  • Concurrency (save + complete racing): serialised by transaction + state guard; exactly one outcome (A04).

Fit engine

Pure function computeFit(person, position):

  • Inputs: person T-scores per dimension T_d, position bands [lo_d, hi_d], weights w_d > 0, tolerance tol_d (default 15).
  • s_d = 1 if lo_d ≤ T_d ≤ hi_d; else s_d = max(0, 1 − dist(T_d, band)/tol_d) where dist is distance to nearest band edge.
  • fit = round(100 · Σ(w_d·s_d) / Σ(w_d)).
  • Flags per dimension: strength if s_d ≥ 0.9, watch if 0.5 ≤ s_d < 0.9, gap if < 0.5.

Move recommendations: for each person, compute fit against all positions in their venture; if bestFit − currentFit ≥ 15 → reposition candidate (top 3 alternatives with driving dimensions).

Coverage & gaps (per department): capability scores from mapped dimensions (member capability = mean of mapped z→[0,1] via clamp((T−20)/60)). coverage_c = max over members. gap_c = max(0, required_c − coverage_c); report gaps > 0.25 ranked by required_c · gap_c.

Balance & synergy: diversityIndex = mean pairwise Euclidean distance across members in 5-dim Big Five z-space, normalised 0–1. balanceTerm = 1 − min(1, |diversityIndex − 0.5| / 0.5) (peaks at moderate diversity). synergy = round(100 · (0.6 · meanCoverage + 0.4 · balanceTerm)).

Constellation axes (deterministic, from z-scores): X = (z_E + z_A + z_SOCIAL) / 3 (people-orientation); Y = (z_C + z_CONVENTIONAL − z_OPENNESS) / 3 (structure-orientation). Star size = fit to selected position; colour = fit band. Position ideals render as target zones.

Edge cases:

  • Person with missing dimension: dimension excluded from sums (weight renormalised); documented in the drawer.
  • Single-member department: diversityIndex = 0 (no pairs); balanceTerm = 1 − min(1, 0.5/0.5) = 0? — actually with 0 pairs the diversity is defined as 0 and balance peaks handling is documented; I1 coverage still renders (MIN_GROUP = 0).
  • Empty department: coverage all zero, gaps = required, synergy 0; renders empty-state, not suppressed.
  • Concurrency (fit run during a sitting): fit runs are append-only labelled runs; a new run does not mutate prior PositionFit rows. Latest-with-warning shown for stale roster.

7. Architecture and API surface

Package layout and the one import rule

app/            # routes (App Router) — thin: load data, render; no scoring
components/     # UI; may import lib/* loaders, never scoring engines directly for decisions
lib/
  access.ts     # single source of authorisation predicates
  tenant.ts     # tenant client + raw-client ESLint guard (zero exceptions)
  scoring/      # pure functions only; no React, no DB
  fit/          # pure functions only
  esoteric/     # isolated; may NOT be imported by scoring/ or fit/ or report-decision paths (I1)
  instruments/  # versioned item banks (JSON) + subscale maps
  audit/        # audit row writers
  crypto.ts     # AES-256-GCM encrypt/decrypt with key rotation
  ui/terms.ts   # single source of reflective framing sentence
prisma/         # schema + migrations
tests/          # vitest unit tests (pure logic)
e2e/            # Playwright specs on the seeded isolated server
integration/    # DB-backed regression tests (CONSTELLATION_FIXTURE_DB=1)
scripts/        # CLI tools (retention, restore-drill, route-probe, acceptance)

The one import rule that keeps it honest: lib/scoring/ and lib/fit/ import nothing from lib/esoteric/, lib/reflective*, or any DB client; they are pure functions of their inputs. Enforced by tests/esoteric/isolation.test.ts and tests/fit/isolation.test.ts (fit outputs byte-identical with/without esoteric data). A PR that adds such an import fails CI.

Endpoint / command table

Surface Who may call Notes
/api/health anyone liveness; no auth
/api/auth/me authenticated returns 401 after revocation
/api/auth/logout authenticated revokes the token server-side (I3-style: revocation row)
/api/assess/answers session owner idempotency key header (WS-8); dedupes on (sessionId, itemId); returns 200 + duplicates: n
/api/build-id anyone single deployed SHA on client and server (A10)
Server actions (app/**/actions.ts) role-gated via lib/access.ts no route re-implements a rule
npm run db:retention / scripts/retention-run.ts ops / scheduler dry-run default; --apply requires org opt-in
scripts/restore-drill.mjs ops refuses non-fixture DB names

Conventions the build must keep

  • Pagination: cursor-based where a list can exceed one page; default page size documented per route.
  • Idempotency: answer saving (WS-8), request settlement (single-use), retention runs (date-keyed, re-run safe), fit runs (append-only) are the four idempotent write families; every new write states its idempotency key.
  • Versioning: BATTERY_VERSION and profileVersion are pinned constants; a change to either is a migration-level event with a recompute.
  • No dates in build plans; stages end on boolean exit tests.

8. Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Birth-field migration corrupts a birth instant wrong chart / wrong reading dual-write window, rehearsed script, export byte-compare (AC-9) implementation lane; dry-run count ≠ row count is the early-warning
Scheduled retention deletes a record a court wants data gone, audit trail only legal-hold predicate (WS-5), dry-run-first, per-org opt-in, kill switch ops; dry-run candidate includes a held/disputed person
Explanation copy over-claims validity staff trust eroded content-owner sign-off gate (WS-6), single-source terms (WS-12) content owner; two phrasings of the same sentence in codebase
Axe gate blocks an urgent fix CI red, merge blocked scan-only week, expiring ignores (90-day), reviewed list engineering; ignore-file growth > 5 entries/month
Adversarial tenancy work reveals a real write leak cross-org data exposure fix forward in the same PR, notify owner same-day engineering; any AC-56 row-count mismatch
Idle timeout disrupts a long sitting mid-assessment sign-out default = status quo (8 h); org-configurable; sequenced after WS-8/9 support reports of mid-assessment sign-outs
Export header breaks a downstream consumer consumer parse failure # prefix convention + README notice (AC-51 note) consumer complaint on first release with header
Golden vectors freeze a wrong answer wrong expected values in CI deviation notes per vector; recompute audit remains independent vector carrying a deviation note older than 90 days
Staging repair touches shared credentials deploy blocked owner rotates; no secret values in any artefact smoke gate red > 1 day
Census test becomes wallpaper new reader unregistered AC-3 tamper check; census doc linked from /consent a new E32-style path merged without census update
DB migration fails on a real database deploy blocked, app down down direction rehearsed on fixture; destructive migrations separate from dual-write (I10) npx prisma migrate status exits 0 (AC-6)
Answer lost on poor network silent data loss persisted outbox (WS-8), server-wins reconciliation, resume banner e2e runner-resilience spec (AC-35, AC-36)
Session expires mid-sitting 401, no write outbox saved locally, resume flow, re-auth (WS-8) e2e 8 h boundary case (AC-37)

A row with no control is a risk being accepted; none are left blank above.

9. Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals

  • STAFF — self only; reads own scores, consent, data rights.
  • MANAGER — department-scoped: team reports, placement, candidate lists for departments in ManagerScope + own department.
  • ADMIN (company) — full company surface: staff, positions, campaigns, data requests, runs, erasure checklist.
  • PLATFORM (owner) — cross-company enumeration, suspension, tenant provisioning; only principal that sees multiple orgs.

Role/action matrix (selected; full matrix in lib/access.ts)

Action STAFF MANAGER ADMIN PLATFORM
View own scores / provenance (WS-6) yes no (uses team surfaces) via impersonation (audited) no
View team report for a department own only scoped departments company any
Trigger a fit run no no yes yes
Settle a DataRequest no no yes yes
Open erasure checklist (WS-5) no no yes yes
Set legal hold no no yes yes
Create a company workspace no no yes (own org) yes
Cross-company enumeration no no no yes
Read esoteric data self with consent no with consent + policy (A12/A19) no (unless consent)
Export staff/placement CSV no scoped company any

What is stored

  • Person-derived data: Staff, Response, Score, PositionFit, TeamSnapshot, Esoteric (encrypted), Consent, PerformanceRating, Session.
  • Audit trail: AuditLog (append-only), RecordRevision.
  • Auth: User (bcrypt hash), SessionRevocation, AuthRateLimit, Invite, PasswordResetRequest.

What is anchored outside the system

  • Backups: daily .sql.gz under /Volumes/Hiksemi 1TB/AI-Drive/Backups/constellation-* (not in git). Restore drill (WS-16) proves them.
  • CI artefacts: browser-failure evidence, route-probe output, recompute-check output (.github/workflows/verify.yml).
  • The esoteric framing sentence and the privacy rule sentence are single-sourced in lib/ui/terms.ts and lib/privacy/min-group.ts respectively, asserted by tests.

Honest statement of signature/approval strength

  • Session tokens are stateless JWTs (8 h) with server-side revocation rows on logout and version-bump on credential change. Revocation is checked on every authenticated request (/api/auth/me returns 401 after logout). There is no idle timeout today (WS-14 adds one, default = status quo).
  • Consent grants are recorded rows (Consent.granted, grantedAt, revokedAt) with single-use settlement for data requests. Consent is enforced at the write path (I6), not only the read path.
  • Erasure is a witnessed manual procedure with a checklist and audit rows — not automated (I3, WS-5). No "delete button" exists; this is deliberate.
  • The esoteric isolation claim ("fit outputs byte-identical with/without esoteric data") is proven by tests, not by review. It is a structural claim, not a cryptographic one.
  • Tenant isolation is proven on the read side (e2e) and unproven on the write side until WS-13's adversarial gate runs.
  • No row in this system is signed by a third party; "approval" means an audited row in AuditLog plus a recorded operator id. Stronger than a log line, weaker than a cryptographic signature.

10. Build order by dependency

No dates. Each stage ends on a boolean exit test.

Phase 0 — hygiene (unblocks evidence for everything else)

  • Work: WS-15 (staging repair + smoke gate).
  • Runs in parallel: none.
  • Exit test: a PR merges and the staging workflow run shows the deploy step green (AC-63); node scripts/route-probe.mjs <staging-url> prints 0 failures (AC-64); ls docs/reports/staging-repair-2026-10.md exits 0 and names the root cause (AC-65).

Phase 1 — proofs and privacy foundation

  • Work (serial, in this order): WS-1 census pin → WS-2 birth encryption (dual-write) → WS-3 DSAR SLA → WS-4 retention dry-run → WS-7 golden corpus + policy → WS-11 accessibility gate (scan-only) → WS-13 tenancy write-side.
  • Runs in parallel: WS-7 and WS-11 can run alongside WS-2/WS-3 (no shared files).
  • Exit test: npx vitest run tests/esoteric/read-path-census.test.ts exits 0 (AC-1); tsx scripts/retention-run.ts --dry-run exits 0 with candidates=\d+ deleted=0 (AC-15); CONSTELLATION_FIXTURE_DB=1 npx vitest run integration/tenancy-adversarial.test.ts exits 0 with >= 14 cases (AC-55); npx playwright test e2e/a11y.spec.ts exits 0 in scan-only mode.

Phase 2 — experience and governance

  • Work: WS-6 provenance page → WS-8 outbox → WS-9 timer → WS-10 self-view → WS-11 floor-on → WS-12 placement provenance → WS-5 erasure workflow → WS-14 sessions → WS-16 restore drill → WS-17 history → WS-2 cutover (drop plaintext columns) → WS-4 apply per org.
  • Runs in parallel: WS-12 after WS-1 (terms pattern); WS-17 after WS-12; WS-5 after WS-3; WS-14 after WS-4; WS-16 after WS-4.
  • Exit test: npx playwright test e2e/provenance.spec.ts exits 0 showing battery version 1.3.0 (AC-26); npx playwright test e2e/runner-resilience.spec.ts exits 0 (AC-35); psql … birthDate IS NOT NULL prints 0 on a fixture copy (AC-7); node scripts/restore-drill.mjs --latest exits 0 (AC-67).

Dependency arrows

WS-1 → WS-2 → (cutover last); WS-3 → WS-5; WS-4 → WS-14 → WS-16; WS-6 → WS-10; WS-8 → WS-9; WS-12 → WS-17.

Smallest first PR proving the pipeline: WS-15 AC-63/64 — one green staging deploy with a probe gate.

11. Decisions register (only questions Ricardo can answer)

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Runner timer semantics — elapsed-active (pausing outside the runner), wall-clock since start, or drop the BUILD-SPEC §7 requirement? (drives WS-9) Elapsed-active (my recommendation) Wall-clock inflates durations on idle sittings and hides the real effort; dropping loses the BUILD-SPEC promise entirely
2 Fit% self-visibility — does a staff member see their own fit percentage against their position, or managers only? (drives WS-6/WS-10) Show, with WS-6 provenance attached Hiding a number from the person it measures while showing it to their manager is hard to defend and inconsistent with /account/data which already exports scores
3 Birth-data encryption posture — WS-2 migration, or documented acceptance of volume-level encryption only with SCOPE §8 wording corrected? (threat-model call) Migrate (WS-2) Volume-level only leaves the one indefensible dataset unencrypted at the column level; accepting it requires correcting SCOPE §8's false claim
4 Company norm activation — keep automatic-on-completion, or explicit admin activation with preview? (blocks Phase-F norms control) Explicit admin activation with preview Automatic keeps every historical score silently re-meaning on a norm transition; explicit adds a human button to a re-meaning event
5 Staging/deploy verification — confirm the #12 staging failure state and who owns the staging credentials so WS-15 can start (failure record is from the task brief, not my own run; E38) Owner confirms and hands over credentials Without confirmation, WS-15 cannot start and every later phase lacks staging evidence
6 Cross-company staff transfer — is "move person to another company" a legitimate flow the write-hardening must preserve as export/import, or does nobody do it? (drives WS-13) Export/import, never move Preserving move requires a cross-org write path that WS-13 is explicitly closing; export/import is reversible and auditable
7 Phase F priority — operations first (backup/monitoring/limits), then SSO, then API, then HRIS; or a commercial driver changes the order? (money/contracts) Operations first SSO-first or API-first without ops foundations ships features nobody can run or bill
8 Org exit (erasure at company level) — keep manual-only, or policy-then-controlled-purge with legal hold? Manual-only for now Controlled purge needs a retention/legal-hold policy that does not exist yet; automating first is irreversible
9 Email hold — lift for invites/reminders/resets with a named provider, or in-app cadence engine first? In-app cadence engine first, provider second Email-first depends on a provider decision and a contract; in-app keeps the existing channels working
10 Export deliverable scope (Phase-F packs) — current CSVs + print accepted, add HR dimension-score CSV, or build full team PDF pack? Current CSVs + print accepted Each step up adds a document-generation surface with its own provenance and accessibility burden

(Marked as already decided by owner and therefore excluded: MIN_GROUP = 0, ten-point ideal bands, company-admin workspace creation, company/group drill-down. See v1.1 owner amendments, 28 Oct 2026.)

1. What this version fixes

  • The source v1.1 PRD (docs/PRD-Constellation-v1.1-2026-10-27.md) cites audit baseline 0c47ddfa (18 Oct 2026) and main at d7e08f5d; the repo I read is at 357303d (31 Oct 2026). The 19 audit findings and 12 agreed changes are carried forward unchanged, but their status is re-verified against the code at 357303d (status ledger §3).
  • The source's counts (43 page templates, 10 route handlers, 23 action modules, 878 tests) are stale: the repo now has 45 page templates, 5 API routes (route.ts), 14 action modules, 155 unit test files. This document uses the verified values and marks the rest UNVERIFIED.
  • The source's Phase 4 note that "Stage 1 is built and verified on branches" (CN-01/02/03 on 1620a87/695d2a7/e140ec0) could not be re-verified by branch inspection this session; left as the source's claim.
  • Depth added: status ledger with evidence, domain model summary, feature table with expected values for the 12 changes and the 19 findings, failure-mode table, role/action matrix, decisions register, evidence appendix. The source had the release gate and decisions but no structured feature table or failure modes.

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: the assessment platform Infinity Brands runs on its own people — staff sit sectioned psychometric batteries; managers read placement and team analytics; reports answer where a person fits, how a team is composed, what a company's shape looks like.
  • Who uses it: staff (self), managers (department-scoped), HR admins (campaigns, data rights, runs), platform owners (tenants).
  • What decision/action it enables: defensible staffing and development conversations, backed by reproducible numbers.
  • What it is deliberately not: not payroll or performance management; not a hiring portal; not AI-narrative generation; not multi-language.
  • The single number that says it works: the release gate passes — zero critical and zero high audit findings open, every P0 closed with a test that was red before the fix, and one real staff member completes an assessment end to end on the release build in staging with consent granted normally and their report opens.

4. Domain model

Summary only; the full column types and invariants are in the enhancement document §4–§5.

Core entities (28 Prisma models): Organization, Venture, Department, Position, Staff, User, Invite, Consent, Session, Response, Score, NormTransition, NormSet, Esoteric, FitRun, PositionFit, TeamSnapshot, AuditLog, Campaign, CampaignMember, Reminder, PasswordResetRequest, DataRequest, ManagerScope, PerformanceRating, SessionRevocation, AuthRateLimit, RecordRevision.

Deciding relationships: Staff ↔︎ User (optional, 1:1); Session → Response → Score (append-only revisions); FitRun → PositionFit/TeamSnapshot (append-only, labelled); AuditLog and RecordRevision are append-only and never deleted (I3). The two tables that decide performance: PositionFit (indexed @@index([runId, staffId])) and Score (indexed @@index([sessionId, isCurrent])).

Invariants I1–I10 (enhancement doc §4) govern all future work; the most load-bearing for v1.1: I1 (esoteric isolation), I2 (append-only scores), I6 (consent at write path), I9 (db:reset refusal).

5. The engine

Scoring: raw → z = (raw − mean)/sd → T = 50 + 10·z, clamped [20, 80], normVersion recorded. Fit: band/tolerance/weights → fit = round(100·Σ(w·s)/Σw) with s = 1 in-band else decay by tolerance; move threshold ≥ 15 delta; coverage = max over members; gaps > 0.25 ranked by required·gap; synergy = round(100·(0.6·meanCoverage + 0.4·balanceTerm)) with balance peaking at moderate diversity. Constellation axes: X = (z_E + z_A + z_SOCIAL)/3, Y = (z_C + z_CONVENTIONAL − z_OPENNESS)/3. All pure functions in lib/scoring/ and lib/fit/, unit-tested. Full state machine, transitions and edge cases (concurrency, empty set, stale version, withdrawal, exhaustion, self-approval, duplicate) are in the enhancement document §5.

6. Feature table

The 12 agreed changes (CN-01…CN-12) and the 19 audit findings (A01…A19) as features, with acceptance tests carrying expected values. Audit findings keep their audit IDs for side-by-side reading with the 18 Oct audit.

ID Feature Priority Why it matters Acceptance test (expected value)
F1 C0 restore source of record + prove health P0 Staff locked out since 21 Oct if the clone is missing git -C "$REPO" log -1 returns a commit and git fsck clean; :3214/api/health answers; deployed SHA named in the release record
F2 C1 least privilege: runtime role no DDL, db:reset/seed refuse non-disposable DBs P0 A superuser runtime role + destructive paths can truncate Organization/AuditLog/User Runtime connection refused on CREATE DATABASE, CREATE ROLE, DROP TABLE (each attempted, refused); db:reset refuses a non-disposable DB name
F3 C2 consent + enabled-bank enforced at the write path P0 A section can be written the subject never agreed to, or that the org switched off Save against an area with no consent grant is refused with a visible reason and writes nothing; replayed payload against a disabled bank is refused; both refusals have a test that fails on pre-fix code
F4 C3 logout revokes the session server-side P0 A copied token keeps working after sign-out A token copied before logout returns 401 after logout on every authenticated route; a test asserts the revoked state, not the absence of a cookie
F5 C4 save cannot cross the completion boundary (transaction + state guard) P0 An answer write can land on an already-submitted section Save against a submitted section refused and stored result unchanged; concurrent save+submit tested, exactly one outcome
F6 C5 recompute appends a revision, never destroys P0 A failed mid-run recompute leaves nothing A recompute that fails partway leaves the previous result intact and readable; every recompute produces a new revision row naming trigger and operator
F7 C6 v3 blueprints accepted or v2 re-approved in writing P0 Every report built on a profile set nobody stands behind The 60-check v3 acceptance runs and passes with the fixture committed, OR a written v2 decision dated with the 22 duplicates named
F8 C7 norm transitions atomic + resumable P0 A cohort can sit half-normalised Killing mid-transition and re-running leaves the cohort fully transitioned once; nothing applied twice; cohort normalisation state queryable
F9 C8 birth time in subject's zone; password byte limit enforced P1 Same input produces different charts on different hosts; two passwords can collide over bcrypt's 72-byte limit Birth time 20:20 SAST produces the same chart with the server set to UTC, Cape Town and New York; a password over the byte limit refused with a clear message; two strings sharing a 72-byte prefix cannot both authenticate
F10 C9 independent scoring audit stops false-rejecting a valid company; detects a deleted bank P1 The one instrument built to prove the arithmetic wrong produces a false alarm The pre-threshold company passes the audit; a deliberately deleted bank is detected; demo-norm copy workaround no longer needed
F11 C10 immutable deployment identity + honest proxy redirects P1 Fresh builds emit mismatched client/server identifiers; signed-out redirects point at localhost One identifier reported on client and server from a fresh build; signing out behind the proxy lands on the public host, never localhost; deployed SHA readable from the running service
F12 C11 named reflective access + one policy function P1 Reflective access differs across surfaces; the company switch is not enforced on the detailed reading path With the company switch off, the detailed reading path refuses exactly as the summary path does; one table lists every report surface and its audience; fixture with manager + staff viewer proves both
F13 C12 no secrets in logs + throttling atomic and configurable P1 Bootstrap logs a password-setting bearer link; throttling has timing/concurrency/config gaps A bootstrap run writes no usable credential to any log/file/console; parallel sign-in attempts counted atomically; throttle thresholds configurable with the default stated in README
F14 C13 interface failures: 390 px overflow, dark-mode contrast, dependency advisories P2 Placement page overflows 390 px by 98 px; warning button fails contrast in dark mode; 8 package nodes carry advisories No horizontal scroll at 390 px on the placement page; warning button meets WCAG AA contrast in both themes, measured; each advisory either patched or documented not-applicable
F15 CN-01 all-departments switcher P1 Managers need cross-department views within their scope Switcher renders departments the manager can see; selecting one changes the team view; no cross-tenant leak (AC-77-style scoping check)
F16 CN-02 constellation map axes P1 Axes and formulas must be visible and signed off Axes render per BUILD-SPEC §5 formulas; "How this is calculated" drawer shows them; content owner signs off the wording before merge (D-04)
F17 CN-03 company-wide placement P1 Admins need a company-wide placement view Company-wide placement renders for admins; managers scoped to their departments; no cross-company enumeration
F18 CN-04…CN-12 (stages 2–4: joins, individual-report expansion, wording) P1 The brief's stages 2–4 remain to close the audit Each change ships with a test that fails on pre-fix code; stage 4 (CN-11 + CN-12) ships together or not at all
F19 Exports obey the same suppression policy as screens P0 lib/hr/export.ts and both export routes carry no suppression logic; one named row per person leaks what ten screens withhold Export rows are filtered by the same policy as the screens; a test asserts parity between screen and export for a below-threshold department
F20 Release gate (6 items) P0 v1.1 is not "all findings closed" but a release showable as safe on a defined surface (1) every P0 closed with a test red before the fix; (2) audit re-run reports zero critical/high open; (3) e2e suites run on fixtures (one-person, 3- and 5-person depts, no-grant and partial-grant staff, complete and partial round, manager scoped to one dept vs a second with different data); (4) restore drill performed from a dated dump, timed and recorded; (5) one real staff member completes an assessment end to end on the release build in staging with consent granted normally and their report opens; (6) privacy rule either unchanged and demonstrated working on a fixture above the line, or changed and written down with its date
F21 Owner decisions D-01…D-08 recorded and applied P0 Two decide what the privacy rule means; one decides whether the rule is the problem at all Each decision that is answered is recorded with its date in the implementation log; no decided question remains in the decisions register

Counts: 21 features; P0 = F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8, F19, F20, F21 = 11; P1 = F9, F10, F11, F12, F13, F15, F16, F17, F18 = 9; P2 = F14 = 1.

7. Architecture and API surface

Same stack and layout as the enhancement document §7 (Next.js 15, Prisma, PostgreSQL 16, Tailwind; package layout; the one import rule). v1.1 adds no new routes beyond those already shipped; its work is hardening, census, and policy enforcement on existing surfaces. Conventions (pagination, idempotency, versioning) are identical to the enhancement document §7.

8. Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
A destructive maintenance path fires while A01 is being fixed data loss on Organization/AuditLog/User roles split first; runtime role loses DDL before any other change lands engineering; restore drill before touching A01
Fixing findings against an unversioned working copy unrepeatable, unauditable change C0 gates everything; no branch before it passes engineering
v2 profiles remain live while v3 acceptance incomplete reports built on an unapproved profile set C6 requires an explicit written position either way; silence is not an option product owner
Fixing the size rule's appearance while the export side channel stays open appearance fixed, leak remains exports obey the same policy in the same release (F19) engineering + audit re-run
Re-seeding the demo mistaken for fixing the rule rule unchanged, fixture changed D-07 is a diagnostic, not a fix; the rule decision recorded separately product owner
Multi-tenant isolation remains unproven unexercised multi-tenant behaviour stated as a limit of the release, not papered over; two-org fixture is the next release's first item audit re-run
Audit harness cannot be re-run (lives outside the repo) no re-verification possible suite paths recorded in the audit's reproduction block; restored with C0 and kept with the release engineering

9. Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals, role/action matrix, stored data, externally anchored artefacts and the honest statement of signature/approval strength are identical to the enhancement document §9. v1.1-specific notes:

  • The v1.1 release deliberately does not certify legal compliance, penetration testing, accessibility conformance, or tenant isolation beyond the read-side e2e (v1.1 §07 blocker note). Those limits were true of the audit and stay true of this release.
  • Reflective access (C11) is put behind one policy function called from every read path; the company reflective-off switch is enforced by the detailed reading path exactly as by the summary path.
  • Bootstrap logs no usable credential; throttling is atomic and configurable (C12).

10. Build order by dependency

No dates. Each stage ends on a boolean exit test.

Stage 0 — restore (gates everything)

  • Work: C0 re-clone at the canonical path, restore .env, rebuild, let the watchdog confirm the port, establish who removed the directory and why nothing alerted.
  • Exit test: git -C "$REPO" log -1 returns a commit and git fsck clean; npm ci && npm run build completes and :3214/api/health answers; the public staff link loads sign-in end to end from outside the tailnet; the watchdog logs a normal tick; a written note names who removed the directory, when, and what alert should have fired; the deployed commit is named in the release record.

Stage 1 — containment (the six that can hurt people or erase data)

  • Work: C1 (least privilege), C2 (consent/enabled-bank at write path), C3 (server-side revocation), C4 (save cannot cross completion), C5 (append-and-retire revisions).
  • Runs in parallel: none — these ship first and alone.
  • Exit test: every P0 in Stage 1 closed with a test that fails on the pre-fix code; the audit re-run reports zero critical-impact findings open from this set.

Stage 2 — correctness (where the numbers can be trusted)

  • Work: C6 (v3 blueprints or v2 re-approval), C7 (atomic resumable norm transitions), C8 (birth time + password bytes), C9 (independent scoring audit).
  • Exit test: the 60-check v3 acceptance runs and passes with the fixture committed (or the v2 decision is written with its date and the 22 duplicates named); killing mid-transition and re-running leaves the cohort fully transitioned once; the pre-threshold company passes the audit and a deliberately deleted bank is detected.

Stage 3 — deployment and access (cheap now, expensive later)

  • Work: C10 (deployment identity + proxy redirects), C11 (named reflective access, one policy), C12 (secrets in logs + throttling), C13 (dependencies + two interface failures).
  • Exit test: a fresh build reports one identifier on client and server; signing out behind the proxy lands on the public host; with the company switch off the detailed reading path refuses exactly as the summary path does; no horizontal scroll at 390 px on the placement page; the warning button meets WCAG AA contrast in both themes, measured.

Stage 4 — the twelve changes (brief stages 2–4)

  • Work: CN-01…CN-12, with stage 1 already built on branches (CN-01/02/03), stage 2 (CN-06, CN-10, then CN-04, CN-05), stage 3 (CN-07/08/09), stage 4 (CN-11 + CN-12 together, one release or none).
  • Exit test: every change ships with a test that fails on pre-fix code; stage 4 ships CN-11 and CN-12 in one release (half-shipped wording is the failure mode); exports obey the same policy as the screens in the same release.

Cross-reference to the enhancement programme

The enhancement programme (docs/PRD-Constellation-enhancement-2026-10-30-v2-enhanced.md) operationalises the v1.1 release gates as workstreams: WS-14 (staging pipeline repair) and WS-15 (restore drill) carry gate items 4–5; WS-11 (accessibility) carries the C13 contrast/overflow work; WS-13 (tenancy write-side) closes the multi-tenant limit named in v1.1 §07. The MIN_GROUP = 0 owner amendment (v1.1 §"Owner amendments") is recorded as decided and is not re-litigated here.

11. Decisions register (only questions Ricardo can answer)

The eight decisions carried forward from the 17 Oct change brief, plus the ten open questions in the enhancement document §11. Items already decided by the owner (MIN_GROUP = 0, ten-point ideal bands, company-admin workspace creation, company/group drill-down, email on hold) are recorded as decided and excluded.

# Question Recommended answer (v1.1 / enhancement) Cost of each alternative
D-01 Who may see that a named person has not granted consent — the manager who benefits, or admin only? Admin only. A visible "has not granted" flag turns consent into something given under observation. Manager visibility speeds workflows but coerces the grant; admin-only keeps the grant free
D-02 Does removing the size rule apply to staff-facing screens too, or only managers and admins? Managers and admins only. Staff-facing suppression protects the individual. Extending to staff exposes individual results to the person they describe with no manager context — arguably correct, but a separate decision
D-03 May a manager choose a company-wide placement or team view, or is that admin-only? Manager, scoped to the departments they can already see (CN-01 already established that pattern; the visibility check stays) Admin-only centralises control; manager-scoped keeps the existing pattern
D-04 Are the two constellation-map axes the metrics expected, and who signs off the plain-language wording for the esoteric figures? Needs a content owner, not a decision — name one person; wording reviewed before CN-02 ships No owner means wording drifts across surfaces
D-05 Does "completed part of the tests" mean any section submitted, or a threshold share? Any section submitted, labelled with the count — a progress signal, not a verdict A threshold hides partial effort and mislabels people
D-06 Which surface is the full individual report — /reports/individual/<id> or /people/<id>/report — and does the other stay light? /reports/individual is the full reading; /people/<id>/report stays light by design Merging them loses the deliberate light surface
D-07 Do we fix the demo data instead of the rule? Yes, and first. Departments of six. It costs one seed change and tells you whether the rule was ever the problem Fixing the rule first conflates a fixture problem with a policy problem
D-08 Restore service from which commit? Deploy current main (d7e08f5d at the time) to end the outage today, then diff against audit baseline 0c47ddfa as the first act of Phase 1 Holding service closed until the audit baseline is rebuilt leaves staff locked out for no benefit
E1 Runner timer semantics (elapsed-active vs wall-clock vs drop) Elapsed-active see enhancement doc §11 #1
E2 Fit% self-visibility Show, with provenance see enhancement doc §11 #2
E3 Birth-data encryption posture Migrate (WS-2) see enhancement doc §11 #3
E4 Company norm activation Explicit admin activation with preview see enhancement doc §11 #4
E5 Staging/deploy verification (confirm #12 failure + credential owner) Owner confirms and hands over see enhancement doc §11 #5
E6 Cross-company staff transfer Export/import, never move see enhancement doc §11 #6
E7 Phase F priority Operations first see enhancement doc §11 #7
E8 Org exit (erasure at company level) Manual-only for now see enhancement doc §11 #8
E9 Email hold In-app cadence engine first see enhancement doc §11 #9
E10 Export deliverable scope (Phase-F packs) Current CSVs + print accepted see enhancement doc §11 #10

Self-check (standard §4)

infx-fnd docs/FRANCHISE-TERRITORY-CONSOLIDATION-PRD-2026-10-28-v2-enhanced.md source ↗

INFX FND — franchise territories, network planning and application consolidation (enhanced)

38 features 19 P0 9 implemented 4 partial 10 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

  • The source PRD is a 463-line implementation proposal that declares itself "not implemented by this document"; every capability it lists is a requirement, not a verified state. This version adds a status ledger that marks each one implemented / partial / absent against the code at commit 6472727, with a file:line on every row (see facts/infx-fnd-territory.md).
  • Two territory schemes for one brand are a real risk in the code, not a future one: zone_set is tenant-scoped with no brand_id, no active-set pointer and no supersession column; tenantZoneSet returns the newest created_at row (src/lib/repo/zones.ts:95-103). The original PRD asks builders to "mark one active" but never states how the code currently picks. This version states it and makes the pointer an invariant (I5).
  • The 7 km ACDC spacing rule is described in the source as "conflict at distance strictly below 7,000 m"; the code has no spacing function at all (grep -rn "spacing\|7000" src/ returns nothing). This version records the rule as absent with the test that will prove it.
  • Hexagon identity is currently a mean-centred flat-top q/r grid with no stable IDs (src/lib/map.ts:44-83); the source calls for H3. This version records H3 as absent and makes stable cell IDs a numbered feature (F13) with an acceptance test.
  • Counts in the source (34 ACDC own stores, 135 Papa zones, 25 TLF candidates) are restated here as dated observations from the source review and seed files, not as live database facts, because no database was queried in this task.
  • The source PRD's "What this version fixes" predecessor is not applicable; this document is the first enhanced version, so the fix list above names what was thin (no per-capability evidence, no code verification, no engine) and what depth was added.

Product in five lines

  • What it is: a company-scoped territory register and planning workspace (Next.js + PostGIS) that lets an owner see their own network, available/reserved/sold/unverified territory status, and plan expansion on the same map used for market research.
  • Who uses it: company owners and network editors at Papa Pasta, ACDC Express and The Local Farmer; analysts in Research; anonymous viewers of restricted share links.
  • Decision it enables: which ground is owned on what terms, whether a candidate site conflicts with an existing right, and whether a radius or hexagon plan reaches more verified buyers under the same constraint.
  • What it is deliberately not: a business census, a revenue forecaster, a CRM, a lease executor, or a promise of franchise profitability.
  • The number that says it works: 115 regression tests pass on the current commit, and the territory register returns the same status in table, map and CSV for a given scheme (acceptance T02).

Proposed additions (from network PRD §4.1, additive only, no re-typing of zone.geom):

  • zone_set: brand_id uuid, kind text CHECK (polygon/radius/named_area), effective_from date, superseded_at timestamptz, source_document_id uuid.
  • zone: territory_kind text, radius_km numeric CHECK (>0 AND <=50), fee_zar numeric, fee_basis text, exclusivity_months int, phase_no int, target_stores int, economics jsonb.
  • ALTER COLUMN geom DROP NOT NULL only after the additive representation exists; legacy 135 rows keep their geometry.

Indexes that decide performance:

  1. location_geom_idx — GiST on location.geom (db/migrations/001_init.sql:51).
  2. zone_geom_idx — GiST on zone.geom (db/migrations/001_init.sql:100).
  3. location_geography_idx — GiST expression on (geom::geography) (db/migrations/012_research_decisions.sql).
  4. Proposed: tenant + scheme + status composite for the register; GiST on proposed geometry columns.

Invariants (numbered so a future feature cannot quietly break them):

  • I1. A source observation or modelled target never creates an own outlet implicitly. An outlet is a verified location row.
  • I2. Commercial availability (unverified/available/reserved/sold/unavailable) is never overloaded onto operational status (open/closed/coming_soon/planned).
  • I3. Imports start unverified unless a privileged, reviewed commercial import substantiates another state. Actor, timestamp and evidence required to move to available.
  • I4. One active reservation per territory, enforced with transactional concurrency control. Double-click retries are idempotent; competing requests return a conflict.
  • I5. Exactly one active territory set per (brand, purpose). The active pointer is an explicit column, not "newest created_at" (src/lib/repo/zones.ts:95-103 is the bug this fixes).
  • I6. Approved boundary amendments create new revisions. Archiving a scheme never cancels existing rights.
  • I7. No conflicting active allocations within the applicable company/right type, even across schemes. Approved exceptions need a documented basis.
  • I8. Every current status shows who reviewed it and when; newer document dates never overwrite a manually reviewed sale or reservation.
  • I9. A radius is authoritative; its polygon is derived and recomputed on change. geom is never hand-edited for a radius territory.
  • I10. Fees, plan totals, candidate scores and study notes are never serialised without pricing:read (src/lib/visibility.ts).
  • I11. A candidate cannot reach approved without a study_document_id or a written evidence_exempt.
  • I12. H3 cell IDs are stable for a saved scenario; the current mean-centred q/r grid (src/lib/map.ts:44-83) is a rendering aid only and must not be used for ownership, reservation IDs or saved territory membership.
  • I13. Counts are by distinct ID; a multi-category brand counted once; closed locations never count; coming_soon counts only as committed.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Commercial lifecycle state machine:

  • States (commercial availability): unverified → available → reserved → sold; plus unavailable.
  • Geometry/plan approval (separate dimension): draft → in_review → approved → archived.
  • Reservation lifecycle (separate dimension): active → expired / released / converted.
  • Transitions:
    • unverified → available: requires reviewed geometry/scope and rights; actor + timestamp + evidence.
    • available → reserved: requires private applicant reference, start/expiry, reason, actor, expected record version. Transactional; one active per territory.
    • reserved → sold: records approved agreement/reference, effective date, actor.
    • available → sold: permitted only with the same evidence and permissions (direct sale).
    • reserved → unverified/review-required on expiry (policy may return to available if no conflict).
    • sold → available: only through a separately evidenced termination/release workflow; an editor cannot toggle.

Spacing arithmetic (ACDC, proposed, not yet implemented):

  • Input: two premises (lat/lon) and a policy radius in metres.
  • Function: geodesic centre distance in metres (PostGIS ST_Distance on geography, or equivalent).
  • Conflict when distance is strictly below the policy radius (7,000 m for the draft Large Store rule).
  • Two 7 km circles overlap when their centres are 10 km apart; overlap is a visual fact, not a policy breach (ACDC-03).
  • Applicability unknown → "Rule not verified", never pass/fail.

Plan arithmetic (planTotals(plan, territories, assumptions), proposed pure function, network PRD §5):

  • Inputs: territory list with target_stores, target_monthly_sales_zar, plus typed assumptions {value, unit, period}.
  • Outputs: territories, target_stores, fees, target_monthly_sales, royalty_month, zone_share_month, head_office_month, per-territory breakdown.
  • Edge cases: per-cluster rounding vs aggregate (GT01: 3% × 7,833,913 = 235,017 stored; aggregate 3% = 5,377,852 vs per-cluster sum 5,377,861 — a R9 difference the output must disclose); zero territories (activation blocked); currency mixing (rejected without an exchange-rate assumption); monthly vs annual (typed field, never shared).

Hexagon arithmetic (proposed):

  • Stable H3 cell IDs at a locked resolution per saved scenario (res 6 ≈ 36.13 km², res 7 ≈ 5.16 km², res 8 ≈ 0.737 km² average; actual areas vary).
  • Coverage counts unique POI IDs via exact point-in-area / geodesic distance, not area-weighted fractions.
  • Boundary-point assignment deterministic; large cells, disconnected selections, holes, coastline and country-boundary effects validated before publication.

Architecture and API surface

Package layout (existing):

  • src/app — Next.js routes and pages (8 pages, 34 API handlers).
  • src/components — UI components (ZoneMap, ResearchHub, WorkspaceNav, CompanyLoader).
  • src/lib/repo — SQL data access; research.ts is the decision engine.
  • src/lib/ingest — adapters and refresh worker.
  • db/migrations — 14 files, 001–013 plus 015 (no 014).
  • test — 24 test files, 115 tests.

One import rule: every data-access function takes a tenantId (or brandId once the model is added) and applies it in the SQL; no query may omit tenant scope. New territory tables follow this rule.

Proposed endpoint groups (names follow existing conventions; all authenticate tenant access and authorise server-side):

Group Routes Who may call
Territories GET /api/network/territories?brand=&set=, GET /api/network/territories/:id member
Overlap GET /api/network/overlap?set= member
Candidates GET /api/network/candidates?brand=&stage=, PATCH /api/network/candidates/:id member (stage writes require approver)
Plans GET/POST /api/network/plans, POST /api/network/plans/:id/activate, GET /api/network/plans/:id/totals member read; owner/manager write
Studies POST /api/network/studies owner, manager, analyst
Import POST /api/network/import/preview, POST /api/network/import/apply owner, manager

Conventions: cursor pagination on lists; optimistic concurrency via revision; idempotent imports via source ref; typed assumptions ({value, unit, period}); conflict response for stale versions/reservations; non-disclosing 404 for inaccessible resources; public responses omit forbidden fields at serialisation.

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: anonymous viewer, member, analyst, network editor, company owner/authorised commercial manager, owner, system (refresh worker).

Role/action matrix (summary):

Capability Viewer/analyst Network editor Company owner / commercial manager
Read authorised own network and current publishable status Yes Yes Yes
Save private research scenarios Analyst capability Yes Yes
Edit candidate data and draft geometry Per Research permissions Yes Yes
Mark rights available / reserve / sell / release No Only if explicitly delegated Yes, with required evidence
Approve authoritative boundaries / publish No No by default Yes, with conflict validation
Read pricing, applicants, agreements Separate capability Separate capability Explicit policy; not inferred from map-view role

Stored: user credentials (scrypt), sessions, locations, brands, territories, research records, audit rows, source documents, scenarios. Anchored outside the system: private database backups under .cache/backups/ (git-ignored), GitHub commit hash, share tokens (24 random bytes), original source files in WorkDrive.

Signature/approval honesty: approvals are role-gated database rows with actor and timestamp. They are not cryptographic signatures and are not non-repudiable. The consolidation PRD states this explicitly; any "signed approval" claim must be read as a recorded, role-gated decision.

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — Source and model foundation. Schema design, source registry, observation importer, legacy adapters; SRC-01–SRC-05, domain model. Exit: ACDC observations reconciled; ambiguous data retained; all three companies represented; migration/restore checked.
  • Stage B — Own network and availability (P0). Company tabs, register/map, state transitions, reservations, shared Explore defaults; UX-01–UX-07, INV-01–INV-06, security. Exit: user can inspect all three companies; available/reserved/sold/unverified work with reviewed or explicitly test data; concurrency/isolation tests pass.
  • Stage C — ACDC spatial policies (P0/P1). Own-store reconciliation, scoped 7 km draft policy, centre editor, exact spacing checks, candidate links; ACDC-01–ACDC-04. Exit: source scope visible; overlap distinguished from rule violation; verified and approximate premises unmistakable.
  • Stage D — Local Farmer planning (P1). Buyer catalogue, named-target review, radius editor, stable H3, fair comparison, saved scenarios; TLF-01–TLF-09, HEX-01–HEX-04. Exit: a real reviewed pilot dataset supports reproducible radius/hex comparisons; unknown capacity prevents feasibility claims.
  • Stage E — Papa hexagon zones (P1). Existing-zone overlay, new draft cell editor, 38-cluster reference view, scheme/rights reconciliation; PAPA-01–PAPA-05. Exit: existing 135 polygons preserved; draft hex scheme editable; conflicting allocations cannot publish.
  • Stage F — Consolidated plan and hardening (P1). Shared pipeline/build phases, performance, accessible mobile flows, regression and Colima evidence; PLAN-01/PLAN-02, performance targets, tests. Exit: end-to-end journeys A–C pass; benchmark and restore evidence complete; local build usable.
  • Stage G — Advanced operations (P2). Typed economics, capacity-constrained allocation, optional routing and additional countries; PLAN-03, ACDC-05, TLF-08. Exit: scenario outputs have correct units, provenance and explicit routing/capacity limits.

Stages A–G are sequential; inside a stage the work runs in parallel. The incumbent (Zoho Projects briefs, spreadsheets) keeps running until Stage F ships — the plan is read-only evidence until then.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Which Papa Pasta scheme becomes the active plan? Luke's 38 clusters active; v7.2 kept as a superseded version; the 96-zone paper archived as a working paper. Clusters are the only scheme with fees, targets and reference stores in one document and the most recent. Activating 135 zones keeps a 2.8-stores-per-territory scheme live, which the zone-owner paper's own arithmetic says cannot pay a zone manager.
2 Does the TLF territory stay at 7 km or move to a named suburb boundary? Keep the 7 km radius. It is what the signed agreement annexure says; changing it is a legal act, not a product one. A polygon looks tidier on a map and contradicts the contract.
3 Import the 17 ACDC candidates before the studies are re-verified? Import now, marked by confidence. A candidate row with a document and a page is more honest than an empty pipeline. Waiting keeps the five Namibian cities in a PDF.
4 Ship fees to anyone below owner/manager? No — fees follow the existing pricing:read capability. No new role, no new flag. A second permission system to keep in sync.
5 Is an empty plan allowed to activate? No. A draft may be empty for the first save; activation requires at least one territory. An "active" plan computing to zero reads as a bug to every user.
6 Should the ACDC 7 km be treated as applicable to all store formats? No — import it scoped to Large Store pending applicability review (ACDC-01). Applying it universally overstates the restriction.
7 Should the TLF 25 historical candidates be treated as approved centres? No — centres unverified; import as candidates requiring review (SRC-04). Pretending they are verified makes the planning numbers meaningless.
8 Should the Papa 38 clusters replace the 135 polygons as the display baseline? No — retain 135 as display baseline; 38 as research layer; promotion requires explicit geometry/rights review (PAPA-03). Replacing silently cancels existing rights.
