INFX FND — the Network branch (enhanced)

21 features 15 P0 9 implemented 1 partial 14 absent 5 decisions

What this version fixes

  • The source PRD proposes a Network top-level branch with Territories/Pipeline/Plan surfaces and SQL migrations. None of it exists in the code at commit 6472727: no /api/network/* route (find src/app/api -path '*network*' empty), no network_plan/study_document/territory_kind/radius_km column on zone, no H3 (grep -rn "H3\|h3" src/ = 0). This version restates every claim as absent in the status ledger instead of "ready to build".
  • The source PRD's product decision (Network as a new top-level branch) is superseded by the consolidation PRD, which places network management inside Companies. Where the two disagree, the consolidation PRD governs and this document says so in one line (see Reconciliation).
  • The source PRD's D1 recommends activating Luke's 38 clusters over the 135-zone set. The consolidation PRD requires explicit geometry/rights review before any promotion and forbids automatic promotion. This version keeps D1 as a decision for Ricardo but marks the recommendation as superseded pending review.
  • The arithmetic in §5 of the source is verified against the seed files and holds (38 clusters, 311 stores, R12,900,000 fees, R179,261,734 monthly sales, royalty 5% = R8,963,087, zone share 3% = R5,377,861, head office 2% = R3,585,234; GT01 royalty 235,017). The R9 aggregate-vs-per-cluster difference is real and must be disclosed by the implementation.
  • The source's acceptance tests F1–F15 are retained and re-numbered into the feature table here with expected values preserved.
  • The source's build order (Stages A–E) is retained but re-stated with the consolidation PRD's dependency stages where they overlap, and with zero dates.

Product in five lines

  • What it is: a build specification for territory storage (polygon/radius/named-area), study-document evidence, candidate linkage and plan arithmetic, operating under the consolidation PRD's governance.
  • Who uses it: implementers building the territory register and planning tools; the same three brands' owners and editors.
  • Decision it enables: how to store and compute territories, candidates and plan totals without losing rights, units or audit.
  • What it is deliberately not: a competing product to the consolidation PRD; a second candidate pipeline; a revenue forecaster.
  • The number that says it works: the acceptance tests F1–F15 from the source (re-numbered here as F-rows) all pass with their stated expected values, including F6 royalty_month = 8963087 ± 0.5%.

Domain model

This document's domain model is the SQL proposal in its §4, reproduced here as the contract the implementation must follow. It is not yet in the code.

Territory sets and territories (§4.1):

ALTER TABLE zone_set
  ADD COLUMN brand_id     uuid REFERENCES brand(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  ADD COLUMN kind         text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'polygon'
                          CHECK (kind IN ('polygon','radius','named_area')),
  ADD COLUMN effective_from date,
  ADD COLUMN superseded_at  timestamptz,
  ADD COLUMN source_document_id uuid REFERENCES study_document(id);
ALTER TABLE zone
  ADD COLUMN territory_kind text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'polygon'
      CHECK (territory_kind IN ('polygon','radius','named_area')),
  ADD COLUMN radius_km      numeric CHECK (radius_km > 0 AND radius_km <= 50),
  ADD COLUMN fee_zar        numeric CHECK (fee_zar >= 0),
  ADD COLUMN fee_basis      text CHECK (fee_basis IN ('territory','store','annual')),
  ADD COLUMN exclusivity_months int,
  ADD COLUMN phase_no       int,
  ADD COLUMN target_stores  int CHECK (target_stores >= 0),
  ADD COLUMN economics      jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  ALTER COLUMN geom DROP NOT NULL;

A radius territory stores its centre in label_point, its radius in radius_km, and a derived polygon in geom. The radius is the source of truth; the polygon is a cache. A trigger or repository function redraws geom whenever radius_km or label_point changes.

Study documents (§4.2):

CREATE TABLE study_document (
  id            uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  tenant_id     uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  brand_id      uuid REFERENCES brand(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  title         text NOT NULL,
  kind          text NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('market_study','site_study','feasibility','terms_of_reference','strategy','financial_model')),
  author        text,
  published_on  date,
  source_url    text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  workdrive_id  text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  pages         int,
  confidence    text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'secondary' CHECK (confidence IN ('primary','secondary','unverified')),
  notes         text NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  created_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);

A study is a record of a document, never a copy of it. confidence = 'unverified' is how the Durbanville report enters the system (it lives in Cliq, not WorkDrive).

Candidates gain a brand, a territory and a source (§4.3):

ALTER TABLE candidate_site
  ADD COLUMN brand_id          uuid REFERENCES brand(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  ADD COLUMN zone_id           uuid REFERENCES zone(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  ADD COLUMN study_document_id uuid REFERENCES study_document(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  ADD COLUMN study_page        text,
  ADD COLUMN score             jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  ADD COLUMN evidence_exempt   text;

The plan (§4.4):

CREATE TABLE network_plan (
  id             uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  tenant_id      uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  brand_id       uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES brand(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  zone_set_id    uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES zone_set(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  name           text NOT NULL,
  version        text NOT NULL,
  status         text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'draft'
                 CHECK (status IN ('draft','active','superseded')),
  assumptions    jsonb NOT NULL,
  created_by     uuid REFERENCES app_user(id),
  created_at     timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX network_plan_one_active ON network_plan(brand_id) WHERE status = 'active';

assumptions is a typed object; the API rejects anything else:

{
  "royalty_pct": {"value": 5, "unit": "percent_of_turnover", "period": "month"},
  "zone_share_pct": {"value": 3, "unit": "percent_of_turnover", "period": "month"},
  "marketing_pct": {"value": 3, "unit": "percent_of_turnover", "period": "month"},
  "technology_fee": {"value": 5000, "unit": "zar", "period": "month_per_store"},
  "store_sales": {"value": 600000, "unit": "zar", "period": "month", "vat": "excluded"},
  "setup_cost": {"value": 144420, "unit": "zar", "period": "once"},
  "currency": "ZAR"
}

Invariants (from network PRD §4.5):

  • I1. A territory set belongs to exactly one brand; a territory belongs to exactly one set. (NOT NULL + unique index)
  • I2. A plan references one territory set version; superseding a plan never mutates a superseded one.
  • I3. Fees, plan revenue, candidate scores and study notes are never serialised without pricing:read.
  • I4. For a radius territory the radius is authoritative; geom is recomputed, never hand-edited.
  • I5. A candidate cannot reach approved without a study_document_id or a written evidence_exempt.
  • I6. Overlap inside a set is computed and surfaced; it never blocks an import.
  • I7. Every total on a plan is recomputed from its territories and assumptions on read; a stored total is a cache with a 0.5% tolerance.
  • I8. closed locations never count toward a territory; coming_soon counts only as committed, never as trading.
  • I9. Every candidate stage change writes a research_event with before/after.
  • I10. A share link never shows a fee, a plan total, a study or a note.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Candidate lifecycle (states and transitions):

  • States: researching → visit_needed → shortlisted → on_hold → rejected → approved.
  • approved requires an approver (manager or owner); non-approvers receive HTTP 403.
  • Editing an approved candidate requires an approver.
  • Reassigning an owner requires an approver unless the new owner is the actor.
  • Every state change records actor, timestamp, reason and before/after in research_event.

Plan arithmetic (planTotals(plan, territories, assumptions)), one pure function, three worked cases that become acceptance tests:

Papa Pasta 38-cluster plan (§5.1):

  • Territories: 38. Target stores: 311. Territory fees: R12,900,000.
  • Target monthly network sales: R179,261,734 (sum of per-cluster targets, average R576,404 a store).
  • Royalty at 5%: R8,963,087 a month.
  • Zone share at 3%: R5,377,861. Head office at 2%: R3,585,234. (3% of the aggregate is R5,377,852; the R9 difference is per-cluster rounding in the source; a plan that recomputes from assumptions must say which of the two it shows.)
  • Reference stores already mapped: 115 (37% of the target network already exists as a pin).

Papa Pasta 135-zone set read the same way (§5.2):

  • Same function, different version: 135 territories, 234–357 target stores, R102,700,000 of fees. The plan screen puts those two rows next to each other and states, in one sentence, that the cluster plan sells fewer, larger territories at a third of the fee income but a higher store target.

The Local Farmer (§5.3):

  • 25 territories, 13 AA at R25,000 and 12 BA at R20,000 = R565,000 of fees.
  • Setup at R144,420 a unit = R3,610,500 across the network.
  • At R50,000 monthly revenue a unit: R3,500 royalty (7%) + R1,500 marketing (3%) = R5,000 a month per unit; R125,000 a month across 25 units at full build.

ACDC Express (§5.4):

  • Candidates are not a plan yet, and the product must say so rather than invent one. What it can compute from the pack: 17 candidates, 1 approved (Maputo, R6,000,000 investment, 12-month exclusivity), 3 shortlisted, 3 needing a visit, 9 under research, 1 on hold — and, per approved store, R200,000 of commitment fee received against R3,200,000 of fit-out managed.

Edge cases (from invariants and acceptance tests):

  • Concurrency: radius and plan writes use revision with FOR UPDATE; stale write returns HTTP 409.
  • Empty set: a comparison with zero matching stores returns total_sites = 0, distinct_brands = 0, not an error; an empty plan cannot activate.
  • Duplicate candidates: within 500 m or matching normalised name, suggested for review, never auto-merged.
  • Self-approval: a non-approver cannot set or edit an approved candidate.
  • Withdrawal: setting rejected is reversible by an approver; no hard delete of history.
  • Exhaustion: radius capped at 50 km; candidate count per comparison 2–4.
  • Stale version: a scorecard version is immutable once created; a new calculation creates a new snapshot.
  • Per-cluster rounding: disclosed, not silently averaged.
  • Currency mixing: rejected without a recorded exchange-rate assumption.
  • Monthly vs annual: typed field, never shared.

Feature table

ID Feature Priority Why it matters Acceptance test (expected value)
F1 Papa Pasta's existing 135 zones survive the migration with brand and geometry intact P0 A second scheme must not erase the first 135 rows, all territory_kind = 'polygon', brand_id = Papa Pasta
F2 Import the 25 TLF territories P0 Radius territories have nowhere to live today 25 rows, 13 AA, 12 BA, every radius_km = 7, fees sum 565000
F3 A TLF territory's geometry equals a 7 km buffer of its centre P0 A radius must be a derived cache, not hand-drawn ST_Area within 1% of πr² (π × 49 km² ≈ 153.9 km²)
F4 Change one TLF radius to 10 km P0 The radius is authoritative; geometry must follow only that row's geom changes; area ≈ 314.2 km²
F5 Import Luke's 38 clusters as a second Papa Pasta set P0 Multiple schemes must coexist 311 target stores, fees 12900000, network_plan activation leaves v7.2 untouched
F6 GET /plans/:id/totals for the cluster plan P0 Plan totals must be derived and auditable royalty_month = 8963087 ± 0.5%; per-territory GT01 royalty = 235017
F7 A viewer without pricing:read calls the same endpoint P0 Fees must never leak no fees, no royalty, no assumptions.royalty_pct key at all
F8 Approve a candidate with no study and no exemption P0 Evidence must gate approval 422, nothing written
F9 Approve with evidence_exempt P0 Exemption must be recorded, not silent 200, research_event row with before/after
F10 Two overlapping territories in one set P1 Overlap is information, not a blocker GET /overlap returns the pair with area > 0; import still succeeds
F11 Activate a second plan for the same brand P0 One active plan per brand first plan becomes superseded; unique index proves one active
F12 Share link for a plan's brand P0 Fees/studies/notes must never reach a prospect 0 fee fields, 0 study titles, 0 candidate notes in the payload
F13 Import the 17 ACDC candidates P1 Candidates must carry evidence and stage 17 rows; stages 9 researching · 3 visit_needed · 3 shortlisted · 1 on_hold · 1 approved; 0 missing coordinates
F14 Map a radius territory at 390 px P1 Mobile users must read territories circle, label and target/trading counts legible; no horizontal scroll
F15 Recompute totals twice P0 Totals must be deterministic identical to the cent (pure function, no clock or random input)
F16 Study document registered with confidence unverified P1 Evidence must be a record, not a copy Durbanville enters with confidence = 'unverified'; PDF stays in WorkDrive
F17 Candidate approved with study_document_id P1 Approval must cite its source research_event row carries study title and page
F18 Radius territory recompute on centre change P0 Geometry must follow the radius changing label_point recomputes geom; area matches new centre
F19 Plan with zero territories cannot activate P0 An empty active plan reads as a bug activation rejected with 422
F20 Currency mixing rejected P1 Numbers without units are meaningless request mixing ZAR and USD without an exchange rate returns 422
F21 Monthly vs annual not shared P0 The zone_roy ambiguity must not recur a plan with an untyped amount field is rejected at validation

Architecture and API surface

Endpoint table (from network PRD §6; all follow house conventions: cursor pagination, revision checks on write, authorisation in the repository layer):

Method and path Who Returns / does
GET /api/network/territories?brand=&set= member territories with geometry, kind, radius, phase, target stores; fees only with pricing:read
GET /api/network/territories/:id member one territory + its stores + its candidates
GET /api/network/overlap?set= member intersecting pairs inside one set, with overlap area in km²
GET /api/network/candidates?brand=&stage= member candidates with study reference and score; notes/scores gated
PATCH /api/network/candidates/:id member stage, zone, score, study link; writes research_event; revision required
GET /api/network/plans?brand= member plans with status and version
POST /api/network/plans owner, manager create a draft from a territory set
POST /api/network/plans/:id/activate owner, manager exactly one active per brand; supersedes the previous
GET /api/network/plans/:id/totals member with pricing:read recomputed totals + arithmetic breakdown per territory
POST /api/network/studies owner, manager, analyst register a study document (no file upload in v1)

GET /api/network/plans/:id/totals returns the derivation, not just the answer:

{ "totals": { "territories": 38, "target_stores": 311, "fees": 12900000,
              "target_monthly_sales": 179261734, "royalty_month": 8963087 },
  "per_territory": [ { "code": "GT01", "target_stores": 14,
                       "target_monthly_sales": 7833913, "royalty_month": 235017 } ],
  "assumptions": { "royalty_pct": {"value": 5, "unit": "percent_of_turnover", "period": "month"} },
  "computed_at": "2026-10-28T09:00:00Z" }

Conventions: cursor pagination on lists; revision checks on write; idempotent imports via source ref; tenant scope on every query; all writes append to research_event or the territory audit table.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
A second zone scheme mistaken for the live one Two sets both look active one active plan per brand, unique index, supersession written to audit trail Stage C exit test F11
A royalty figure quoted in the wrong unit R5.38m shown as annual instead of monthly every number carries {value, unit, period}; the API rejects an untyped assumption schema + F6
Fees leak to a prospect through a share link Fee fields in a public payload field allow-list at creation and serialisation (existing D3 pattern, extended) F12
A candidate approved on a hunch Approved candidate with no study study_document_id or evidence_exempt F8, F9
A radius territory drifts from its radius Circle no longer matches 7 km radius authoritative, geometry derived, one repository function F3, F4
The 17 candidates read as 17 stores Pipeline count inflated plan screen shows approved separately from researched §5.4
Geocoding mistaken for a site Street centroid shown as a verified premises street-level geocodes flagged in the pack; visit_needed is the stage §8
Per-cluster rounding hidden Aggregate shown as exact output discloses per-cluster vs aggregate difference (R9 for GT01) F6
Currency mixing Mixed totals shown rejected without a recorded exchange-rate assumption F20
Empty plan activated An "active" plan computing to zero activation requires at least one territory F19
Stale write on a territory record Silent overwrite revision check with FOR UPDATE; HTTP 409 F2–F4

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: anonymous viewer, member, analyst, manager, owner, system (refresh worker).

Role/action matrix (summary): read Explore/map — all authenticated plus anonymous under share restrictions; create/edit candidate — member; approve candidate or scorecard — manager/owner; manage relationships — manager/owner; manage sharing — manager/owner; run ingestion — operator/owner; fees follow the existing pricing:read capability (no new role).

Stored: user credentials (scrypt), sessions, locations, brands, territories, research records, audit rows, study documents, plans, scenarios. Anchored outside the system: private database backups under .cache/backups/ (git-ignored), GitHub commit hash, share tokens (24 random bytes), original source files in WorkDrive.

Signature/approval honesty: approvals are role-gated database rows with actor and timestamp. They are not cryptographic signatures and are not non-repudiable.

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — foundations. Migration for zone_set.brand_id, zone territory columns, study_document, candidate_site additions, network_plan. Backfill Papa Pasta. Exit: F1 passes and the app still renders 5,073 locations and 135 zones.
  • Stage B — territories. Radius support (store, recompute, render), overlap endpoint, territory detail with target vs trading. Import TLF. Exit: F2–F5.
  • Stage C — plan arithmetic. planTotals() as a pure, tested function; network_plan CRUD; activation and supersession; totals endpoint with derivation. Exit: F6, F7, F11, F15.
  • Stage D — pipeline. Study registration, candidate brand/territory/study fields, approval guard, stage audit. Import ACDC. Exit: F8, F9, F13.
  • Stage E — surfaces. /network, /network/territories, /network/pipeline, /network/plans/:id; map additions; mobile pass at 390 px. Exit: F10, F12, F14 and a browser walk of all four surfaces.

Stages A–E are sequential; inside a stage the work is parallel. The incumbent (Zoho Projects briefs, the spreadsheets) keeps running until Stage E ships — the plan is read-only evidence until then.

This build order is the network PRD's own; where it overlaps with the consolidation PRD's stages (A–G in that document), the consolidation PRD's dependency ordering governs, because the consolidation PRD is the governing document.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
D1 Which Papa Pasta scheme becomes the active plan — the 135-zone set, the 96-zone paper, or Luke's 38 clusters? Superseded pending review: the consolidation PRD requires explicit geometry/rights review before any promotion and forbids automatic promotion (PAPA-03). Ricardo to decide after review. Activating 135 zones keeps a 2.8-stores-per-territory scheme live, which the zone-owner paper's own arithmetic says cannot pay a zone manager.
D2 Does the TLF territory stay at 7 km, or move to a named suburb boundary? Keep the 7 km radius. It is what the signed agreement annexure says; changing it is a legal act, not a product one. A polygon looks tidier on a map and contradicts the contract.
D3 Do we import the 17 ACDC candidates before the studies are re-verified? Import now, marked by confidence. A candidate row with a document and a page is more honest than an empty pipeline. Waiting means the five Namibian cities stay in a PDF.
D4 Does the Network branch ship fees to anyone below owner/manager? No — fees follow the existing pricing:read capability. No new role, no new flag. A second permission system to keep in sync.
D5 Is network_plan allowed to hold a plan with zero territories? No. A draft may be empty for the first save, but activation requires at least one territory. An "active" plan that computes to zero reads as a bug to every user who sees it.

PRD — INFX FND: research to site decision (enhanced)

13 features 2 P0 19 implemented 2 partial 0 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

  • The source PRD is titled "Core implementation delivered" but carries no per-capability evidence; this version adds a status ledger with a file/line citation on every row (see facts/infx-fnd-product.md).
  • Counts in the source (3,461 open stores, 22 closed, 46 brands) are dated 26 October 2026 observations; they are restated here as dated observations, not current facts, and the live-database counts (5,073 / 61 / 135) are marked UNVERIFIED because no database was queried in this task.
  • The Voltex "63 physical sites" claim is restated as a dated coverage observation with disputed coordinates, not a verified census.
  • R1–R8 are all implemented in code (src/lib/repo/research.ts, migrations 006–015); the remainder is now a numbered feature table (F1–F13) instead of a brainstorm table with no tests.
  • "Moderated usability 4 of 5 users" and "assistive-technology audit" are moved out of the delivered column and into the feature table as unverified acceptance targets.

Product in five lines

  • What it is: a multi-tenant franchise research workspace (Next.js + PostGIS) that maps competitor stores, scores candidate sites and records evidence.
  • Who uses it: sales and expansion staff at Papa Pasta, ACDC Express and The Local Farmer, plus anonymous viewers of restricted share links.
  • Decision it enables: where to expand, why an area is worth considering, and how reliable the evidence is.
  • What it is deliberately not: a business census, a revenue forecaster, a CRM, or a replacement for a site visit.
  • The number that says it works: 115 regression tests pass on the current commit, and the research workflow (R1–R8) is implemented end to end.

Domain model

Entities and the tables that carry them.

Entity Table Purpose
Company / tenant tenant A client workspace (Papa Pasta, ACDC Express, The Local Farmer)
Brand (shared catalogue) brand A franchise system or trading name, shared across tenants
Location location A physical site with a point geometry and status
Territory set zone_set A versioned set of zones owned by one tenant
Zone zone A polygon territory with demographics and pricing
Company-brand relationship tenant_brand_link The primary relationship for one (tenant, brand) pair
Research view research_view A named, versioned, live query over Explore state
Candidate site candidate_site A saved research candidate, never a trading store
Evidence brief research_snapshot An immutable comparison snapshot
Evidence review evidence_review An open issue on a record, dimension and source
Coverage observation coverage_observation A dated brand-coverage measurement with reconciliation
Company research profile company_research_profile Objective, freshness threshold, confirmation state
Optional commercial role company_brand_role Buyer/supplier/demand-anchor/investment role per (tenant, brand)
Scorecard version scorecard_version Approved criteria, weights and directions
Refresh schedule refresh_schedule A reviewed, opt-in ingestion schedule

Indexes that decide performance:

  1. location_geom_idx — GiST on location.geom (db/migrations/001_init.sql).
  2. zone_geom_idx — GiST on zone.geom (db/migrations/001_init.sql).
  3. location_geography_idx — GiST expression on (geom::geography) (db/migrations/012_research_decisions.sql).

Invariants:

  • I1. Tenant scope is applied in every query, export, attachment and saved-view resolver; a candidate or view from tenant A is never readable by tenant B.
  • I2. A candidate site is never a trading store and never enters store, brand or density counts.
  • I3. The primary relationship is one value per (tenant, brand); optional roles in company_brand_role never alter primary relationship totals.
  • I4. Counts are by distinct location ID (stores) and distinct brand ID (franchise systems); a multi-category brand is counted once.
  • I5. A missing coverage value is unknown, never zero and never a favourable ranking.
  • I6. Refresh never closes a store on a feed omission; closures require explicit evidence or a reviewable policy.
  • I7. Saved views are live queries; restoring them re-checks membership, visibility and territory existence.
  • I8. Anonymous viewers never receive pricing, source references, phone numbers or confidence fields.
  • I9. A scorecard with a missing mandatory input returns insufficient_evidence, not a score.
  • I10. Public briefs are granted only to an existing share link and only when the full catchment fits the link's geography.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Candidate lifecycle (states and transitions):

  • States: researching → visit_needed → shortlisted → on_hold → rejected → approved.
  • approved requires an approver (manager or owner); non-approvers receive HTTP 403.
  • Editing an approved candidate requires an approver.
  • Reassigning an owner requires an approver unless the new owner is the actor.
  • Every state change records actor, timestamp, reason and before/after in research_event.

Score arithmetic (pure function score() in src/lib/repo/research.ts:196-227):

  • Input: metrics (direct/indirect/complementary sites, own nearest km, access, operations) and approved criteria (metric, weight, low, high, direction, required).
  • Normalise each metric: (value - low) / (high - low), clamped to [0, 1].
  • Contribution: 100 * (higher ? normalised : 1 - normalised) * weight / 100.
  • If a required metric is null: status insufficient_evidence, score null.
  • If only an optional metric is null: status score_range, score null, range [lower, upper] where upper adds the missing weight.
  • Otherwise status scored, score = sum of contributions.

Comparison arithmetic (compare() in src/lib/repo/research.ts:563-690):

  • Radius: 0.1–50 km, single value applied to all candidates.
  • Per candidate: stores within geodesic radius, distinct brands, verified franchise systems (brand where network_type = 'franchise'), unknown-network brands, stale sites, unresolved reviews, nearest own-store distance.
  • Role subtotals (buyer, supplier, etc.) are reported separately and do not change relationship totals.

Edge cases:

  • Concurrency: research_view and candidate_site use revision with FOR UPDATE; a stale write returns HTTP 409.
  • Empty set: a comparison with zero matching stores returns total_sites = 0, distinct_brands = 0, not an error.
  • Duplicate candidates: within 500 m or matching normalised name, suggested for review; never auto-merged.
  • Self-approval: a non-approver cannot set or edit an approved candidate.
  • Withdrawal: setting rejected is reversible by an approver; no hard delete of history.
  • Exhaustion: radius is capped at 50 km; candidate count per comparison is 2–4.
  • Stale version: a scorecard version is immutable once created; a new calculation creates a new snapshot.

Feature table

ID Feature Priority Why it matters Acceptance test (expected value)
F1 Drive-time / delivery catchments P2 Straight-line radius misrepresents travel reach When a reviewed routing provider is enabled, a comparison shows travel minutes; without one it shows "not available", never a straight-line substitute
F2 Custom drawn catchment areas P1 Research is not limited to existing territories A saved candidate accepts a polygon; self-intersecting or out-of-bounds polygons are rejected with an error
F3 Field-visit checklist and attachments P2 Site visits capture entrance, access and parking A candidate stores checklist items and attachment references; attachments respect tenant scope
F4 CRM and task integration P2 Approved opportunities must enter execution Export of an approved candidate creates a task record in the agreed system; no automatic outreach without approval
F5 Natural-language research assistant P2 Explains filters and source-backed findings Every statement cites a source record; no invented conclusion when evidence is missing
F6 Revenue forecasting P2 Deferred experiment, not a release requirement No forecast is produced without a separately validated financial model; missing model returns "not available"
F7 Moderated usability trial P0 Automated checks are not a substitute for users At least 4 of 5 participants find a named area and explain direct/complementary counts within 2 minutes
F8 Assistive-technology audit P1 Code-level audit is not certification A screen-reader pass and 390 px reflow check complete with documented results; no WCAG claim before then
F9 Off-device backup and declared RTO P1 A local dump is not disaster recovery A restore from an off-device copy succeeds within the agreed RTO; result recorded
F10 Voltex coordinate resolution P0 Disputed pins distort proximity results Montana and Centurion reviews are resolved or remain explicitly disputed; no silent "verified" state
F11 Company objective and scorecard approval P1 Hypotheses are not approved criteria All three company_research_profile rows have confirmed = true with an approved_by actor
F12 Notification opt-in policy P1 Prevents unsolicited messages No email or chat message is sent unless a destination and preference exist; default is no notification
F13 Stable hexagon cell IDs P2 Current cells change with dataset and zoom The same input produces the same cell IDs across runs

Architecture and API surface

Package layout:

  • src/app — Next.js routes and pages (8 pages, 34 API handlers).
  • src/components — UI components (ZoneMap, ResearchHub, WorkspaceNav, MapResearchActions).
  • src/lib/repo — SQL data access; research.ts is the decision engine.
  • src/lib/ingest — adapters and refresh worker.
  • db/migrations — 14 migration files, 001–013 and 015.
  • test — 28 test files, 115 tests.

One import rule: every data-access function takes a tenantId and applies it in the SQL; no query may omit tenant scope.

Endpoint groups (representative; full list in src/app/api):

Group Routes Who may call
Explore/map /api/zones, /api/locations, /api/proximity, /api/places Any authenticated viewer; anonymous via share token
Research /api/research (GET/POST) Authenticated members; writes require role checks
Admin /api/admin/* Managers and owners
Sharing /api/admin/share-links, /api/share/[token]/* Admins create; anonymous reads under restrictions
Health /api/health Anyone (no secrets)

Conventions: cursor pagination on research lists; optimistic concurrency via revision; idempotent imports via source ref; tenant scope on every query; all writes append to research_event or relationship_edit.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Stale write on a view or candidate HTTP 409 "changed; reload" revision check with FOR UPDATE Implementer (tests)
Missing coverage data "Unknown coverage", not zero Coverage ratio only when numerator and denominator match geography and date Reviewer
Disputed coordinates Evidence review row remains open Montana/Centurion reviews created at migration 012 Data owner
Feed omission Store stays open; change held for review Refresh worker never closes on omission Operator
Revoked share access 404 "unavailable" Server-side check on every read Implementer (tests)
Pricing leak attempt 403 / field omitted price not in ShareField union Implementer (tests)
Icon fetch failure Last good icon or labelled fallback brand_icon.error; no block on map data Implementer
Slow external search/tiles Local filters still work; provider status shown Provider health separated from app health Operator

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: anonymous viewer, member, manager, owner, system (refresh worker).

Role/action matrix (summary): read Explore/map — all authenticated plus anonymous under share restrictions; create/edit candidate — member; approve candidate or scorecard — manager/owner; manage relationships — manager/owner; manage sharing — manager/owner; run ingestion — operator/owner.

Stored: user credentials (scrypt), sessions, locations, brands, territories, research records, audit rows. Anchored outside the system: private database backups under .cache/backups/ (git-ignored), GitHub commit hash, share tokens (24 random bytes).

Signature/approval honesty: approvals are role-gated database rows with actor and timestamp. They are not cryptographic signatures and are not non-repudiable.

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — Evidence and usability foundation (delivered): coverage model, issue queue, company readiness, coherent map/detail controls, reliable access. Exit: representative users can explain counts and gaps; tenant/security checks pass; no regression in map or bulk editing.
  • Stage B — Reusable research (delivered): saved views, candidate shortlist, audit trail, scoped permissions. Exit: restore a view; save/edit a candidate; verify candidates never inflate store counts.
  • Stage C — Decision workflow (delivered): common-catchment comparison, snapshot briefs, export. Exit: two-to-four site comparison matches independently computed fixtures; exports reproduce the snapshot.
  • Stage D — Explain and monitor (delivered): approved scorecards, scheduled source review, optional change digests. Exit: scores disclose missing evidence; suspicious refreshes cannot silently close stores; alert preferences respected.
  • Stage E — Deferred experiments (open): drive-time, custom catchments, field visits, CRM handoff, research assistant, revenue forecasting. Exit: separate scope, data/provider costs and pilot evidence approved.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 First-release scope A and B, then C (already delivered); D delivered without notifications Including D notifications adds operator and compliance work
2 Principal research objective per company, especially The Local Farmer Confirm buyer discovery vs growing-unit placement before scoring Scoring on unconfirmed objectives produces misleading rankings
3 Who may approve classifications, candidate stages and scorecard versions Managers and owners only (current code) Broader approval risks unaudited changes
4 Saved-view default visibility Private by default; company sharing explicit Company-default risks leaking research context
5 Catchment default per company Radius first; drive-time only if justified Drive-time needs a paid provider and licensing
6 Evidence freshness thresholds by source/brand Source-specific thresholds; do not assume daily refresh Uniform thresholds mislabel slow sources as stale
7 Supported access arrangement and operational owner Managed reconnecting local connection (already installed) Ad hoc terminal sessions break access
8 Brief format and audience Versioned PDF (browser print) and CSV first; server PDF later Server PDF adds a dependency and cost

  • Accessibility findings 13–16 remain open and are listed as features, not as completed work.
  • The status ledger separates delivered software from unverified human acceptance (screen-reader pass, 390 px reflow, moderated usability).

Product in five lines

  • What it is: the interface layer of INFX FND — a calmer map workspace with recognisable brands, structured discovery and consistent geographic counts.
  • Who uses it: the same sales and expansion staff, on desktop and mobile, plus anonymous share viewers.
  • Decision it enables: find and compare candidate sites without decoding a crowded map.
  • What it is deliberately not: a new application, a framework rewrite, or a replacement for the research workflow.
  • The number that says it works: 115 regression tests pass, and the map readiness p95 is 1,365 ms against a 3,000 ms target.

Domain model

Shared with PRODUCT-IMPROVEMENTS-PRD-v2-enhanced.md §5; the UI reads the same tables. UI-specific invariants:

  • U1. One validated query state drives the map, list, legend, summary and detail views.
  • U2. Province scoping uses geometry, not a text-field match alone.
  • U3. Store count = matching location IDs; brand count = matching brand IDs; owner-group count is a third, separately named measure.
  • U4. A filtered figure is labelled "matching results"; the unfiltered total is labelled "all recorded stores in this zone".
  • U5. Catalogue brands with zero mapped stores remain discoverable with "0 mapped stores"; they are never silently removed.
  • U6. Relationship colour is never the only channel identifying a brand; name and icon always accompany it.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

The UI has no scoring engine of its own; it renders the comparison engine from the product PRD. UI state transitions:

  • Area: National → Province → Zone. Moving the camera is distinct from changing the analysis boundary; panning alone does not change totals unless "Current map extent" is selected.
  • Filter state: OR within a facet, AND across facets; reset display mode does not erase research filters.
  • Company switch: reloads own-brand network, relationships, presets, territories and results; clears incompatible selections with an explanation.

Edge cases: late responses after a company switch are rejected; a late national response never replaces a selected zone's results; unsaved changes are visible.

Architecture and API surface

  • Component boundaries: application shell, company selector, filter panel, brand row, map toolbar, scoped summary, detail drawer.
  • Reuse: the same identity and category rendering is used in Explore and Catalogue.
  • Import rule: tenant ID is included in every query, response, cache key, saved state and internal URL.
  • Asset pipeline: one cached icon per brand; no network request per store marker; fetches run in a bounded background job, not in the interactive map request.

INFX Media Website — Enhanced PRD

1. What this version fixes

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a statically exported Next.js 16 marketing site for INFX Solutions, selling "TakeOver" in-store retail-media campaigns to South African QSR brands.
  • Who uses it: QSR marketing directors and brand managers evaluating a TakeOver; internal sales using the pricing and case-study pages.
  • Decision or action it exists to enable: a visitor books a 30-minute TakeOver briefing via the 12-field form, or downloads the media kit / subscribes from the footer.
  • Deliberately not: a backend, a CMS, an authenticated app, or a live analytics product. It is a static site with two third-party form endpoints.
  • Single number that says it works: 13 page routes build with exit 0 (npm run build → 17/17 static pages), 0 lint errors, 0 type errors.

4. Domain model

Entities (one line each):

  • LeadSubmission — a briefing request captured from the 12-field form; today it is a client-side object posted to Web3Forms and never stored here.
  • PricingTier — a City/Region/National TakeOver package with price range, CPM, impressions, attention time and feature list.
  • CaseStudyMetric — a before/after measurement quoted on the case-study page (foot traffic, sponsor revenue, technician time).
  • CompetitorBenchmark — a row in the intel comparison (CPM, engagement, reach, trend) for INFX vs. five alternatives.
  • QSRCampaign — a published QSR campaign outcome row (brand, channel, spend, outcome, ROI).
  • ClientTile — a logo slot in SocialProof (name + initials + colour).

Carrying tables (source files, since there is no database): | Entity | Source file | Key columns (type) | |---|---|---| | LeadSubmission | src/app/book-briefing/page.tsx formData state | firstName (string), lastName (string), email (string), phone (string), company (string), title (string), category (string), tier (string), budget (string), brandVision (string), consent (boolean), honeypot (string) | | PricingTier | src/app/packages-pricing/page.tsx tiers[] | name (string), stores (string), priceRange (string), cpm (string), impressions (string), attention (string), features (string[]) | | CaseStudyMetric | src/app/case-study/page.tsx metrics[] | label (string), before (string), after (string), accent (string) | | CompetitorBenchmark | src/app/intel/page.tsx COMPETITORS[] | name (string), type (string), cpm (number), engagement (number), reach (number), trend (string) | | QSRCampaign | src/app/intel/page.tsx QSR_CAMPAIGNS[] | brand (string), channel (string), spend (string), outcome (string), roi (string) | | ClientTile | src/app/components/SocialProof.tsx clients[] | name (string), initial (string), color (string) |

Performance indexes (what actually decides load time):

  1. page.tsx:16-19 — next/dynamic with ssr: false for NeuralNetworkBackground (keeps canvas out of SSR bundle).
  2. Next.js route-level code splitting — one JS bundle per route (235.0–251.4 KB gzip per enhancement PRD E8).
  3. trailingSlash: true in next.config.ts — determines static asset path resolution for the export.

Invariants:

  • I1 output: 'export' in next.config.ts means no server runtime exists in this build; no src/app/api route may be added without changing hosting.
  • I2 Every form POST leaves the origin for https://api.web3forms.com/submit; there is no first-party persistence, so a successful HTTP response is not proof of a stored lead.
  • I3 A/B variant is assigned once per browser and stored in localStorage under infx_ab_hero_cta; getSnapshot must not reassign on repeat reads or the hook loops.
  • I4 safeJsonLdStringify is the only serializer for JSON-LD; dangerouslySetInnerHTML count must remain 0.
  • I5 prefers-reduced-motion: reduce must disable Framer Motion globally via MotionConfig reducedMotion="user" and gate every canvas loop; new animated components must check matchMedia.
  • I6 Pricing CPM figures (R0.80 / R0.53 / R0.32) are quoted to two decimals and must be recomputed, not re-typed, if impressions or prices change.

5. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

The only runtime engine is the hero CTA A/B test (src/lib/ab-test.ts) plus the form submission lifecycle.

A/B test (useHeroAB):

  • States: control | variant-a | variant-b.
  • Store: localStorage key infx_ab_hero_cta.
  • Pure function getHeroCopy(variant) returns { headline, cta } for the three states.
  • Transitions: first visit → assign VARIANTS[Math.floor(Math.random()*3)], persist, return; later visits → return stored value; invalid stored value → treated as first visit and reassigned.
  • Server snapshot → control (SSR and hydration render control copy; client swaps after hydration).
  • Cross-tab: storage event re-runs getSnapshot via useSyncExternalStore.
  • Edge cases enumerated:
    • Empty set: localStorage.getItem returns null → first-visit assignment path.
    • Stale version: stored string not in VARIANTS → isVariant false → reassign.
    • Withdrawal: user clears storage → treated as first visit on next read.
    • Exhaustion: all three variants remain eligible forever (no exhaustion); assignment is uniform random, not balanced.
    • Concurrency: cross-tab storage event propagates changes; same-tab writes do not fire storage, so two tabs can disagree until reload.
    • Duplicate: repeated getSnapshot calls return the same value (assignment happens only when stored value is invalid).
    • Self-approval: n/a — no approval step exists; the client assigns its own variant with no server check.
    • Tracking: trackClick calls window.gtag only if present, then console.log; with no analytics script installed, clicks are logged to the console only.

Form submission lifecycle (src/app/book-briefing/page.tsx):

  • States: idle → validating → submitting → success | error.
  • Pure validation: validate() requires firstName, valid email, company, consent === true.
  • Honeypot: if formData.honeypot is non-empty, submit is silently dropped.
  • On success: leadId = INFX- + 8 uppercase alphanumeric chars (client-generated, not server-verified).
  • Edge cases: malformed email rejected client-side only (no server-side validation exists); network failure surfaces errors.submit.

7. Architecture and API surface

Package layout (import rule: pages import from src/app/sections, src/app/components, and src/lib only; no page imports another page; content constants live in the component that renders them):

infx-media-website/
├─ src/app/
│  ├─ layout.tsx            # root metadata, JSON-LD, skip link
│  ├─ page.tsx              # home composition
│  ├─ <route>/page.tsx      # 13 routes
│  ├─ sections/             # 8 scroll narrative slices
│  ├─ components/           # 17 shared UI (5 unused)
│  ├─ sitemap.ts / robots.ts
│  ├─ error.tsx / loading.tsx / not-found.tsx
├─ src/lib/ab-test.ts       # A/B engine

└─ .github/workflows/deploy.yml

API / command surface (there are no first-party endpoints): | Surface | Caller | Notes | |---|---|---| | POST https://api.web3forms.com/submit | book-briefing + Footer forms | third party; sentinel key YOUR_ACCESS_KEY | | npm run dev/build/start/lint/typecheck | developer / CI | scripts in package.json | | docker build | operator | multi-stage Node 20 → nginx |

Conventions the build must keep:

  • output: 'export' with trailingSlash: true: no server runtime, no API routes in the same build.
  • Static assets under public/; headers defined in public/_headers (Cloudflare Pages) and Dockerfile nginx (Docker).
  • TypeScript strict: true; npm run typecheck must exit 0.
  • Lint must exit 0 (0 errors, 0 warnings).

9. Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: anonymous visitor (read all pages, submit forms), form submitter (sends payload to Web3Forms), deployer (CI via GitHub Actions), host (GitHub Pages / Cloudflare / nginx).

Role / action matrix: | Principal | View pages | Submit form | Change content | Change config | |---|---|---|---|---| | Anonymous visitor | yes | yes | no | no | | Form submitter | yes | yes | no | no | | Deployer (CI) | yes | yes | yes (via git) | yes | | Host | serves | n/a | no | serves headers |

What is stored: nothing server-side in this repo. Form payloads are transmitted to Web3Forms. Browser localStorage holds the A/B variant key infx_ab_hero_cta. No cookies are set by application code.

What is anchored outside the system: Web3Forms receives submissions; GitHub Pages (or chosen host) serves static assets; _headers/nginx define CSP. The lead ID is client-generated and is not a security token or receipt.

Honesty statement: there is no signature, approval, or tamper-evident record anywhere. POPIA consent is captured as a form field value ("Yes"/"No") inside the email payload, not as a durable consent record. There is no server-side validation, so a crafted POST bypasses all client checks. Any claim of "POPIA-compliant data capture" refers to the form text, not to an audit.

10. Build order by dependency

Stage 0 — Verification (baseline)

  • Work: run npm run lint, npm run typecheck, npm run build, npm audit; record counts in the facts file.
  • Exit test: lint exit 0 AND typecheck exit 0 AND build exit 0 AND audit count recorded.

Stage 1 — Conversion integrity (P0)

  • Work (parallel): configure or replace the form backend key (F13/F39); implement first-party persistence or choose Web3Forms for real (F30); add CRM webhook (F31); fire briefing_submitted (F32); fix lead ID to be server-issued (F14).
  • Exit test: a preview submission returns a persisted lead ID within 5 seconds and the analytics event is recorded.

Stage 2 — Trust and correctness

  • Work (parallel): verify or remove fictional social proof (F21/F37); verify pricing arithmetic with finance (F17/F18, D5); self-host OG image (F29); add per-route canonicals (F28); delete 5 unused components (F27).
  • Exit test: all 13 routes carry a canonical URL matching their path; OG image resolves locally; unused-component grep returns 0.

Stage 3 — Measurement

  • Work (parallel): mount useHeroAB in the hero (F33); add scroll-depth and CTA events (F32); A/B copy wired via getHeroCopy.
  • Exit test: GA4 receives briefing_submitted and ab_hero_cta_click in the network tab on a test submission.

Stage 4 — Security and delivery

  • Work (parallel): move delivery to a host that honours public/_headers (D2); run npm audit in CI (F38); update the 26 vulnerable dependencies.
  • Exit test: curl -I on the live URL returns all 6 headers AND the CI audit step is green.

Stage 5 — Content operations

  • Work (parallel): introduce CMS for FAQ/pricing (F35); publish a verified case study (F19/F37); add cross-site links (F36); soften or verify competitive intel (F20).
  • Exit test: an FAQ edit in the CMS reflects after rebuild with no code change.

11. Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
D1 Form backend: keep Web3Forms with a real key, or build a first-party /api/leads? If lead persistence is required, build first-party; otherwise configure the Web3Forms key Web3Forms = $0 and no persistence; first-party = requires hosting change away from static export
D2 Hosting target: GitHub Pages, Cloudflare Pages, or Vercel? Cloudflare Pages (free, honours _headers, keeps static export) GitHub Pages = free but no headers; Vercel = free tier + SSR/API but changes the build model
D3 Analytics: GA4 only, GA4 + PostHog, or none? GA4 + PostHog (free tiers) if conversion is to be measured GA4-only = cheaper, no session replay; none = zero visibility
D4 Fictional client tiles in SocialProof: keep illustrative or remove? Remove until a real client grants permission (F37) Keeping risks a trust/legal incident; removing reduces social proof
D5 Are the pricing CPM figures (R0.80/R0.53/R0.32) and "225x/380x/937x cheaper" derived from real impression counts? Finance verifies or softens to ranges Unverified numbers risk a CFO rejecting the proposal
D6 Should the competitive intel page stay public with mock data? Keep but label "illustrative comparison" Removing loses a differentiation page; keeping unlabelled over-claims

maio-os PRD-v2-enhanced.md source ↗

Maio OS — Cockpit PRD (enhanced)

18 features 5 P0 7 implemented 5 partial 14 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

  1. PRD.md claimed a React 18 + TypeScript + Vite migration, a @maio/ui package, Yarn/Lerna monorepo, Apollo/Express GraphQL + OpenAPI, GitHub Actions + Colima, Docusaurus, OAuth 2.0/OIDC and Grafana/Loki. None of these exist: the cockpit is a vanilla-JS section workspace served from one index.html with a Python generator and a Node companion. Evidence: index.html:1, PRODUCT.md:1-51, DESIGN.md:1-12. The old document is a stale proposal, not a description of the product.
  2. PRD.md acceptance criteria were all ticked ✅ with no evidence. Under the strength standard a capability with no proof is absent, so every row in the status ledger below is evidenced against the code at d304b61.
  3. PRD.md proposed floating windows, pop-up search and a desktop metaphor in places; PRODUCT.md:14-15 and DESIGN.md:233-238 make the fixed section workspace an explicit product rule. Any future proposal that reintroduces floating UI is rejected by the invariants in §5.
  4. Thin areas deepened: navigation is now specified as a state machine with the pure parseHash function and its edge cases; the gated-action path is specified with its cooldown arithmetic; the API surface is an endpoint table with callers; security is stated honestly as an origin/host/token guard rather than as OAuth.

Product in five lines

  • What it is: a fixed-section household cockpit — 18 sections across Workspace, Work, Life, Knowledge, System — rendered from one index.html plus a generated data.js snapshot and a local companion on 127.0.0.1:9111.
  • Who uses it: Ricardo alone, on this Mac and over the tailnet, to inspect projects, agents, documents, machine vitals and the household's esoteric practice.
  • Decision it enables: "what needs attention now, and where does a record live" — answered in 0–4 ms per section hop with provenance stamped on every screen.
  • Deliberately not: a window desktop, a multi-user SaaS, a public website, or a React application. Legacy window geometry is ignored.
  • Number that says it works: 18 sections render, all 13 core sections open in 0–4 ms, contrast sweep 0 failures, 0 horizontal overflow at 1440×900 and 390×844 (DESIGN.md audit pass).

Domain model

Entities (one line each):

  • Section — a fixed top-level destination in the workspace (Overview, Projects, …); carries a renderer and a visibility flag.
  • Project — an indexed repo under DevMini; carries name, path, status, counts.
  • Agent — a roster entry (name, role, portrait, sheet link).
  • PRD — a product document discovered by the generator; keyed by document path, not by title.
  • LibraryItem — a published page or internal report shown in the Library shelves.
  • StackProbe — a service probe result (live/down, port, latency) shown on Stack.
  • MachineReading — RAM, models, sessions, launchd, docker counts from the companion.
  • KanbanTask — a task on the fleet board (Scheduled/Triage columns included).
  • VaultNote — a read-only note from the Maio Hub vault, opened via /api/vault/note.
  • FileView — a read-only view of an AI-Drive report opened via /files/<rel>.
  • AlmanacReading — a dated daily reading from the vault plus a computed moon.
  • Practice — The Path manual and grimoire content, parsed not summarised.

Carrying tables (in the data.js snapshot, not a database):

  • projects[], roster[], prds[], stack[], library.public[], library.internal[], docs[], summary{}.
  • companion holds no cockpit database; Wheel and Astro own their own SQLite/Prisma stores.

Indexes that decide performance:

  • SERVICE_BY_LABEL Map in companion/collectors.mjs — O(1) service lookup for restart requests.
  • FILE_EXTS Set and VAULT_NOTE_ALLOW Set in companion/server.mjs — O(1) extension gating.
  • VIEWS Map in index.html — one instance per section, so scroll position survives navigation.

Numbered invariants:

  • I1. data.js is never hand-edited; the generator owns it (data.js:1, DESIGN.md don't-rules). A hand edit is a defect even if it renders.
  • I2. No floating windows, dock, or pop-up search exist in the workspace; details open in the main area with breadcrumbs (DESIGN.md:233-238, PRODUCT.md:14-15).
  • I3. Beast is the only vault writer; the cockpit and companion only read the vault (PRODUCT.md:30, companion/server.mjs:934-940).
  • I4. Snapshot and live data must stay visually distinguishable; gaps render as honest empty states, never invented records (PRODUCT.md:19, DESIGN.md:112-113).
  • I5. Routes are bookmarkable and participate in ordinary Back/Forward history; unknown or malformed fragments fall back to Overview (index.html:2851-2904).
  • I6. Gated actions require confirm===true, an allowlisted action, an allowed origin/host, and (for non-loopback) MAIO_FIX_TOKEN; cooldowns are 60 s per service and 5 min for refresh (companion/server.mjs:865-895).

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Navigation engine (the one engine in the cockpit):

States: overview | section | detail (project/agent) | file (AI-Drive report). detail and file are the two reused views and are never addressable without a subject (index.html:2855-2858).

Transitions: applyHash → parseHash → applyLink → showSection / openDetail / openFile. hashchange re-runs applyHash; Back/Forward follow section history.

Pure function at the core: parseHash(hash) in index.html:2851-2904. Inputs: a raw hash string. Outputs: one of {kind:'section',id}, {kind:'project',name,n}, {kind:'agent',name}, {kind:'file',rel}, {kind:'report',q,shelf}, {kind:'almanacDay',date}, {kind:'projectMiss',name}, {kind:'agentMiss',name}, or {kind:'invalid'}.

Edge cases enumerated:

  • Empty hash → null → Overview.
  • #desk alias → Overview (index.html:2856).
  • Hash longer than HASH_MAX → invalid.
  • Bad %-encoding → invalid (caught).
  • Plain unknown section id → invalid → Overview.
  • #detail / #file without a subject → invalid (no empty view).
  • #project: with no name → invalid.
  • #project:name@k with k < 1 or k > 99 → invalid.
  • #project:name that matches no project → projectMiss (list + notice, not a crash).
  • #agent: with no name → invalid; unknown agent → agentMiss.
  • #file: with absolute path, dot-segment, disallowed extension, or > 300 chars → invalid.
  • #report: / #docs: with no query → invalid.
  • #almanac: with a non-YYYY-MM-DD suffix → invalid.
  • Any other head:rest → invalid.

Gated-action path (the second engine): idle → confirm (UI two-click) → server validation (action allowlist, confirm===true, origin, host, token) → cooldown check (60 s service / 5 min refresh) → execution → log entry (FIX_LOG_CAP 50) → response. Edge cases: concurrency — one action at a time (fixBusy); duplicate — blocked by cooldown; withdrawal — the confirm step can be abandoned with no server call; exhaustion — cooldown returns retryInSec; self-approval — not applicable (single user; the guard is the host/token boundary, not a second person); stale version — in-memory cooldowns reset on server restart, which is accepted.

Architecture and API surface

Package layout (one import rule: data.js is generator-owned; nothing in index.html or the companion may write to it):

  • index.html — shell, renderers, navigation, inline scripts.
  • assets/ — theme.css, workspace.css, icons.svg, portraits.
  • companion/ — server.mjs, collectors.mjs, almanac.mjs, path.mjs, okmcp.mjs.
  • generator/ — maio_os_gen.py, maio_os_merge.py.
  • ops/ — deploy.sh, launchd plists, refresh script.
  • apps/ — astro, top8, wheel, maiotastic (each owns its build).

Endpoint table (who may call): | Endpoint | Method | Caller | Auth/guard | |---|---|---|---| | /, /index.html, /data.js, /assets/* | GET | browser | none (static) | | /local.js | GET | browser | host allowlist; 25 s cache | | POST /api/fix | POST | browser (cockpit) | action allowlist, confirm===true, origin, host, MAIO_FIX_TOKEN for non-loopback, cooldown | | GET /api/fix/log | GET | browser | host allowlist | | GET /api/vault/search | GET | browser | origin (absent on loopback OK) | | GET /api/vault/note | GET | browser | origin + .md/.mdx extension | | GET /files/<rel> | GET | browser | extension allowlist, no traversal, no dotfiles | | GET /health | GET | monitor (proposed F16) | none |

Conventions: no pagination (single-user, small payloads); idempotency for refresh-data via cooldown, not via request id; versioning not implemented — if a v2 of the companion API is ever added, prefix /api/v2/ rather than changing shapes in place.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
data.js missing or unparseable Failure state with description; cockpit does not render sections Boot guard in index.html sets markFailed Builder (manual)
Companion offline LOCAL_STATUS = offline/absent; machine/vault sections show honest empty states; folded apps show companion note ON_COMPANION gate + LOCAL_STATUS Builder (manual)
Vault read wedges Almanac section degrades; other cards still land 8 s withTimeout in collector; re-read only when reports dir changes Builder (manual)
Service restart fails Toast/receipt with error; cooldown still recorded restartService probe + receipt Builder (manual)
Refresh hammered 429 with retryInSec 5 min cooldown on refresh-data Automated (unit test proposed)
Unknown/malformed route Falls back to Overview parseHash returns invalid → Overview Automated (proposed F17)
Stale snapshot "Snapshot · " stamp + warnbox Generator freshness + UI label Builder (manual)

A row with no automated control is a risk accepted in v2; F15/F17 convert the manual rows to automated ones.

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: Ricardo (single user), tailnet clients (read-only pages), local processes (collectors, generator).

Role/action matrix: there is no role system. The only privileged operations are the 5 gated actions, and they are guarded by origin + host + optional token + cooldown, not by identity. Vault writes are not available through this system at all (I3).

What is stored: data.js snapshot, companion in-memory cooldowns, fix log (last 50 entries, JSONL), Wheel/Astro SQLite stores. No passwords or tokens are stored in the repo; MAIO_FIX_TOKEN lives only in the environment.

What is anchored outside the system: the vault (Maio Hub) is the source of truth for esoteric content and is written only by Beast; data.js is regenerated, not edited.

Honest statement of strength: the origin/host/token guard is a same-origin + network-boundary control, not cryptographic authentication. It stops casual cross-site and DNS-rebinding abuse on a trusted machine; it does not prove who the user is and it is not a substitute for OAuth. Anyone with shell access to this Mac can call the endpoints directly. That is acceptable for a single-user local cockpit and is the reason OAuth is a non-goal.

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — Lock the core. Work: document and unit-test parseHash edge cases; add in-repo smoke test for the 18 sections; keep node --check and python3 -m py_compile green. Exit test: smoke script prints 18/18 sections rendered and all parseHash edge cases pass.
  • Stage B — Harden the companion API. Work: add /health (F16) and an OpenAPI document (F13); convert the rate limiter to a tested module. Exit test: GET /health returns 200 with uptime and the OpenAPI document validates.
  • Stage C — Deployment artifacts. Work: add a root Dockerfile (F14) and a GitHub Actions workflow (F15). Exit test: docker build exits 0 and serves index.html; the workflow runs green on a PR.
  • Stage D — Monitoring and regression. Work: wire an optional health probe; add accessibility regression to CI. Exit test: CI reports 0 a11y violations on the two required viewport sizes.

No dates. Stages are ordered by dependency; A must pass before B, B before C.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Should the cockpit migrate to React/TypeScript as the old PRD proposed? No — keep the vanilla section workspace; treat the old migration as a non-goal Yes costs a full rewrite and risks the proven 0–4 ms navigation and accessibility baseline; No keeps the shipped, audited product
2 Should a public GitHub Pages build be restored? No — keep the private repo + companion-only model Yes re-opens the host/origin and SEO surface that was deliberately closed; No keeps the security boundary simple
3 Should monitoring (Grafana/Loki) be added now? Defer — single user, no SLO to meet Now costs setup and noise for no benefit; defer keeps focus on features
4 Should cockpit tests live in-repo or in the external harness? Add a small in-repo smoke suite (F17) and keep the deep UI audit external In-repo only loses the 53-scenario depth; external only keeps the repo untestable by CI

Esoteric Command — Astro Cartography PRD (enhanced)

24 features 8 P0 33 implemented 0 partial 4 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

  1. apps/astro/PRD.md described a create-next-app skeleton with zero astrology logic, no engines, no security headers, no tests and no CI. The repo now ships a full Next.js 16 application: 34 pages, 43 API routes, 24 Prisma models, 84 lib modules, 74 components, 34 test files and 540 passing tests. The code governs; the old PRD is a stale proposal.
  2. apps/astro/PRD.md listed every gap as P0/P1 with no evidence and proposed middleware.ts + output: 'export'. The shipped control surface is src/proxy.ts (Next.js 16 proxy) with CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options, and the app is SSR because swisseph is a native addon; static export is impossible and is not a goal.
  3. apps/astro/PRD_FEATURE.md acceptance criteria were ticked as satisfied by code; this document re-verifies each against src/app/lib/report-pdf.ts, report-share.ts, the three /api/report/* routes, /report, /share/[token] and scripts/verify-report.ts.
  4. Thin areas deepened: the family-location sweep is now specified as a state machine with its ranking arithmetic and its exploratory gate; the cockpit mount and origin rules are stated as invariants; the security header set is an explicit table with its one deliberate exception (SAMEORIGIN).

Product in five lines

  • What it is: a Next.js 16 server-rendered family astrology workspace — natal charts, synastry, transits, astrocartography, Vedic, BaZi, Human Design and a family location sweep — mounted at /astro inside the Maio OS cockpit.
  • Who uses it: Ricardo, and the family through read-only share links on the local network. No accounts, no cloud, no analytics.
  • Decision it enables: "where should the Maio family live, and what is the cosmic weather for a person or date" — always returned with an explicit availability label (exploratory or verified) and an accuracy register.
  • Deliberately not: a standalone public website, a prediction engine, a verified ranking authority, or a multi-user SaaS. The cockpit is the only entry point.
  • Number that says it works: 34 pages, 43 API routes, 24 Prisma models, 540 tests passing, 0 TypeScript errors, 0 lint errors, build exit 0.

Domain model

Entities and the tables that carry them (SQLite via Prisma, 24 models in prisma/schema.prisma).

Indexes that decide performance:

  1. transit_logs(member_id, date) — prisma/schema.prisma:188 — drives daily and forecast pages.
  2. location_assessments(location_type, location_name) — prisma/schema.prisma:348 — drives place assessment and the sweep.
  3. astrocartography_lines(member_id) and location_bookmarks(member_id) — prisma/schema.prisma:329,393 — reused across every town in the sweep.

Numbered invariants:

  • I1. Every calculation reads birth inputs from family_members; no calculation invents a birth date, time or coordinate. A member with unresolved inputs is excluded and named in skippedMembers.
  • I2. No engine runs against mocked ephemeris data. verify-report.ts asserts the sweep returns 28 real towns with real harmony scores; if the sweep throws, the PDF export fails with HTTP 500 rather than emitting a placeholder.
  • I3. Verified family rankings stay withheld unless exploratory=true is passed explicitly (calculation-context.ts:48-50). The default path returns HTTP 422 with availability: "unverified".
  • I4. The cockpit is the only entry point: the app binds 127.0.0.1:3212 (companion/server.mjs:617) and is reverse-proxied under /astro. No public host, no auth, no analytics.
  • I5. src/proxy.ts sets X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN and CSP frame-ancestors 'self'. DENY or 'none' is forbidden because it blanks the cockpit frame with no error.
  • I6. data.js is generator-owned and is never hand-edited; the app does not read or write it.
  • I7. src/app/lib/base-path.ts is the single declaration of the mount point (BASE_PATH = "/astro"); next.config.ts imports it. A hand-written fetch("/api/...") is a defect.
  • I8. The location model is labelled interpretive in the accuracy register (accuracy-register.ts, id location); UI copy may not present a family harmony score as a real-world suitability verdict.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

The product has one engine: the family location sweep behind /best and the report. It is a pure-ish function over real stored ACG lines.

States:

  • idle — no member selection; the page renders the candidate list only.
  • input-invalid — one or more selected members lack a resolvable birth input; the sweep throws CalculationInputError and names the member and the issue.
  • exploratory-blocked — exploratory !== true; withCalculationContext returns HTTP 422 { availability: "unverified" } before any computation.
  • computing — members resolved, lines loaded once, towns scored.
  • ranked — towns sorted and returned with calculation.availability = "exploratory".
  • failed — sweep threw; the API returns 500 and no report is produced.

Transitions:

  • idle -> exploratory-blocked on any calculate request without exploratory=true.
  • exploratory-blocked -> computing only when the caller passes exploratory=true AND every selected member passes birthInputIssues and effectiveTimezoneOffset.
  • computing -> ranked when at least one member has lines; assessedMembers = memberLines.length, totalMembers = members.length.
  • computing -> input-invalid when a member's ensureMemberAcgLines throws (the error is re-thrown as CalculationInputError).
  • computing -> failed when memberLines is empty ("No profiles could be assessed...").
  • ranked -> idle on a new selection.

Core pure function: familyHarmonyScore(domains) over 12 wheel domains, 0..100, and familyRankingScores(members):

  • memberThemeScore = sum(domains[d] for d in WHEEL_DOMAINS) / 12.
  • averageScore = mean(memberThemeScore).
  • balancedScore = min(memberThemeScore).
  • Sort key: balanced mode → balancedScore desc, then averageScore desc; average mode → averageScore desc, then balancedScore desc; tie-break name asc (compareFamilyPlaces).

Edge cases enumerated:

  • Empty family: familyRankingScores throws "An empty family cannot be ranked." — no ranking is produced.
  • Member with unresolved birth input: excluded before scoring; named in skippedMembers with a reason; if all members fail, the sweep throws.
  • Member whose ACG lines cannot be loaded: the whole sweep aborts with CalculationInputError rather than silently dropping that member.
  • Optional paran crossings: computed in a separate try/catch; a failure there is logged and does not hide a successful main-line calculation.
  • Town with no active lines: still scored; affectedMembers may be 0 and the town remains in the list.
  • Duplicate paran blends across members: deduplicated by planetA-angleA-planetB-angleB-lat.
  • Stale version: CALCULATION_VERSION = "accuracy-2.0" and methodVersion are stamped on every result; inputFingerprint is a SHA-256 over the selected members.
  • Self-approval: not applicable — there is no approval step; the exploratory gate is the only control and it is enforced server-side.
  • Withdrawal: a share row persists for 365 days; expiry is enforced in getShare (expires_at.getTime() < Date.now() → null).
  • Exhaustion: 28 candidate towns only; the list is fixed in candidate-towns.ts and is not generated at runtime.

F15 Daily briefing with audio P2 Today's signal in one screen /daily returns 200 and /api/daily-briefing and /api/daily-briefing/audio return 200
F16 Celestial globe P2 Visualises ACG lines on a sphere /globe returns 200 and renders without console errors
F17 Vedic astrology and Tara Bala P2 Sidereal chart and compatibility categories /vedic and /tarabala return 200 with ayanamsa labelled
F18 Human Design gates P2 Gate and channel activations /hd-gates returns 200 with defined/undefined centres
F19 Solar returns P2 Annual return charts /solar returns 200 with a solved return date
F20 Progressions and solar arc P2 Timing of personal cycles /progressions returns 200 with progressed positions
F21 Eclipses atlas P2 Eclipse catalogue and personal contacts /eclipses returns 200 with NASA-catalogue anchors
F22 Local space P2 Azimuth-based relocation view /local-space returns 200
F23 Relocation analysis P2 Recomputed houses and angles at a destination /relocate returns 200 with relocated chart
F24 Itinerary planner P2 Travel timing against personal cycles /itinerary returns 200 and persists via /api/itineraries

Architecture and API surface

Package layout and the one import rule: src/app/lib/base-path.ts declares the mount point once; next.config.ts and every hand-written URL consume it. No component may write fetch("/api/...").

Endpoint table (selected; all routes live under /astro because of basePath):

Method Path Caller Purpose
GET /api/members client components list family members
GET/PUT/DELETE /api/members/[id] client components read, update, delete a member
GET /api/best-location /best, /report run the family sweep (gated by exploratory)
GET /api/location-assessment /assess assess one place for one or more members
POST /api/report/pdf /report build and download the branded PDF
POST /api/report/share /report create a share snapshot
GET /api/report/share/[token] /share/[token] read a snapshot (404 if unknown/expired)
GET /api/synastry-report/pdf synastry UI synastry PDF
POST /api/synastry-report/share synastry UI synastry share snapshot
GET /api/daily-briefing /daily daily briefing payload
GET /api/daily-briefing/audio /daily TTS audio
GET /api/daily-briefing/script /daily spoken script
GET /api/globe /globe globe data
GET /api/astrocartography /acg ACG lines
GET /api/forecast /forecast forecast window
GET /api/transits etc. timing pages transits, eclipses, progressions, solar returns, vedic, tarabala, bazi-luck, hd-gate-transits, local-space, relocation, itinerary, bookmarks

Conventions:

  • Pagination: none; result sets are small (28 towns, 13 family members) and are returned whole.
  • Idempotency: POST /api/report/share and POST /api/synastry-report/share always create a new row; callers must not retry on a 5xx without checking the list first.
  • Versioning: none. The calculation version is stamped in the payload (methodVersion: "accuracy-2.0"), not in the URL.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Unresolved birth input CalculationInputError naming the member and the issue; sweep aborts birthInputIssues + effectiveTimezoneOffset in selectedCalculationMembers Sage
Missing ACG lines member cannot be assessed; named in skippedMembers with a reason ensureMemberAcgLines throws and is re-thrown Sage
Exploratory gate closed HTTP 422 { availability: "unverified" } with reviewUrl: "/accuracy" withCalculationContext server-side Wolverine
Real sweep throws HTTP 500, no PDF, no share row buildFamilyReportPdf propagates the error Forge
Unknown or expired share token HTTP 404 getShare returns null Forge
Host header injection on share URL falls back to http://localhost:3212 regex /^[A-Za-z0-9.\-]+(:\d{1,5})?$/ in shareUrlFor Wolverine
Native addon missing after Node upgrade npm ci / build fails .nvmrc pins Node 22; Dockerfile rebuilds addons Colossus
Phantom PDF pages regression guard in verify-report.ts fails footer ownership and bottom margin handling Forge
Cockpit frame blank would happen if X-Frame-Options were DENY invariant I5 forbids it Wolverine

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: one human (Ricardo) plus the family as read-only viewers of share links. There is no authentication, no role matrix and no token issuance. Trust is the origin/host guard plus loopback binding.

  • Stored: family birth data, natal placements, derived charts, location assessments, report snapshots in report_shares (SQLite, git-ignored).
  • Anchored outside the system: nothing is signed. Share tokens are crypto.randomBytes(24) base64url; their unguessability rests on 192 bits of entropy, not on any signature.
  • Honesty statement: the app makes no authentication or authorisation claim. Anyone who can reach 127.0.0.1:9111 (or the tailnet origin) can read every page. Share links are unguessable URLs, not capabilities with expiry enforcement beyond the 365-day default and the getShare expiry check. This is a deliberate trade for a single-household, local-first product.

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A — mount and contract (no dependencies). F1, F2, F3, F4, F10, F11. Exit: GET /astro returns 200 with the header set and grep -rn 'fetch("/api' src/ is empty.
  • Stage B — calculation honesty (depends on A). F5, F6, F7, I3, I8. Exit: GET /api/best-location without exploratory=true returns 422; a member with an unresolved input is named in the error; verify-report.ts reports all checks PASS.
  • Stage C — portable outputs (depends on B). F8, F9, F14. Exit: PDF header %PDF-, 9 pages, size > 20 KB; share round-trip and unknown-token 404 both pass.
  • Stage D — feature surface (depends on A). F12, F13, F15-F24. Exit: every listed route returns 200 in a smoke run and npm test reports 540 passed.
  • Stage E — release hygiene (depends on A-D). CI green, Docker build green, README and accuracy register current. Exit: npm run lint 0 errors, npx tsc --noEmit exit 0, npm run build exit 0.

No dates; each stage ends on its boolean exit test.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Should family rankings ever lose the exploratory gate and become "verified"? Keep the gate until every engine in the accuracy register is reference-covered; then flip eligibleForVerifiedRanking to true in one commit Removing it now presents an interpretive model as a verdict; keeping it forever hides real progress
2 Should share links expire after 365 days or never? Keep 365 days with the existing cleanup script Never-expiring links grow the table and leak old snapshots indefinitely; shorter expiry breaks family links on the couch
3 Should the app gain a standalone public origin? No — keep it embedded in the cockpit on loopback A standalone origin adds auth, TLS, monitoring and a second deploy target for no household benefit
4 Should robots/noindex stay, or should the app be indexable? Keep noindex, nofollow Indexing a personal astrology app with family birth data would be a privacy failure
