Signet v1.1 hardening — enhanced proposal

50 features 0 P0 19 implemented 1 partial 56 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

The v1.1 hardening PRD (docs/next/v1.1-hardening-prd.md) is a review of main at 5a3b53b and claims ~76 gap items (H-A..H-O). Re-checked against the tree at 1c304ee (branch h1/h-u8-round-changes): 19 items are fully implemented, 1 is partial (H-A4: resourcekey carry-through done, Drive interstitial P3 not found), and 56 are absent from this tree. The original hardening PRD gave no status ledger and no per-item evidence, so a reader could not tell which gaps were already closed. This version adds: a status ledger with one row per H item and a file:line/commit for each; every still-open item restated as a rule plus a regression test that fails on the old behaviour; and a build order by dependency with boolean exit tests. Nothing here changes the original PRD or the frozen docs/prd/signet-build-prd.html.

Product in five lines

  1. Signet is an approval and proofing platform for Infinity Brands: artwork, documents, video and websites are uploaded as immutable versions, reviewed in rounds inside spaces, and decided with a threshold; every decision is a record that survives a dispute.
  2. Users: designers, reviewers, gatekeepers, project owners, guests/clients, and agent principals.
  3. Decision enabled: which version is approved, by whom, against which threshold, and when — with a verifiable chain.
  4. Deliberately not: a general workflow builder, billing, identity system, or real-time collaborative editing.
  5. Success number: 31 of 32 P0 acceptance criteria pass (C-24 out of v1 scope by owner decision SA-1), 1112 test functions, chain verifies from genesis after restore.

Domain model

Entities (one line each): Principal (human/agent/guest), Brand (security boundary), Membership (principal↔︎brand role), Project, Asset, Version (immutable, sha256), Round (with mandatory due date), Assignment (reviewer + role), Decision (approve/reject/approve-with-changes, signature-bound to version hash), Comment/Thread (with pins), Space (designer/interim/main/client), Template (immutable once used), GuestAuthor (grant-bound), GuestLink, Event (append-only audit), Notification, and the linkfetch staging artefacts.

Tables that carry them (columns that matter): version(sha256, asset_id, superseded_by), round(due_at timestamptz not null, status, threshold), decision(version_hash, signature, etag), comment(author_id nullable, guest_author_id nullable, check exactly one), event(seq, kind, payload), notification(recipient, kind, subject, link, read_at), comment_read(principal_id, comment_id, read_at).

Indexes that decide performance: round (asset_id, status), decision (round_id, version_hash), event (org_id, seq) for chain verification. New read paths (inbox, labels, search, reminders) need their own indexes per H-V8 before they ship.

Invariants:

  • I1. internal/domain imports only the standard library; no DB, HTTP, clock, third party.
  • I2. Handlers never reimplement a rule; rules live in domain first.
  • I3. Nothing is hard-deleted; versions/decisions/comments/events are superseded or archived; event is append-only (no UPDATE, no DELETE).
  • I4. Only a human principal decides; principal.kind = agent may create/plan/upload/comment but never sign/approve/release.
  • I5. A round cannot exist without a due date; a closed round is immutable.
  • I6. Exhaustion stays blocked: all decided with threshold unmet → blocked, never approved/advanced.
  • I7. Template immutability after first use: ErrTemplateInUse, SQLSTATE guards.
  • I8. Idempotency: replay of the same Idempotency-Key returns the first result; a different body under the same key returns 409.
  • I9. Guest scoping: a guest token for brand A returns 404 for brand B's assets; guests never read internal spaces.
  • I10. Zoho adapter is off by default and never changes a review fact (off-path guarantee tested).

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

States of a round: open → waiting → changes_required → blocked → ready_for_release → released, plus superseded (on new version) and closed (decline-and-stop). Transitions are driven by domain.Evaluate (pure, stdlib only): inputs = set of decisions with values + roles + threshold + mandatory flags; output = Waiting | ChangesRequired | Blocked | Released (with AutoReleases when no gatekeeper). Edge cases enumerated: concurrency (two simultaneous approvals → exactly one release, serialised on the round row), empty set (no decisions → waiting), stale version (ErrStaleVersion, zero rows), withdrawal (supersedes winner, replays idempotently), exhaustion (all decided, threshold unmet → blocked, owner notified exactly once), self-approval (refused), duplicate (idempotent replay sets Replayed), mandatory participant outstanding (blocks release), gatekeeper release vs autorelease.

F38 H-O3: admin redelivery of dead webhooks P2 No redelivery Test: dead delivery redelivered once and marked delivered; idempotent per click (Idempotency-Key); agent token refused

F40 H-D1: measure internal/app under default timeout P1 No recorded measurement Recorded run under 600 s closes F4, or measured figure justifies H-D2
F41 H-D2: split internal/app into test packages P2 One package, 529 tests Each package fits 10 min under -race; CI drops -p=1 or 60 m override
F42 H-D3: in-place panel update in detail.js P2 Flaky serial browser test 20 consecutive -count=1 runs at -parallel 8 pass
F43 H-D4: read exceptionDetails.exception.description P2 Chrome exceptions reported as (code 0) Deliberately throwing script reports its message
F44 H-D5: smoke test for deploy/local scripts P2 No test harness Runs in CI without secrets
F45 H-D6: -race in CI full job P2 No race detector in CI CI runs -race on every PR within timeout
F46 H-P1: side-by-side/overlay/swipe compare for image+PDF P1 Only appended pages Browser test: two PDFs differing on page 2 open on page 2; all three modes render, zooming one side zooms other; guest sees only shared versions; screenshots at 1440 and 390 px
F47 H-P2: PixelDiff changed-region rectangles P2 Only ratio returned Table test: identical pair → no regions; one changed block → one rectangle within 1% of truth; anti-aliasing noise under threshold → no regions

Note: F50 is the P3 half of H-A4 (Drive virus-scan interstitial). Open feature count = 50 (F1..F50). H-U11 is covered by F19.

Architecture and API surface

Package layout (AGENTS.md): cmd/signet (single binary), internal/domain (pure, stdlib only), internal/store (postgres), internal/httpapi (routing/JSON/auth), internal/auth (sessions/magic links/policy), internal/media (ingest), migrations (numbered, forward only), web (embedded FE).

Import rule: internal/domain imports only stdlib; handlers never reimplement a rule; if a rule is needed in a handler it belongs in domain first.

Endpoint conventions: all routes under /api/v1/, brand-scoped through middleware (wrong brand or missing membership → 404, never 403), cursor pagination 50/page, idempotency via Idempotency-Key with store.LockRequest receipts, ETag/If-Match on decisions, step-up proof for decisions, no hard-deletes. New endpoints (inbox, search, labels, bulk, activity feed, readyz/metrics, redelivery) must follow these.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Exhausted threshold Round shows blocked, no advance Domain Evaluate + ErrExhausted TestEvaluateExhaustionStaysBlocked, TestPGRoundExhaustionNotifiesOwnerOnce
Stale version decision Client error + replay marker ErrStaleVersion TestPGIdempotentReplayAndStaleVersion

DB down /readyz 503 (to be built, F36) (to be built)

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: human, agent, guest. Roles: owner, editor, approver, gatekeeper, mandatory, observer, guest. Role/action matrix (PRD §8) frozen by internal/auth/prd_action_matrix_test.go (TestPRDDistinctActionCells): withdraw (owner yes, editor no, approver/gatekeeper/mandatory cond, observer no, guest cond), export (owner only), archive (owner+editor), manage_members (owner only); agents never administer or withdraw.

What is stored: versions with sha256, rounds with due dates, decisions bound to version hash with step-up proof and signature, comments with pins, events (append-only, seq-ordered), notifications, comment_read, guest_author. What is anchored outside: daily anchor files (SIGNET_ANCHOR_DELIVERY=file|smtp), evidence pack verifiable by cmd/signet/verify.go without our software. Signature/approval claim honesty: step-up auth required; forged unsigned quorum cannot release; proof minted for one version's bytes refused on another version with identical bytes. Zoho adapter is off by default and off the review path (SA-1).

Build order by dependency

  • Stage A (no deps, parallel): F40 (measure), F43 (exception field), F44 (deploy smoke), F45 (-race CI), F2 (redaction doc), F39 (a11y checks), H-F1 (done).
  • Stage B (depends on A measurement): F41 (split internal/app) if F40 justifies; F42 (in-place panel).
  • Stage C (domain first, test-first): F10 (markup shapes), F11 (edit/reopen), F12 (mentions), F17 (checklist), F47 (PixelDiff regions), F16 (reminders) — each with table-driven domain tests including exhaustion.
  • Stage D (store + routes): F4 (reviews-to-do), F5 (comments grouped), F6 (reply/resolve/jump), F7 (inbox API), F13 (attachments), F14 (export), F15 (reactions), F18 (upload revision), F19 (rename), F20 (labels), F21 (bulk), F22 (search), F23 (activity feed), F8 (subscriptions), F9 (anchor receipts), F36 (readyz/metrics), F37 (backup), F38 (redelivery), F32-F35 (storage), F1 (quota), F24 (compression), F25 (lazy images), F26 (tiles), F27 (parallel upload), F28 (first-page priority), F29 (ETag), F30 (indexes), F31 (perf CI), F46 (compare view), F48 (brand-kit), F49 (model checker), F3 (panel UI), F23.
  • Stage E (integration + verification): browser journeys for F3, F6, F10, F18, F25, F46; evidence packs; role-matrix rows for new routes; CI gates.

Exit tests (boolean): Stage A ends when F40 recorded run exists and F45 CI green. Stage B ends when go test -race ./internal/app fits 10 min. Stage C ends when every new domain function has a table-driven test including the exhaustion case. Stage D ends when every new route has a role-matrix row and a store test. Stage E ends when all F-acceptance tests pass against real Postgres.

Signet — project workspace and review flow — enhanced proposal

0 features 0 P0 2 implemented 0 partial 12 absent 0 decisions

What this version fixes

The workflow PRD (docs/next/workflow-workspace-prd.md, status: proposal, not implemented) defines 12 UXW requirements and 6 delivery slices but gives no status ledger, no per-requirement evidence, and acceptance criteria written as prose outcomes. Re-checked against the tree at 1c304ee: no inbox package, no project explorer, no flow matrix, no grouping/favourites, no activity feed exists; only H-N1 (notification table) and H-I5 (comment read state) are implemented, and neither is wired into a visible inbox surface. This version adds: a status ledger with one row per UXW requirement and evidence of absence; every UXW requirement restated with an acceptance test carrying an expected value; security/permission and performance constraints turned into explicit rows; and a build order by dependency (UXW-S1..S6) with boolean exit tests. The proposal remains a proposal: nothing here claims implementation.

Product in five lines

  1. Signet is an approval and proofing platform for Infinity Brands; this proposal redesigns how a person finds projects, reads their files' review state, and acts — without changing approval semantics.
  2. Users: designers/producers, reviewers, managers/gatekeepers, project owners, clients/guests.
  3. Decision enabled: which file needs attention next, where it sits in its review flow, and what action is permitted — found in one navigation, not reconstructed across pages.
  4. Deliberately not: changing approval rules, a workflow automation builder, replacing proof renderers, arbitrary nested folders, cross-brand moves, billing, a new identity system, real-time collab editing.
  5. Success number: zero unauthorised stage/actor/comment-excerpt/aggregate-count leaks in A10, and p95 project selection to first matrix rows ≤500 ms on the declared fixture.

Status ledger

One row per UXW requirement. Evidence is a file:line, a quoted command, or a commit in the tree at 1c304ee. No UXW requirement is implemented in this tree; H-N1 and H-I5 are partial foundations only.

Domain model

Organization model (brand → optional project group → project → optional file section → asset → immutable version). Brand is the security boundary; groups and sections are organizational metadata, not permissions or review spaces. Groups one level deep only; no cross-brand moves; ungrouped projects and files remain first-class; every file section uses the project's review flow (cannot override thresholds or spaces).

Stage states (presentation labels over plan levels; "Not started" is a presentation state, not a persisted round): Preparing file, Not started, In review, Changes required, Blocked, Ready for release, Released, Returned/awaiting confirmation, Historical/superseded. Movement rules: threshold satisfaction is not release when a gatekeeper exists; without one use existing AutoReleases; exhaustion stays blocked; new version visibly distinguishes past released rounds; realtime/stale responses cannot roll the UI backwards or overwrite a newer version/round.

Tables: reuse existing project, asset, version, round, assignment, decision, comment (threading is the comment.thread_id column at migrations/0001_init.sql:168, not a thread table — grep -rniE "create table[a-z ]*thread" migrations/ → 0 matches), notification, comment_read. New (forward-only migrations, if accepted): project_group (optional, one level), file_section (optional), principal_ui_pref (favorites, per principal, server-side) — UI preferences are not approval evidence and must not enter the event chain.

Indexes that decide performance: workflow read projection must be bounded (≤50 asset rows per page, cursor pagination); no per-cell query; one bounded batch fetch of stage summaries per asset page.

Invariants:

  • W-I1. Brand remains the security boundary; groups/sections never create permissions.
  • W-I2. Every workflow read checks read permission per row and stage, not only the project header.
  • W-I3. No fabricated round: "Not started" is presentation only.
  • W-I4. Exhaustion stays blocked in the matrix (no false green).
  • W-I5. A new version does not inherit a prior version's approval in the matrix.
  • W-I6. Realtime/stale responses cannot overwrite a newer version/round; revalidate on focus and before mutations; show conflict and refresh.
  • W-I7. Old deep links, bookmarks and browser history continue to resolve.
  • W-I8. Unauthenticated human navigation leads to sign-in with validated same-origin return target; API calls keep JSON 401; no open redirect.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

This proposal adds no approval engine. The workflow read projection is a pure read model over existing entities: inputs = (principal, project, cursor, filters); outputs = ordered asset rows, each with applicable plan revision, ordered visible stages, round identity, display outcome, due info, progress, permitted actions. Authorization applied per row/stage. Conventions: 50 rows/page, cursor pagination, bounded stage-summary batch, no unbounded history materialisation, revalidate on focus.

Feature table

IDs keep the UXW- prefix. Every row carries an acceptance test with an expected value.

Architecture and API surface

Keep one Go binary, embedded frontend, Postgres queue. No new frontend framework, ORM or queue service. Domain evaluation remains pure and stdlib-only. Handlers and JS consume authoritative state and allowed actions; neither reimplements thresholds, due arithmetic or release rules.

Add a bounded project-workflow read projection (if needed): asset/version identity, applicable plan revision, ordered visible stages, round identity, display outcome, due info, progress, permitted actions. Route/schema decided in the implementation slice with a contract test. Read permissions checked for every row and stage. Avoid one request/query per cell; paginate assets and fetch stage summaries in bounded batches; never materialise unbounded project history. Grouping/favourites use forward-only migrations and existing archive conventions; no update/delete of audit events; UI preferences are not approval evidence. Existing mutation endpoints retain idempotency, authorization, transaction locks, audit generation. UI visibility is not authorization. Old deep links resolve.

Performance budgets (targets, measured not claimed): first page ≤50 asset rows; cursor-load older pages; only visible thumbnails load. p95 project selection to usable first matrix rows ≤500 ms (30 warm navigations, local HTTPS). Workflow read API p95 ≤200 ms on declared fixture (100 projects, 500 files, 6 stages) in 100 requests; query/request counts do not grow per stage cell. Interaction feedback ≤100 ms; network outcomes show pending state, not optimistic approval. Compressed JS/CSS budget ≤100 KB excluding lazily loaded proof tools. Polling pauses in hidden tabs; failed refreshes do not clear data or unbounded-retry.

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it

Revoked membership mid-use Canonical denial, no name leak Server-side scoping on every row A10 (docs/next/workflow-workspace-prd.md:161)

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals and role/action matrix as in the hardening doc (owner/editor/approver/gatekeeper/mandatory/ observer/guest; agents never decide/approve/release/sign). Guest access never grants project explorer or internal-stage metadata merely because the shell is shared. Project groups and file sections are organizational metadata, not permissions. Activity feed is a projection of durable records, not a second audit log; guests see only allowed client activity. UI preferences (favorites, expansion, scroll) are not approval evidence and do not enter the event chain. Read permissions checked per row and stage, not merely the project header. Unauthenticated human navigation → sign-in with validated same-origin return; API calls keep JSON 401.

Build order by dependency

  • UXW-S1: Shell, navigation/session return, explorer and favorites (UXW-01/09/10; approved desktop+mobile frames; no permission regressions).
  • UXW-S2: Server workflow projection and matrix (UXW-02/03/08; plan/round contracts audited; A2-A4, A10).
  • UXW-S3: In-context proof, exact links and upload (UXW-04/06/07; S2; A6/A7 and Back/scroll evidence).
  • UXW-S4: Visible inbox and notification experience (UXW-05; H-I1-H-I6, H-N1, relevant H-N2 and H-C2 dependencies; A5).
  • UXW-S5: Cross-surface visual consistency, responsive and performance closure (all P0 slices; A1-A10, measured budgets, regression suite).
  • UXW-S6: Optional groups, file sections and project activity (UXW-11/12; only after P0 release; archive/history tests).

Stage behind a reversible configuration switch while incomplete; preserve old route compatibility and existing data. Complete P0 before presenting the redesigned workspace as delivered. P1 can follow without blocking a usable project-and-review flow. Each slice is a small reviewable PR linked to its acceptance IDs with implementation-status updates and runnable evidence.

Exit tests (boolean): S1 ends when explorer + favorites + session return pass A1/A7/A8 with no permission regressions. S2 ends when matrix renders server-supplied stages and A2-A4/A10 pass. S3 ends when proof-in-context + upload recovery pass A6/A7 with Back/scroll evidence. S4 ends when inbox passes A5. S5 ends when A1-A10 + measured budgets pass. S6 ends when groups/activity pass archive and history tests.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Should the redesigned workspace ship behind a flag while incomplete? Yes — reversible config switch, old routes preserved (§11) No flag risks a half-finished workspace replacing the working one
2 Should project groups be one level deep in v1? Yes — one level; arbitrary nesting excluded (§4) Deeper nesting needs migration and permission rework
3 Should the matrix read projection be a new endpoint or extend existing? Inspect existing project/plan/round/permission APIs first; add bounded projection only if needed, with contract test (§9) New endpoint adds maintenance; reusing may overload existing routes
4 Should UI preferences (favorites) be stored server-side? Yes, per principal on server; expansion/scroll per session with storage-blocked fallback Client-only loses favorites across devices and breaks revoked-project removal
5 Should the old sidebar navigation be removed? No — keep old deep links resolving; new shell added alongside (§9, W-I7) Removing breaks bookmarks and browser history

1. What this version fixes

The source enhancement PRD (548 lines, PROPOSED, nothing shipped) is a code-grounded audit and is accurate on the numbers it quotes (E1–E74). What was thin: it stopped at diagnosis and workstreams — it gave no single governing reconciliation line between itself and docs/PRD_v2.md, no numbered invariants, no persisted BeachPulseScore shape, no per-signal freshness arithmetic, and no explicit "absent until proven" discipline on the three claims it made softly. This version fixes:

  1. Soft claim restated as rule + failing test: source §6 row "§20 non-goals respected, with one exception: Spotter verified rendered whenever has_spotter is true" (src/App.tsx:422, which reads beach.provenance?.spotterVerified — not a has_spotter column) is now invariant I12 with the test src/pulse/spotterClaim.test.tsx expecting the string "· Spotter verified" to be absent unless spotterRightsVerified === true (currently fails on HEAD).
  2. Unverifiable claim named: source §2 says "0 Playwright/e2e files" (E5); this version keeps it as absent and adds the control row F23 with expected value 0 e2e files on HEAD (verified by find . -path ./node_modules -prune -o -name '*.spec.*' -print | wc -l → 0). No soft qualifiers anywhere.
  3. Thin section deepened: source §7 workstreams had acceptance criteria as commands but no dependency-stage exit tests; §11 here gives boolean exit tests per stage.
  4. Staleness correction: LANE-REPORT.md (base a41a28e) claims v2 §7.5 crowd-from-events is "Missing"; server/dayQuality.ts implements it (E57). This version marks it implemented, not missing (E63).
  5. Count reconciliation: 41 tables, 72 route registrations / 66 unique paths, 30 test files / 545 it() blocks, 154 TS/TSX files, 40,068 lines — all re-derived by command in the evidence appendix; source numbers match.

Reconciliation (one line, citing both): where the enhancement PRD and docs/PRD_v2.md overlap, the enhancement PRD governs shipped-state claims because it is the only document diffed against the code on disk at ef4be99 (its §6 pillar diff and §3 evidence table, E1–E74); docs/PRD_v2.md governs scope intent beyond the shipped product because it is the product-vision document (§2 pillars, §19 open decisions) that predates the code audit — state is separated from intent, so the two do not contradict once each is read in its own lane.

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a beach-intelligence PWA (React 19 + Express 5 + Postgres 16) shipping condition, discovery, booking and engagement surfaces, today running on 41 tables and 66 API paths.
  • Who uses it: beachgoers (family/friends/solo/couples/party/chill/beach-club), merchants, coordinators, institutional buyers.
  • Decision/action it enables: "which beach, right now, within a drive, with these conditions and amenities — and can I book before I leave" (PRD.md §1).
  • Deliberately not: a social feed or person-following graph (PRD_v2 §20); surf forecasting beyond wave height/direction; hotel/rental booking.
  • The single number that says it works: every condition signal carries {observedAt, state ∈ {fresh,ageing,stale,expired}, source, ttlMinutes} and no expired signal renders a number anywhere (WS-2 AC #2).

4. Domain model

Entities (one-line purpose each):

  • Beach — a physical beach and its static profile (name, slug, lat/lon, capacity_band, coverage flags).
  • BeachCondition — one ingested reading per beach per provider fetch (observed_at, received_at, provider_fetched_at, signal_ttl).
  • BeachCoverage — reviewable per-beach signal availability (has_weather/marine/tide/water_quality, source_kind).
  • ProviderHealth — per-provider state/latency/failure counters.
  • User / AppUser — account.
  • Session — opaque 32-byte token, SHA-256 stored, 30-day sliding (NEW, WS-1).
  • UserRole — user/merchant/coordinator/admin/ops (NEW, WS-1).
  • BeachSighting — beach-scoped UGC, native (7d TTL) or social link, moderation state.
  • BeachEvent — host beach(es), type, discipline, window, audience, coordinator owner.
  • BeachPulseScore — persisted score per (beach, audience, scope, window) with component breakdown (NEW).
  • Booking / BookingCancellation — reservation against date-scoped inventory.
  • AmenityInventory — per (merchant, type) counter today; becomes per (merchant, type, date) (WS-9).
  • AdPlacement / SponsorCampaign — paid, labeled, never enters organic lane.
  • BeachFollow — user↔︎beach subscription (NEW, WS-12).
  • AuditLog — privileged actions with session_id + actor_role (WS-1).

Tables that carry them (columns that matter): beach (id, slug, lat, lon, capacity_band), beach_condition (beach_id, observed_at, received_at, provider_fetched_at, signal_ttl jsonb, source), beach_sighting (id, beach_id text, audience, time_of_day, moderation_state, captured_at, expires_at, author_user_id nullable), booking (id, user_id, merchant_id, amenity_type, starts_at, ends_at, quantity, total_cents, currency, status, stripe_payment_intent_id), amenity_inventory (merchant_id, amenity_type, date date, total_count, available_count), beach_pulse_score (beach_id, audience, scope, window, score numeric, components jsonb, computed_at).

Indexes that decide performance: (1) beach_condition (beach_id, observed_at desc) for latest-per-beach; (2) beach_pulse_score (scope, audience, window, score desc) for leaderboard reads; (3) booking (merchant_id, amenity_type, starts_at) for availability.

Numbered invariants:

  • I1 A condition signal in expired state renders no numeric value in any surface (list card, detail strip, pulse input, day plan, itinerary).
  • I2 Paid placement (ad_placement, sponsor, takeover) never appears in the organic BeachPulseScore computation or listBeaches body — enforced by source-shape test server/ads.test.ts:447-460.
  • I3 BeachPulseScore is computed server-side and persisted; the client only reads it.
  • I4 A booking is confirmed only after a Stripe payment-intent succeeded webhook is processed.
  • I5 amenity_inventory.available_count is scoped to (merchant_id, amenity_type, date); two bookings on different dates never collide.
  • I6 Every user-facing age is computed from observedAt, never receivedAt.
  • I7 Native sightings expire at captured_at + 7 days; expires_at is non-null for every row.
  • I8 A coordinator cannot write conditions, water quality, hazards, or pulse — no such route exists (shipped by absence, verified by route table).
  • I9 Refresh endpoints require requireRole("admin","ops").
  • I10 beach_coverage.has_marine = false ⇒ seaTempC: null + availability: "no_station" (never a blank slot).
  • I11 < 3 crowd signals in 24 h ⇒ value: null, method: "insufficient_signal" (never interpolate from nothing).
  • I12 "· Spotter verified" renders only when spotterRightsVerified === true (currently violated on HEAD — test fails).
  • I13 All copy is English; no other language anywhere in the document or UI strings (standard §0.1).

5. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

5.1 Freshness state machine

States: fresh (age ≤ ttl), ageing (≤ 3× ttl), stale (≤ 24 h), expired (> 24 h), unknown (null inputs).

Transition function (pure, clock-injected): classifySignal({observedAt, receivedAt, now, ttlMinutes}) → {state, ageMinutes, ageBasis: "observed", ttlMinutes}.

  • ageMinutes = max(0, (now - observedAt) in minutes) (clock skew clamped to 0 → fresh).
  • null observedAt ⇒ unknown, never fresh.
  • Boundary: exactly 24 h ⇒ stale (not expired); 24 h 01 m ⇒ expired.

Per-signal TTLs: airTempC/windKmh/cloudPct/uvIndex 90 min; seaTempC/waveM 3 h; tide 6 h; waterQuality 24 h; goldenHour 24 h (labelled derived).

Edge cases: negative age (clamp 0), null inputs (unknown), provider down (state derived from health table, not asserted), stale seed data (stamps observed_at in the past ⇒ never fresh on seed).

5.2 Crowd estimator (pure)

Inputs: check-ins in last 3 h (count, recency-weighted), latest sighting age, active published non-paid event windows (reuse dayQuality bump), hour-of-day curve, beach.capacity_band. Output {value: number|null, confidence, method, sampleSize}.

  • < 3 signals in 24 h ⇒ value: null, method: "insufficient_signal".
  • Paid takeover events do not raise the estimate (mirrors dayQuality).
  • Monotonicity: more check-ins never lowers the estimate.

5.3 Beach Pulse score (server-side, persisted)

BeachPulseScore(beach, audience, scope, window) = weighted blend of:

  • live conditions (crowd, water quality, wind, wave, tide, temp, cloud, UV, golden-hour light) with per-audience weights (§6.2 of PRD_v2: family/friends/solo/couples/party/chill/beach-club),
  • community signals (check-ins, vibe votes, accuracy ratings, sighting recency+volume),
  • freshness (recency of like-minded visitors; decay even if conditions perfect).

Windows: live (now), today, week, season. Scopes: radius (default hero), island, cluster, region. Sponsored never enters (I2). Persisted to beach_pulse_score; recomputed on condition refresh.

7. Architecture and API surface

Package layout: modular monolith — server/ (Express, routes, providers, jobs), src/ (React PWA), server/migrations/ (idempotent SQL), server/scripts/ (migrate/seed/refresh). One import rule: src/ never imports from server/ and vice versa; shared types live in neither (duplicated contracts verified by tests).

Endpoint table (selected, who may call):

Endpoint Caller Gate
GET /api/beaches any public, rate-limited
POST /api/conditions/refresh admin/ops requireRole("admin","ops")
POST /api/conditions/ingest admin/ops requireRole("admin","ops")
POST /api/beach-events* coordinator/admin requireRole("coordinator","admin")
POST /api/ads* admin requireRole("admin")
GET /api/merchant/* merchant/admin requireRole("merchant","admin")
GET /api/institution/* institution member requireInstitutionMember
POST /api/bookings user (authed) requireUser + future date + price parity
POST /api/me/follows user requireUser
POST /api/sightings/:id/report any authed requireUser

Conventions: pagination via limit/offset with default 50; idempotency keys on booking + checkout; versioning via /api/v1 deferred (currently unversioned, noted as debt); all provider data includes observed/received time, source, freshness state.

8. Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks
Provider down banner "provider down"; no fabricated number provider_health.state derived; health stays 200 uptime probe on /api/health
Stale reading (>24h) value suppressed, "No current reading" classifySignal ⇒ expired ⇒ suppress (I1) contract test
Double booking 409 conflict date-scoped inventory + unique constraint integration test
Payment never confirms booking stays pending, auto-cancel after 15 min webhook absence ⇒ no confirmed job + test
Unsafe sighting hidden, report count surfaced report path + moderation queue coordinator/admin
Clock skew age clamped 0, state fresh max(0, age) unit test
Empty crowd signal "No recent reports" < 3 ⇒ null (I11) unit test

A row with no control is a risk accepted: premium self-service boolean (F-premium) has no payment gate today — accepted until WS-9 ships; documented here, not left blank.

9. Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: anonymous, user, merchant, coordinator, admin, ops, institution member.

Role/action matrix: see F1/F2 and the endpoint table above. Institution data stays behind requireInstitutionMember.

What is stored: session token hash (SHA-256, never raw), user profile, bookings, sightings, pulse scores, ad placements, audit log. Media is transient (7-day TTL, S3 lifecycle).

Anchored outside the system: Stripe payment intents (money truth), signed QR tokens (server/tokens.ts), provider raw payloads (raw.providerFetchedAt).

Honest signature/approval strength: the current "Spotter verified" badge is a DB boolean with no data-rights verification (contradicts PRD.md §17) — treated as unverified until real rights exist (I12). No MFA, no password storage in scope (WS-1 out of scope).

10. Build order by dependency

  • Stage A (truth): WS-1 (identity) → WS-2 (freshness) → WS-3 (provider health). Exit: F1, F2, F3, F4, F5 all pass; npm run typecheck green.
  • Stage B (money correctness): WS-9 (booking) depends on Stage A. Exit: F8, F9, F10, F11, F12 pass; no confirmed without webhook.
  • Stage C (moderation + truth of crowd): WS-4, WS-6 (pulse), WS-10 (moderation) depend on Stage A. Exit: F7, F13, F14, F15, F17 pass.
  • Stage D (engagement surface): WS-11 (events), WS-12 (coordinator + follows) depend on Stage C. Exit: F20, F22, F30 pass.
  • Stage E (ops + perf): WS-14, WS-15, WS-16, WS-17 depend on nothing new. Exit: F23, F24, F25, F27, F28 pass.
  • Stage F (monetization): WS-7 (ads/takeovers) depends on Stage D. Exit: promoted lane live, labeled, separate.

Zero dates. Each stage ends on the named boolean test.

SunScout Product Requirements Document — v2 (Full Scope, enhanced)

17 features 0 P0 0 implemented 5 partial 12 absent 5 decisions

SunScout Product Requirements Document — v2 (Full Scope, enhanced)

1. What this version fixes

docs/PRD_v2.md (Draft v2, 420 lines) is the product-vision document: 8 pillars, 5 open decisions, a build sequence. It made scope claims with no code evidence and predated the code audit. What was thin: no reconciliation with the shipped product, no status against main, no acceptance tests with expected values, and it left 5 decisions open without recommendations. This version fixes:

  1. Unverifiable claim named: v2 §14 lists BeachPulseScore, SponsorCampaign/Takeover, Coordinator, Companion, SocialLinkAttribution as "new entities" — none exist as tables on main (verified: grep -rhoE "create table if not exists [a-z_]+" server/migrations/*.sql | sort -u → 41 tables, none of those five). Treated as absent, not "planned".
  2. Stale claim corrected: v2 §7.5 "events as crowd-forecast input" was marked "Missing" by LANE-REPORT.md; server/dayQuality.ts implements eventBoost/EVENT_CROWD_BUMP_MAX and excludes paid takeovers (E57). State here: implemented.
  3. Thin section deepened: v2 §16 build sequence (7 steps, no exit tests) is replaced by §11 here with boolean exit tests per stage, reusing F-IDs from the companion enhanced doc.
  4. Open decisions closed with recommendations: all five v2 §19 decisions now carry a recommended answer in §12 (D1–D5), none left undecided.
  5. Scope separated from state: this document extends docs/PRD-sunscout-enhancement-2026-10-30-v2-enhanced.md and does not contradict it; where the two overlap on shipped-state, that document governs.

Reconciliation (one line, citing both): where the enhancement PRD and docs/PRD_v2.md overlap, the enhancement PRD governs shipped-state claims because it is the only document diffed against the code on disk at ef4be99 (its §6 pillar diff and §3 evidence table, E1–E74); docs/PRD_v2.md governs scope intent beyond the shipped product because it is the product-vision document (§2 pillars, §19 open decisions) that predates the code audit — state is separated from intent, so the two do not contradict once each is read in its own lane.

2. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a global, beach-centric beach-intelligence platform spanning conditions, discovery, booking, engagement (sightings, events, follows), dynamic rankings (Beach Pulse), and brand sponsorship — all beach-scoped, never person-following.
  • Who uses it: beachgoers, merchants, event coordinators, brand advertisers, institutional buyers.
  • Decision/action it enables: "the right beach for me, right now, within a practical drive, with conditions and amenities I need — and what is actually happening there — and can I book before I leave" (v2 §1).
  • Deliberately not: a social feed or person-following graph (v2 §20); surf forecasting beyond wave height/direction; hotel/rental booking; restaurant reservations.
  • The single number that says it works: BeachPulseScore persisted per (beach, audience, scope, window) and recomputed on condition refresh, with sponsored content excluded from the organic lane by source-shape test.

4. Domain model

Extends the domain model in the companion enhanced doc §4. v2 adds these entities (all absent on main today, verified against 41 tables):

  • Sighting — beachId, eventId?, mediaRef | externalUrl, caption, timeOfDayTag, audienceTag, capturedAt, expiresAt, moderationState, consentFlags, authorRef?.
  • Event — beachIds[], type, discipline?, windowStart, windowEnd, audience, registrationUrl?, coordinatorId, sponsorTakeoverId?, liveChannelId?.
  • BeachPulseScore — beachId, audience, scope, window, componentScores{}, computedAt. (NEW table beach_pulse_score.)
  • VibeVote — tag, votes, userVoted (existing type retained).
  • AccuracyRating — beachId, feature, rating, userId, at.
  • CheckIn — beachId, userId, at, privacy (existing).
  • SponsorCampaign / Takeover — sponsorId, scope (beach/island/region), surface, window, creativeRef, additive|replacing. (NEW)
  • Coordinator — userId, organization, managedEvents[]. (NEW role + table)
  • Companion — profileId, name, relationship (family/friend/solo). (NEW)
  • SocialLinkAttribution — sightingId, platform (tiktok/instagram), externalUrl, creatorHandle. (NEW)
  • BeachFollow — userId, beachId, createdAt. (NEW, F22)

Columns that matter for the new tables: beach_pulse_score(beach_id, audience, scope, window, score numeric, components jsonb, computed_at); sponsor_campaign(sponsor_id, scope, surface, window_start, window_end, creative_ref, mode text check (mode in ('additive','replacing'))). PostgreSQL remains system of record; object storage (S3-compatible) for transient media with TTL lifecycle enforcing 7-day expiry.

Indexes: beach_pulse_score(scope, audience, window, score desc); sponsor_campaign(scope, window_start, window_end); beach_follow(user_id, beach_id) unique.

Invariants (v2 additions):

  • I14 Beach Pulse is never influenced by paid placement (I2 restated, v2 §8.3).
  • I15 An event's crowd-forecast influence is system-applied and truth-based (§7.5); a coordinator cannot use it as a marketing lever.
  • I16 Native media is never served past 7 days (§5.2, §18).
  • I17 Earned vs paid: paid owns visual/branding/curated-content layer only; live conditions and Pulse are never for sale (§8.3).

5. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Extends companion doc §5. v2's engine is Beach Pulse (§6 of v2) plus the engagement loop.

Beach Pulse state machine:

  • States per (beach, audience, scope, window): computed | stale (older than refresh cadence) | missing.
  • Transition: on condition refresh ⇒ recompute all (beach × 7 audiences × 4 scopes × 4 windows) scores for affected beaches; persist; mark computed.
  • Pure core function: computePulse(beach, audience, scope, window, inputs) → {score, components} where inputs = live conditions + community signals + freshness, with per-audience weights (v2 §6.2: family prioritises kid-safe water quality/lifeguard/shade/parking/accessibility/low hazard/calm water; friends: social heat/club availability/activity/crowd; solo: calm water/low crowd/safety/hidden-gem; couples: golden-hour light/scenery/low crowd/romantic vibe; party/clubs: club check-in heat/music/bookability/crowd/nightlife; chill: calm water/low crowd/golden-hour/low noise; beach-club: club inventory/booking availability/check-in heat).
  • Edge cases: empty community signal (freshness decays to 0, score still computed from conditions); sponsored beach (excluded from organic lane — I14); stale condition (component marked expired, contributes 0 with flag); self-approval (no single user can approve their own sighting into Pulse — moderation gate).

Engagement loop arithmetic: sighting published ⇒ recency boost to that beach's Pulse for the matching audience for 24 h decaying; check-in ⇒ crowd estimate input; event weekend ⇒ crowd-forecast bump (capped by EVENT_CROWD_BUMP_MAX, paid takeovers excluded).

7. Architecture and API surface

ClickUp: the build edition (under the register)

What this version fixes

  • The build edition is the later document and governs configuration counts (12 automations, 16 fields, 5 views, 14 tests). Where the requirements baseline or the deep build state a larger number, the larger number is the pre-cut design and is not the build. Cited: build edition lines 80–81 and 640–650 (2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:80-81, 2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:640-650).
  • The requirements baseline's R11 ("documents that capture a…") is carried forward as an open item, not filled. This document does not invent scope for it.
  • The deep build's 26-rule automation manifest and 28-field register are superseded by the 12-rule / 16-field build set. Any deep-build reference to a dropped rule or field must be marked superseded (see clickup-implementation-v2.md).
  • No workspace exists. Every item below is a specification, not a status report.
  • The -2.html build-edition duplicate was diffed: 711 lines against 786; content delta is formatting and a summary callout only. The longer file is the record.

Product in five lines

  1. What it is: the executable build of the register — one Workspace, eleven Spaces, ten Teams, a seven-file configuration pack, a migration route off Zoho, and a thirty-day pilot with a kill criterion.
  2. Who uses it: the proof cohort (Finance daily control, Technology service desk) plus the Visibility cohort (Leadership) in wave one; everyone else after.
  3. Decision/action it enables: a build that can be run by a person or an agent without another meeting, with two blocking decisions left to Ricardo (plan tier, rollup mechanism) and three non-blocking owner questions.
  4. Deliberately not: a second system run forever; the kill criterion stops funding if the proof cohort does not move.
  5. The number that says it works: tasks created in ClickUp as a share of new work in the cohort's function reaches a majority by day 30.

Same as the register: ClickUp REST API v2, resolve-or-create by name, pagination, least-privilege service account. Security, permissions and the audit record

Same principals and matrix as the register. Build-specific: the service account used by the setup runner holds only the scopes needed for resolve-or-create; no delete scope; migration runs read-only against Zoho exports. Legal/HR Cliq export must be signed off before Cliq goes read-only.

Build order by dependency

Stage A — Resolve and decide (no writes):

  • Record answers to D1–D5 (decisions register) plus the two Ricardo-only decisions (plan tier, rollup mechanism).
  • Exit: every open decision has a recorded answer or explicit deferral; no [open] remains in the pack.

Stage B — Provision the skeleton (parallel, no data):

  • Create the one Workspace, ten Teams, eleven Spaces, Folders, Lists, sixteen fields, five views per Space.
  • Exit: F1–F7, F19 pass against the live workspace.

Stage C — Content and behaviour (parallel):

  • Space 10 goals (6-goal skeleton), Space 11 meetings + AI Notetaker, four dashboards, seven templates, twelve automations.
  • Exit: F8–F12, F17–F18, F25–F26 pass.

Stage D — Migration (sequential, data-bearing):

  • Export Zoho Projects to Excel; run spreadsheet importer into existing Lists; dual run one full cycle.
  • Exit: F15, F16, F21, F22, F29, F30 pass; import report reviewed.

Stage E — Pilot and gate:

  • Proof + Visibility cohorts, thirty days, kill criterion.
  • Exit: F23 passes (majority of new work in ClickUp by day 30) or stop and keep Zoho.

Stage F — Wave two (only if Stage E passes):

  • Chat pilot in two groups; Cliq goes read-only after export sign-off.
  • Exit: F12, F28 pass.

Infinity Brands — ClickUp implementation (deep build)

15 features 8 P0 0 implemented 11 partial 0 absent 5 decisions

ClickUp: the deep build (entity model, schema, automations, migration)

What this version fixes

  • The deep build specifies 28 custom fields and 26 automation rules. The build edition (clickup-build-v3.md) specifies 16 fields and 12 automations, with an explicit cut list. The build edition governs the build set. This document keeps the deep build's extra fields and rules as conditional scope, each tagged conditional or superseded, and names the conflict rather than averaging it. Cited: build edition lines 80–81 and 640–650 (2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:80-81, 2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:640-650); deep build automations at 2026-11-01-infinity-brands-clickup-implementation-prd-v2-0-deep-build.html:8480.
  • The deep build's field register (28 fields) is adopted as the implementation detail behind the build edition's 16-field core; finance, approval and agent fields (F_OBLIGATION_TYPE … F_HUMAN_APPROVED) are conditional on their feature being approved, not part of the core 16.
  • The earlier implementation pass (2026-10-29-infinity-brands-clickup-implementation-prd.html, 2143 lines) is the v1.0 baseline; this document supersedes it. Differences are not re-litigated here.
  • No workspace exists. Everything below is specification.
  • The earlier 2,143-line implementation pass is superseded by this document; the -2 duplicates were diffed and their content delta is formatting and summary callouts only, not requirements.

Product in five lines

  1. What it is: the deep build — entity model, field-by-field schema, 26-rule automation manifest (12 in the build set), routing table, store-opening stage model, finance completeness algorithm, and migration order.
  2. Who uses it: the builder and the QA agent; Ricardo approves the open decisions.
  3. Decision/action it enables: a builder can implement without guessing field types, statuses, routing, or automation triggers.
  4. Deliberately not: a replacement for the register or the build edition; it conforms to them and flags conflicts.
  5. The number that says it works: the Store Completeness Index and the finance completeness algorithm run against the 16-field (plus conditional) schema without a missing key.

Domain model

Entities with one-line purpose (deep build §23):

  • Workspace: single tenant; billing unit.
  • Space: accountable function (11).
  • Folder: brand / client / store / year / quarter grouping.
  • List: work container; carries tasks and views.
  • Task: one piece of work, one home Space.
  • Field: 16 core + up to 12 conditional; see schema below.
  • Status: per-workflow contract; see transition matrix.
  • Automation: 12 build-set rules (+14 conditional).
  • Goal / Task Target: OKR cascade.
  • Doc: meeting minutes, decisions, SOPs, SLA reports.
  • Dashboard: 4, one card per question.
  • Team: 10 on Business.
  • Template: 7 document templates.

Tables that carry them (columns that matter):

  • spaces.csv: id, name, prefix, accountable, shape.
  • fields.csv: id, display name, type, applies-to, required, options, edit rights, consumed by.
  • views.csv: name, filter, sort, scope (5 standard).
  • automations.csv: id, trigger, condition, action.
  • goals.csv: level, owner, check-in.
  • tests.csv: id, area, expected value.
  • migration.csv: source, what moves, what does not, owner.

Indexes that decide performance: (1) task by assignee+status; (2) task by Brand+Work Type+Quarter; (3) Goal/Task Target by owner.

Numbered invariants (carried from the register, I1–I8) plus implementation invariants:

  • I9: Status transitions follow the per-workflow contract; illegal transitions are blocked and logged.
  • I10: Finance completeness requires the full key set (obligation type, amount, period key, paid date, expiry, completeness flag); a missing key fails the algorithm, it does not skip.
  • I11: Store-opening work advances only when the stage checklist register is satisfied.
  • I12: Agent-raised tasks land in Intake Drafts (sandbox) and cannot reach a real List without a human approval gate.

Field-by-field schema (core 16)

The build edition's 16-field core, in creation order:

ID Display name Type Applies to Required Notes

F_BU Business unit Dropdown All tasks Yes BU1–BU6, BO1, BO2, FXS; set on intake
F_FUNCTION Function Dropdown All tasks Yes 143-type taxonomy, versioned
F_QUARTER Quarter Text/Dropdown All tasks Yes Naming rule: quarter lives in Folder + name
F_WORK_TYPE Work-Type Dropdown All tasks Yes Briefed-In, Daily, Recurring, Reporting, Projects/Ad-hoc, Meeting Action
F_PRIORITY Priority Level Dropdown All tasks Yes High, Medium, Urgent
F_FREQUENCY Frequency Dropdown All tasks Yes Once Off, Monthly, Weekly
F_CLIENT_BRAND Client / Brand Dropdown All tasks Yes 14 brands
F_ENGAGEMENT_TIER Engagement Tier Dropdown All tasks Yes HOF, ZOR, COR, ZEE
F_SERVICE_TIER Service Tier Dropdown Service tasks Yes Gold, Black, Silver
F_DUE Due date/time Date All tasks Yes ISO YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM
F_OWNER Owner / functional owner User All tasks Yes Workspace user
F_REQUESTED_BY Requested by Text/User All tasks Yes Audit
F_RESPONSE_DUE Response Due Date Service tasks Yes First response clock
F_RESOLUTION_DUE Resolution Due Date Service tasks Yes Resolution clock
F_FIRST_RESPONSE_AT First Response At Date Service tasks No Set by R-08-04

Conflict named: the deep build ships all 28 as core; the build edition ships 16. Adopting 28 without the finance/approval/agent features being approved inflates the schema and the setup cost. Resolution is D6.

Automations (build set = 12, deep build = 26)

The two documents do not share a rule register. The build edition ships automations.csv with 12 rules A01–A12; the deep build ships 26 rules R-08-01–R-08-26. Only seven deep-build rules have a direct conceptual counterpart in the build set. The build edition is later and governs; the deep build's rules are superseded unless their feature is separately approved.

Build set (verified: 12 data rows in AI-Drive/Agent-Output/batman/clickup-prd-v3-1/config/automations.csv):

Rule Trigger Condition Action Deep-build counterpart
A01 Task created Recurring, Frequency empty Comment asking for Frequency none
A02 Task created Service Tier set Set Response Due R-08-03
A03 Status becomes Delivered First Response At empty Stamp First Response At R-08-04
A04 Date reached Recurring task due today Create next occurrence none (deep build §28.2 rule)
A05 Form submitted Any Create task in 00 Requests, Source=Form R-08-01, R-08-02
A06 Chat message flagged Any Create task with message linked none
A07 Task overdue two days Priority High or Urgent Notify owner and lead R-08-05, R-08-08
A08 Goal pace below plan Progress behind quarter Post to team Channel none
A09 Meeting Doc created Any Create action tasks from action lines none
A10 Bulk change or import Any Write a line to the Configuration Register none
A11 Guest added to a Folder Any Notify account owner none
A12 Weekly, Monday 06:00 — Post team agenda to Channel R-08-23 (closest)

Deep-build rules not in the build set: R-08-06, R-08-07, R-08-09 to R-08-22, R-08-24 to R-08-26 — 19 rules, superseded unless their feature is approved (finance completeness, creative approval, agent contract, store openings, expiry tracking, budget monitor). Adopting them without the feature recreates the 26-rule sprawl the build edition cut.

Conflict named: a rule ID from the deep build cannot be imported into the build set. automations.csv is the only rule register for the build; the deep build's R-IDs are reference material, not deployable rules.

The engine — status machine and finance arithmetic

States (per workflow, deep build §25): Intake → Accepted → In Design → In Review → Approved → Production → Published for creative; Intake → Accepted → In Progress → Waiting → Resolved → Verified → Closed for service; Planned → In Progress → Evidence Due → Verified → Closed for finance controls. Cancelled is terminal from any non-terminal state.

Transitions that must be blocked: a task cannot move to Approved without an approver and a current asset version; cannot move to Verified with Verification Result = Pending; cannot move to Published without an approval; cannot move to Closed with an open dependency. Illegal transitions are refused and logged (I9).

Pure function at the core — finance completeness:

finance_completeness(period_key, obligations, evidence) -> {expected, actual, missing[], duplicates[], late[], score}

Inputs: period_key (YYYY-MM), the obligation register rows active for that key, and evidence rows (task + document URL + paid date). Outputs: counts and a score in [0,1]. The function is pure: same inputs, same score; no clock, no network.

Edge cases, each with an expected value:

  • Empty set: expected = 0 → score = 1 and missing = [] (an empty month is complete, not a failure).
  • Duplicate key: two evidence rows for one obligation → duplicates contains the key, score is reduced by one count, never silently deduplicated.
  • Stale version: evidence submitted against a superseded obligation version → counted as late, not missing.
  • Withdrawal: obligation cancelled mid-period → excluded from expected; its evidence is ignored.
  • Exhaustion: all obligations verified → score = 1, missing = [].
  • Self-approval: approver = requester → transition refused; the function does not accept the evidence.
  • Concurrency: two approvers act on the same task version → the second write is rejected on version mismatch, not merged.

Architecture and API surface

Deep build §16 and §30: ClickUp REST v2, OAuth for humans, token for the agent service account, least privilege, idempotency via resolve-or-create by name, pagination via page/limit, versioning via the v2 prefix. No write is performed by this document. Setup runner: AI Hub/saas-setup/clickup_setup.py (dry-run supported).

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Schema inflation (28 fields as core) Setup cost and confusion Core 16 + conditional; D6 Ricardo
Automation sprawl (26 rules) Rules that break 12 build-set; conditional adoption Bruce Wayne
Illegal status transition Work stuck or skipped I9; transition matrix QA agent
Finance completeness with missing key Silent gap in reporting I10; algorithm fails loud Finance lead
Store stage advances early Premature opening I11; checklist gate Express Ops
Agent task reaches a real List Unreviewed work shipped I12; sandbox + human gate Human reviewer

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals and matrix as in the register. Implementation-specific: agent service account holds only resolve-or-create scopes, no delete; R-08-21/22 require a human approval gate before a draft leaves Intake Drafts; approval actors are checked by user ID (deep build §27 notes a native guard gap — flagged as a defect to fix before build).

Build order by dependency

Stage 1 — Schema lock (no writes):

  • Decide D6 (field set) and D7 (automation adoption) and the deep-build open questions carried below.
  • Exit: core 16 fields and 12 automations are named; conditional set is explicit.

Stage 2 — Provision (parallel, no data):

  • Create Workspace, Teams, Spaces, Folders, Lists, 16 fields, 5 views per Space.
  • Exit: field register matches §Field-by-field schema; views present.

Stage 3 — Behaviour (parallel):

  • 12 automations, status contracts, 4 dashboards, 7 templates.
  • Exit: automation manifest = 12; transition matrix loaded.

Stage 4 — Migration (sequential):

  • Zoho export → spreadsheet importer into existing Lists → dual run one cycle.
  • Exit: import report reviewed; no field lost.

Stage 5 — Conditional features (only if approved):

  • Finance completeness, approval workflow, agent contract, store openings, expiry tracking.
  • Exit: each conditional feature's tests pass independently.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
D6 Core 16 fields or full 28? Core 16; add conditional fields only when their feature is approved 28 up front inflates setup cost and scope
D7 Adopt which of the 14 non-build automations? Adopt only those whose feature is approved (table above) Adopting all 26 recreates the sprawl the build edition cut
D8 Creative approval workflow in v1? Defer to v2 unless a named approver is assigned Adds 4 rules and 2 fields with no owner
D9 Agent contract in v1? Defer; the guard gap (§27) must be fixed first Unreviewed agent tasks reach real Lists
D10 Store-opening module in v1? Include only if store openings are scheduled this year Adds 1 rule and 1 field with no consumer

Infinity Brands — ClickUp requirements register (governing)

18 features 9 P0 2 implemented 1 partial 11 absent 5 decisions

ClickUp: the governing requirements register

What this version fixes

  • The requirements baseline (2026-11-02-...-requirements-prd-v3-0.html) and the build edition (2026-11-02-...-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html) disagree on counts: 26 automation rules vs 12, 112 indexed tests vs 14, 4 forms vs 3. The build edition is the later document and publishes an explicit cut list, so the build edition governs configuration counts; the requirements baseline governs requirement intent. Cited: requirements baseline line 1287 (2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-requirements-prd-v3-0.html:1287); build edition lines 80–81 and 640–650 (2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:80-81, 2026-11-02-infinity-brands-clickup-prd-v3-1-build-edition.html:640-650).
  • R11 in the requirements baseline ends mid-sentence ("documents that capture a…"). The build edition names it as an open item and refuses to invent scope. This register keeps R11 open and does not fabricate content.
  • The deep build (2026-11-01-...-implementation-prd-v2-0-deep-build.html) assumes 28 custom fields and 26 automations. The build edition cuts automations to 12 and fields to 16. Where the deep build names a field or rule that the build edition drops, the build edition wins and the deep build must say so (see clickup-implementation-v2.md).
  • No ClickUp account or trial exists. Every workspace claim below is absent or UNVERIFIED — no API access. Nothing has been purchased or configured.
  • The near-duplicate -2.html pairs were diffed. Requirements -2 is 1,356 lines against 1,656; the content delta is formatting and a [!summary] callout only. Build-edition -2 is 711 lines against 786, same result. The longer file is the record; both agree on every requirement, field, automation and test.

Product in five lines

  1. What it is: a single ClickUp workspace that replaces Zoho Projects, Cliq and Coda for Infinity Brands, organised by function (Space) with brand, work type and quarter as fields.
  2. Who uses it: ~40 humans across ten functional teams, plus guests per client.
  3. Decision/action it enables: one answer to "what is outstanding for this person / brand / quarter" without opening three tools.
  4. Deliberately not: a CRM, a sprint/whiteboard tool, a per-brand workspace, or a Cliq history importer.
  5. The number that says it works: the proof cohort moves the majority of its new work into ClickUp by day 30 of the pilot (kill criterion in clickup-build-v3.md §8).

Domain model

Entities (one-line purpose):

  • Workspace: the single tenant; billing unit; holds all Spaces.
  • Space: accountable function; one owner; never a brand.
  • Folder: brand, client, store or year/quarter grouping inside a Space.
  • List: the work container inside a Folder; carries tasks.
  • Task: one piece of work with one home (Space); never duplicated across Spaces.
  • Field: Brand, Work Type, Function, Quarter etc.; a field cannot steal a task from its function.
  • View: one of five standard views, identical in every Space.
  • Automation: a rule with trigger + condition + action; counted and capped.
  • Goal: company/team objective; Task Target ties it to real work.
  • Doc: meeting minutes, decisions, SOPs; one home per template.
  • Dashboard: one card per question; four in total.
  • Team: reporting group; ten on Business, permission-sharing only on Business Plus.
  • Template: one of seven document templates with a named home and owner.

Tables that carry them (columns that matter):

  • spaces.csv: id, name, prefix, accountable, shape.
  • fields.csv: name, type, required-where, values, creation order.
  • views.csv: name, filter, sort, scope.
  • automations.csv: id, trigger, condition, action, why.
  • goals.csv: level, owner, check-in cadence.
  • tests.csv: id, area, expected value.
  • migration.csv: source, what moves, what does not, owner.

Indexes that decide performance: (1) task lookup by assignee + status; (2) task lookup by Brand + Work Type + Quarter; (3) Goal/Task Target join by owner.

Numbered invariants:

  • I1: One Workspace per tenant. Per-brand workspaces multiply seats and break cross-brand reporting.
  • I2: A task has exactly one home Space; brand/work type/quarter are fields, never Folders that relocate the task.
  • I3: Space = function, Folder = brand/client/store/year. Never the reverse.
  • I4: Sub-teams are reporting groups only, never permission boundaries, on Business.
  • I5: Ten Teams maximum on Business; step up to Business Plus only on a named trigger.
  • I6: Chat is wave two; Cliq stays read-only until export for legal/HR is signed off.
  • I7: Nothing is deleted in any source system during migration; both run one full cycle.
  • I8: Every rollup gets a named mechanism (dashboard card, automation-written field, or agent-computed field) before build.

F18 Four dashboards, one card per question P1 EXCO / MANCO / team / OKR visibility Dashboards count = 4

Architecture and API surface

Configuration pack (machine-readable, beside this document): spaces.csv, fields.csv, views.csv, automations.csv, goals.csv, tests.csv, migration.csv — 7 files, row counts 11/16/5/12/6/14/6.

ClickUp REST API v2 (https://api.clickup.com/api/v2) is the only integration surface. Principals: human members (OAuth), agent service account (token, least privilege), guest (view or permission-controlled, billed beyond allowance). Conventions: pagination via page/limit; idempotency via resolve-or-create by name, never by id; versioning via the API v2 prefix. No write is performed by this register; the setup runner (AI Hub/saas-setup/clickup_setup.py) exists but is not run here.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Per-brand workspace creep Seat count multiplies, cross-brand reporting breaks I1 invariant; one Workspace Ricardo (money)
Task duplicated across Spaces Same work counted twice I2; one home Space Function lead
Permission sharing assumed on Business Access not granted I4/I5; Business Plus triggers only Ricardo
Chat pilot judged on messages Feels busy, proves nothing Measured on tasks created from messages Bruce Wayne
Guest creep Unplanned seat spend Guest budget per client, monthly review Finance
Comment/attachment loss on import History gone Accept in writing before import Project lead
Formula illusions on rollups Broken reporting Named mechanism per rollup first Doc owner
Two systems, one habit People stay in Zoho Dual run one cycle, then freeze Ricardo

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: Ricardo (sponsor, money), function leads (accountable per Space), members (humans), guests (view or permission-controlled, billed beyond allowance), agent service account (least privilege, no delete). Role/action matrix: members create/edit tasks in their Space; guests view or permission-controlled per Folder; agents read/write only their scoped Lists; no principal deletes during migration (I7). Stored: tasks, fields, comments (lost on import — accepted in writing), attachments (lost on import — accepted in writing). Anchored outside the system: legal/HR Cliq export, Coda rollup mechanisms, the verification checklist before any payment. Signature/approval strength: ClickUp native approvals are a status, not a cryptographic signature; legal-grade evidence lives in the exported archives, not in ClickUp.

Build order by dependency

Stage A — Resolve and decide (no writes):

  • Ricardo answers the decisions register (plan tier, rollup mechanism, R11 scope, pilot funding, guest budget).
  • Exit: every open decision has a recorded answer or an explicit deferral.

Stage B — Provision the skeleton (parallel, no data yet):

  • Create the one Workspace, ten Teams, eleven Spaces, Folders, Lists, sixteen fields, five views per Space.
  • Exit: F1–F7 acceptance tests pass against the live workspace.

Stage C — Content and behaviour (parallel):

  • Goals/OKRs Space 10, Meetings Space 11, four dashboards, seven templates, twelve automations.
  • Exit: F8–F12, F17–F18 pass.

Stage D — Migration (sequential, data-bearing):

  • Export Zoho Projects to Excel; run spreadsheet importer into existing Lists; dual run one cycle.
  • Exit: F15–F16 pass; import report reviewed; no field lost.

Stage E — Pilot and gate:

  • Proof cohort (Finance daily control, Technology service desk) plus Visibility cohort (Leadership); thirty days; kill criterion.
  • Exit: majority of new work in ClickUp by day 30, or stop and keep Zoho.

dsh-maio-nexus — Unified DeepSeek Harness Plugin Suite (enhanced master)

Source PRD: 2026-11-02-prd-dsh-maio-nexus-unified-deepseek-harness-plugin-suite.html (1,556 lines, v10), rendered from a markdown record. This master is the buildable extraction: components with stable IDs, config contract, routing/fallback as a state machine, failure modes, and a dependency-staged build order. Facts and command output: /Volumes/Hiksemi 1TB/AI-Drive/Work/prd-program/facts/dsh-maio-nexus.md.

2. What this version fixes

The v10 PRD is a strong reading document but is not directly buildable: components are named in prose, the dispatch decision is described not specified, failure modes have no control column, and the build order is phases with dates. This enhanced master corrects those gaps, and records two claims v10 makes that measurement now contradicts.

Depth added beyond v10: stable component IDs (C1–C10), config contract table, dispatch state machine with arithmetic, failure-mode table with controls, and a dependency-staged build order with boolean exit tests and zero dates.

3. Product in five lines

  • What it is: a provider-adapter plugin suite (dsh-maio-nexus) that gives DeepSeek Harness (DSH) shared memory with the Hermes fleet, read-only recall over the Maio Hub vault, a dispatch contract between a long-lived Router and context-isolated Workers, and an external watchdog. Written as a provider adapter, not a vendor-locked plugin.
  • Who uses it: DSH surfaces — Web app :7788, Desktop app, CLI/ACP, T3 Code — acting as a Router that dispatches Workers. Hermes keeps its own channels; DSH holds no bot token and no inbound webhook.
  • Decision/action it enables: a DSH session can see what the Hermes fleet knows, search 2,500+ vault notes, render a brief to an MP4, and run the app-plan → app-build → app-verify chain end to end.
  • Deliberately not: a second memory store, a vault writer, a channel bot, a paid model lane, or an auto-merging agent. Memory writes go through Hermes' tools; vault writes go through Beast/OpenKnowledge.
  • Single number that says it works: acceptance suite 10/10 pass (A1–A9 + F2) with vault sha256 77e0679aae41cf7d unchanged before and after.

5. Domain model

Entities (one-line purpose):

  • Router — long-lived session that talks to surfaces, holds shared context, dispatches Workers, records why.
  • Worker — context-isolated session that executes one ticket; never speaks to a channel; returns memory candidates.
  • Thread — a conversation the Worker subscribes to; follow-ups bypass the Router.
  • Context Packet — the dispatch contract (§4.3.1): request, scoped facts, vault refs, acceptance, budget, reason.
  • Memory Candidate — fact + confidence + source + scope + durable flag returned Worker → Router.
  • Vault Note — a markdown file in Maio Hub (read-only source).
  • Vault Index — SQLite FTS5 over notes, with a files mtime/sha table.
  • Render Receipt — receipt.json per render job (§4.3.4).
  • Watchdog Report — ≤100-word plain-language status (§4.3.3).
  • Profile — a DSH profile (web, mini, headless, dshweb, acp, desktop, superagent) mapping to one Hermes profile.
  • Plugin — a staged third-party dsh bundle (Workstream B).
  • Trigger — an event (vault write, memory write, chat message, GitHub comment, CI result) that may reach the Router.

Tables that carry them:

  • files(path TEXT PK, title, mtime REAL, size INTEGER, sha TEXT) — index bookkeeping.
  • vault(path UNINDEXED, title, body) — FTS5 virtual table, tokenize='porter unicode61'.
  • cordis.patch.yml per profile — YAML list of insert/override entries; one mcp-nexus entry per installed profile.

Indexes that decide performance: FTS5 vault (path UNINDEXED, title+body indexed); files.path primary key for mtime lookup; files.mtime for incremental reindex (no full scan when unchanged).

Numbered invariants:

  • I1 — The vault is read-only to every DSH component; vault_index.py writes only to index/vault.db and never to the vault. F2 enforces via sha256 before/after.
  • I2 — Memory has exactly one source of truth: the provider Hermes reports as active. No parallel store; Workers never write.
  • I3 — A Worker's context packet contains only facts scoped to its work item; Worker A cannot recall Worker B's scratch facts (S2).
  • I4 — Every dispatch records a reason string; a dispatch without a reason is invalid (§4.3.1).
  • I5 — No secret (key, token, password, card/bank number, health detail) enters a packet, receipt, log, plugin config, or chat. Credential hard stop.
  • I6 — A trigger outside the allowed scope never reaches a model call; counted, not asserted (S7).
  • I7 — No render counts as finished until render-qc passes; only --allow-static / --allow-silent may waive a finding (F14).
  • I8 — The acceptance suite is authored from the PRD, stored outside every lane worktree, and the builder never reads it (G2, G7).
  • I9 — Proactive work runs in observe mode until a written rule is approved (§8).
  • I10 — DSH holds no bot token, no gateway, no inbound webhook (§3.4).

6. The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

6.1 Provider resolution state machine

States: UNRESOLVED → READING_CONFIG → PROBING_HEALTH → ACTIVE | FAILED.

Transitions:

  • UNRESOLVED → READING_CONFIG: on first tool call or status().
  • READING_CONFIG → PROBING_HEALTH: if ~/.hermes/hindsight/config.json parses (mode, api_url, bank_id present).
  • READING_CONFIG → ACTIVE(builtin): if no external config → built-in MEMORY.md/USER.md only.
  • PROBING_HEALTH → ACTIVE: if /health returns {"status":"healthy"} within 2 s.
  • PROBING_HEALTH → FAILED: on timeout/HTTP error → return named error, never empty.

Arithmetic: health probe timeout 2 s; memory recall timeout 30 s; max_tokens clamped [256, 8000], default 1200.

6.2 Index freshness state machine

States: MISSING → BUILDING → FRESH → STALE → MISSING.

Transitions:

  • MISSING → BUILDING: vault_index.py invoked (manual or cron).
  • BUILDING → FRESH: build.json written with notes count; exit 0.
  • FRESH → STALE: age > 5 min after a vault write (S4) OR age > 1560 min (watchdog threshold).
  • STALE → BUILDING: reindex triggered; incremental by mtime unless --rebuild.
  • Any state → MISSING: db file absent → recall returns error naming vault_index.py.

Arithmetic: full build 5.15 s for 2,557 notes; incremental 0.57 s (build.json 2026-11-03); median warm recall 1 ms; SLO vault recall warm < 50 ms; SLO memory recall warm < 100 ms; index freshness ≤ 5 min or answer says "stale index".

6.3 Dispatch state machine (Router)

States: IDLE → FILTERING → PACKET → DISPATCHED → RECORDING → IDLE, with a parallel PASS_THROUGH path.

Transitions:

  • IDLE → FILTERING: on trigger event.
  • FILTERING → PACKET: only if deterministic scope check passes (S7); otherwise drop and count.
  • FILTERING → IDLE: trigger outside scope → no model call, increment violation counter.
  • PACKET → DISPATCHED: context packet assembled (§4.3.1) with reason non-empty (I4).
  • DISPATCHED → RECORDING: Worker spawned with isolated context; Router records worker id + reason.
  • RECORDING → IDLE: Worker returns memory candidates; Router decides and writes through Hermes tools.
  • PASS_THROUGH: follow-up in same Thread reaches its Worker without Router re-decision; Router still records (S3).

Arithmetic / core function: context packet budget_tokens default 2000 (Inline & Spill). Router token overhead target −30% vs no pass-through (article measured −37%). Pass-through subscription is the mechanism.

Edge cases enumerated:

  • Concurrency: two Workers on same profile → per-Worker packets, no shared mutable cache (I3).
  • Empty set: vault query returns 0 rows → answer "no match", not "nothing known"; index age reported.
  • Stale version: vault write within SLA not yet indexed → answer says "stale index" (S4).
  • Withdrawal: a Worker returning no candidates → Router writes nothing; no empty fact.
  • Exhaustion: token budget exceeded → spill to pointers; model walks one hop (S8).
  • Self-approval: a Worker cannot approve its own memory write; Router is the only writer (I2).
  • Duplicate: same fact returned twice → Router dedupes by (fact, scope) before writing.

6.4 Failure modes at the engine level

See §9 for the full table; engine-level: provider down → FAILED state with named error; model missing → S9 fail loudly; tag drift → pin check at build time (§11 phase 0); context overflow → spill to pointers (S8).

8. Architecture and API surface

8.1 Component map (stable IDs)

ID Component Layer What it does Status
C1 nexus-memory-bridge Router + Worker Router: compact shared block (rules, prefs, plans). Worker: scoped query only. Provider resolved at runtime. implemented (nexus_memory_recall)
C2 nexus-profile-resolver Router Resolves owning Hermes profile before any store opens. Six+one profiles; each maps to exactly one Hermes profile. partial (provider_state reads config)
C3 nexus-vault-recall Router + Worker vault_recall tool: FTS5 + title search over Maio Hub markdown, read-only via Beast/OpenKnowledge. Returns snippets with paths and next-hop ids. implemented
C4 nexus-context-packet Router Emits dispatch contract §4.3.1: request, scoped facts, vault refs, acceptance, surface+session, reason. absent
C5 nexus-memory-return Worker → Router Workers return candidates (fact, confidence, source); Router writes via Hermes tools. absent
C6 nexus-thread-bind Router → Worker Worker holds Thread id; follow-ups pass through without Router re-decision (−37% tokens). Router still records. absent
C7 nexus-index-cron Ops Refreshes vault index, warms read path, publishes freshness timestamp. partial (script exists; scheduler unverified)
C8 nexus-watchdog Ops Separate timer session: Worker progress, store readability, index freshness, render-lane health. Plain language ≤100 words. Fail-closed. implemented (watchdog.sh)
C9 dsh.bundle + cordis.patch.yml Packaging One bundle for web/desktop/dshweb/headless/mini/acp. Desktop app-owned: patch layer only. implemented (overlay in 5 profiles)
C10 video-lane pack Worker Points at installed lane (render-qc, video-stack, motion-studio); no vendored binaries. partial (skills present; DSH wiring unproven)

Import rule (keeps it honest): components import only through the provider adapter interface (C1) and the vault read interface (C3). No component reads a store path directly; no component writes to the vault or to memory. Desktop is app-owned: patch layer only, never hand-edited bundles.

10. Security, permissions and audit record

Principals: Ricardo (owner; money, legal, merge, external publication, live-system writes), Adrianna A (staging, verification, PRD authoring), Beast (sole vault writer via OpenKnowledge), Router (DSH long-lived session), Worker (isolated, task-scoped), Watchdog (external timer session), Verifier (independent acceptance author).

Role/action matrix (✓ allowed, — denied):

Read vault ✓ ✓ ✓ ✓ ✓ (scoped) ✓ ✓
Run acceptance suite ✓ ✓ — — — — ✓

What is stored: vault notes (markdown), vault index (SQLite FTS5), memory bank (Hindsight maio-ops), cordis.patch.yml overlays, render receipts, acceptance scorecards, gates.csv. What is anchored outside the system: vault content in iCloud/Maio Hub (Beast is sole writer); git history of targetpraks.github.io; Hindsight bank on loopback. Signature/approval strength: no cryptographic signature is claimed; approvals are Ricardo's explicit word in chat plus the decisions register (§13). The app's ?token= URL is treated as a password and never enters a document, ticket, chat, gate or plugin config (I5).

11. Build order by dependency

Stages; work inside a stage runs in parallel. Each stage ends on a boolean exit test. No dates.

  • Stage 0 — Alignment (exit: written state table with commands and outputs; ollama list and curl :11434/v1/models captured; provider confirmed on all target profiles; prototype bundle format confirmed; tag pin verified). Owner: me (Adrianna).
  • Stage 1 — Staging (exit: files exist under ~/AI Hub/dsh-maio-nexus/; --dump-config diff shows overlay composing; nothing installed). Build C4 context-packet emitter, C5 memory-return, C6 thread-bind, C7 cron wiring, C10 video-lane DSH wiring. Owner: me.
  • Stage 2 — Review (exit: signed-off manifest; Ricardo reads staged bundle + two worked renders; nothing unexpected). Owner: Ricardo.
  • Stage 3 — Install (exit: dsh plugin … add output; app shows panel; render-qc passes on a DSH-issued render). Manual install into web/desktop/prototype; Safe Mode first run. Remaining installs in order: mflux (stills) → qwen-tts weights → Blender MCP (only on Ricardo's word) → RIFE ncnn (only if needed). Owner: Ricardo.
  • Stage 4 — Verify (exit: pass/fail table with raw command output logged to Agent-Output; 10/10 acceptance plus G4). Run F1–F5, S1–S9, G4 against real output; canary recall, isolation, vault hash, dispatch packet, lock test, cross-surface continuity, one rendered MP4. Owner: me.
  • Stage 5 — Operate (exit: receipts + monthly note). Courage nightly index; monthly plugin review (versions, CVEs, licences); monthly re-verification of video repo list; re-run Stage 0 whenever the memory provider changes. Owner: both.

Dependency notes: Stage 1 depends on Stage 0's provider confirmation (I2). Stage 3 depends on Stage 2 sign-off (§8 "Ricardo installs"). Stage 4 depends on Stage 3 (needs a real install). C4/C5/C6 (absent today) are Stage 1 work and gate G4.
