Migrate the fleet

25 features 18 P0 2 implemented 2 partial 6 absent 0 decisions

Migrating the fleet's memory (v2.1)

What this version fixes

  • "PROPOSED — nothing executed" is false. Phase 0 ran on 2026-11-02 06:42–06:44: six JSONL files and a manifest under AI-Drive/Agent-Output/adrianna/memory-migration-2026-10-30/, all six sha256 values verified. An install, rollout and revert were also scripted and at least partly run that morning (rollout_profiles.sh, revert_mnemosyne.sh, cleanup_mnemosyne.sh, .env.bak-mnemosyne-20261102T070554). Nothing of Mnemosyne remains.
  • "29 configs; dexter, lois, shadow have none" is stale. There are 32 live config files (31 profiles plus the main config) and every one names provider: hindsight — the three named profiles got it in the 2026-11-02 config rewrite. What they lack is a bank binding: 28 of 31 have hindsight/config.json. So they are half-wired, which is worse than the original claim: they will write somewhere, just not where anyone chose.
  • The counts are stale and were already stale when printed. 166,333 facts at 09:00 became 175,023 at 10:41 the next day, and the three banks take about 129 new facts an hour. Every count in this document is timestamped.
  • The Phase 0 export decays. It froze 171,425 facts; 28 hours later the same banks hold 175,023. A frozen export cannot be the import source — the archive is evidence and a rehearsal, and the cut-over needs a fresh one.
  • The bake-off comparison understated the gap the other way. Re-scored from the saved runs, Hindsight is 20/22 at rank one and 22/22 at rank five; Mnemosyne is 17/22 and 20/22. Both are 13/13 on single-fact recall. The decision is about cost and egress, not about quality — and the document should say so in one line instead of implying parity.

Product in five lines

  1. What it is: a migration of the fleet's long-term memory from a 4.5 GB cloud-extracted Hindsight container to a local SQLite engine, without losing a fact, a timestamp or a source.
  2. Who uses it: every Hermes profile in the fleet; the seats that write memory unattended (beast, scribe, builder, the executive seats) matter most.
  3. Decision it enables: which engine holds the fleet's memory, at what running cost and with what rollback.
  4. Deliberately not: a memory redesign. Extraction quality, consolidation and "dreaming" are follow-ups, not scope.
  5. The number that says it works: facts in each bank after import equal facts exported for it within 0.5%, and a rollback returns a real memory id from the original bank before anything is deleted.

Status ledger

States: implemented (proven by a command), partial (exists, incomplete), absent (proven missing), unverified (not checked in this pass).

Capability State Evidence Gap
Hindsight serving the fleet implemented /health healthy; 3 live banks, 175,023 facts at 10:41; hermes memory status → Provider: hindsight runs a cloud extractor at ollama.com
Phase 0 export with per-file hash implemented manifest.json + 6 JSONL, 171,425 facts, all six shasum -a 256 match frozen at 2026-11-02 06:44; now 3,598 facts behind

Domain model

Entity Purpose
Bank an isolation domain: maio, maio-ops, maio-personal; one per group of profiles
Document one retained conversation chunk as ingested, with a content hash and a length
Chunk a slice of a document that facts are extracted from
Memory (fact) one extracted statement with a type, a timestamp and a source document
Observation a consolidated belief built from several facts, carrying a proof count
Link a typed edge between memories: semantic, temporal, entity, caused-by
Entity a named thing the extractor resolved, used for graph traversal
Operation a server-side unit of work with a status; where a failed import shows up

Columns that decide correctness, from the live API:

Table Deciding columns (type)
documents id text PK · content_hash text · created_at timestamptz · text_length int · memory_unit_count int · document_metadata jsonb · tags text[]
memories id uuid PK · text text · fact_type enum(world, experience, observation) · document_id text FK · chunk_id text · mentioned_at timestamptz · proof_count int · state enum(valid, invalidated) · source_memory_ids uuid[] · metadata jsonb
links from_id uuid · to_id uuid · link_type enum(semantic, temporal, entity, caused_by)
operations id uuid · status enum(pending, processing, completed, failed)

Scale today: 175,023 memories, 4,465 documents, 6,611,274 links, 30,734 observations.

The three indexes that decide performance and, here, correctness:

  1. memories(document_id) — the upsert path resolves a document by id and deletes its memory units before rebuild.
  2. memories(mentioned_at) — temporal recall and every ordering claim depend on it.
  3. documents(content_hash) — the only handle that can tell a duplicate ingestion from a legitimate second session.

Invariants:

  • I1 Identity. Every imported memory carries the source id it came from (hindsight_id) under a unique key. Two imports of the same record resolve to one row.
  • I2 Timestamp. mentioned_at survives import as the record's own time, not the import time. A memory's position on the timeline is part of the memory.
  • I3 Source. Every imported memory names its document and its bank. A memory with no source is a rumour and must not be imported.
  • I4 Kind. fact_type survives import. Collapsing world, experience and observation into one kind destroys the distinction between a statement and a belief with evidence.
  • I5 Single writer per bank. Exactly one engine writes a bank at a time. Dual-write is not permitted, because it cannot be reconciled by id across two id spaces.
  • I6 Read-back before delete. Nothing in Hindsight is stopped or removed until a recall against the new engine returns a real id from the old one.
  • I7 One archive of record. The export directory plus its manifest and hashes is the archive. No second, drifting copy is created.
  • I8 Reversibility. Every profile can return to Hindsight by changing one line and restoring its config file.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

A migration is a state machine over banks, and the states are checkable:

EXPORTED ─► HASHED ─► IMPORTED ─► RECONCILED ─► CUT_OVER ─► FROZEN ─► DECOMMISSIONED
              │            │            │
              └────────────┴────────────┴──────► ABORTED  (bank stays on Hindsight)
  • EXPORTED — every document and every memory of one bank is on disk, counts recorded at the start.
  • HASHED — a sha256 per file, written into the manifest; the state is only reachable when the re-read hash matches.
  • IMPORTED — every record written into the target bank with I1–I4 intact. Resumable: the import key is the source id, so a restart re-walks and updates rather than appends.
  • RECONCILED — imported equals exported per bank within tolerance, with missing and extra lists printed, not summarised.
  • CUT_OVER — the profile's memory.provider names the new engine, verified by hermes memory status.
  • FROZEN — the old bank takes no new writes; the container is stopped but the volume is held.
  • DECOMMISSIONED — the volume is removed, only on an explicit go-ahead, only after the hold.

The pure function at the core:

reconcile(exported_set, imported_set, key=hindsight_id) → {
    missing: [id],        # exported, not imported          → blocks CUT_OVER
    extra:   [id],        # imported, not in the export     → blocks CUT_OVER (unknown provenance)
    duplicated: [key],    # key present more than once      → blocks CUT_OVER (I1 broken)
    matched: int,         # key present in both
    tolerance: (imported - exported) / exported
}

pass  ⇔ missing = 0 ∧ extra = 0 ∧ duplicated = 0 ∧ |tolerance| ≤ 0.005

Reconciliation is per bank and per file, and the comparison is on the id, never on the text. Text comparison looks precise and is not: the extractor rewrites phrasing, so two runs over the same source produce different strings for the same fact.

Edge cases, each with its rule:

Case What happens Rule
A record is re-indexed twice a second write of the same source id I1 upsert on hindsight_id; the second write updates in place. Without that key the second run duplicates all 171,425 facts and doubles every proof count
A document is re-ingested under its old document_id Hindsight deletes the document and its memory units, then rebuilds: new uuid per memory, chunk ids renumbered, proof_count reset, links to the old nodes orphaned never re-import a document into a bank that already holds it; import facts, not documents
Empty bank reconcile prints matched 0, tolerance 0, and passes trivially a bank with 0 exported records cannot be "verified" — mark it not applicable, not pass
Stale export imported count is 3,598 lower than live re-export at cut-over with the fleet paused, or import the delta by id; never mix a frozen export with live counts
Record invalidated between export and import (state: invalidated) it is in the export but should not be live carry state and invalidated_at; import invalidated records as archived, not as valid memories
Fact under 40 characters the current importer skips it silently remove the length filter; the measured loss is 536 + 409 + 188 = 1,133 facts
Duplicate text across banks same sentence in two banks is legitimate dedupe within a bank by id, never across banks by text
Import dies at record 40,000 restart would re-add the first 40,000 under the old importer idempotent key + resumable cursor; restart re-walks and matches
Disk exhaustion mid-import partial bank, unknown state import into a bank suffixed _stage, reconcile, then promote by rename or re-point
Self-approval (the operator marks reconciliation pass) nobody checked the reconciler prints the numbers; a second principal reads them
Two profiles writing one bank facts from unrelated seats mixed I5 + per-profile bank; the 28 existing bindings show the fleet already depends on this

What must be preserved, stated precisely, because this is the whole migration:

  1. Identity — the source id of every record, carried as hindsight_id under a unique constraint, so a record can be found again after the engine changes.
  2. Timestamp — mentioned_at as the record's own time, plus created_at of its document, so "when did this happen" keeps its answer.
  3. Source — document_id, bank, and the original fact_type, so a fact can be traced to the conversation it came from and remains a statement rather than a belief.
  4. State — valid / invalidated with invalidated_at, so a withdrawn fact does not come back to life.
  5. Links — the 6.6 M typed edges are not re-creatable. Either they are migrated as first-class rows, or the document states in writing that the new engine starts with a flat memory and rebuilds links over time. Silence here is the defect.

What breaks if a record is re-indexed twice, concretely: with a stable key, nothing but wasted time. Without one, the bank doubles, every proof_count doubles (a belief supported by 3 facts is now supported by 6), the reconciliation tolerance check fails by 100%, and the export archive stops being a description of the live bank. That is why I1 sits above every other requirement in this document.

Feature table

ID Feature Priority Why it matters Acceptance test (expected value)
F1 Export every bank to JSONL with counts at start P0 The archive is the only thing that survives a bad migration 3 banks × 2 files; wc -l equals the count read at the same minute, exactly
F2 sha256 per file in a manifest P0 An unhashed archive is a guess 6 hashes in manifest.json, each equal to shasum -a 256 of its file
F3 Stable import key hindsight_id under a unique index P0 Without it, a second import duplicates 171,425 records importing the same file twice leaves the bank's count unchanged
F4 Timestamp preservation P0 "When did this happen" is half of what a memory is a record mentioned 2026-10-14 imports with mentioned_at 2026-10-14, not the import date
F5 Source and kind preservation P0 A belief and a statement must stay distinguishable imported observation stays observation with its proof_count, not world with 1
F6 Per-profile bank isolation before first write P0 Two seats sharing a bank cannot be un-mixed later writing in profile A leaves profile B's recall empty
F7 Resumable, idempotent import P0 A 175 k-record import will be interrupted interrupt at 40,000, restart, final count equals the export count
F8 Remove the <40-character filter P0 1,133 facts are lost silently today imported count includes the 536 maio-ops records under 40 chars
F9 Per-bank reconciliation with missing/extra/duplicate lists P0 A tolerance alone hides which records went missing missing=0, extra=0, duplicated=0, tolerance ≤ 0.5% for each of the 3 banks
F10 Reconcile on id, not text P0 The extractor rewrites phrasing between runs two runs over one source produce different text and the same id set
F11 Canary profile with the 13 single-fact probe questions P0 One profile fails cheaply; 31 fail expensively 13/13 at rank one, scored by scorer.py against corpus.json
F12 hermes memory status proof of the switch P0 The config file is not the runtime grep -qE "^ Provider: +mnemosyne$" exits 0
F13 Rollback drill, written and run P0 An untested rollback is a hope; P1 defect by definition flip back, recall returns a real id from maio-ops, completed before any deletion
F14 Writes after cut-over are exportable P1 Rollback loses anything the new engine wrote unless it can be exported export the canary bank after 24 hours of use and re-import into Hindsight without duplicates
F15 No cloud egress on a write P0 The entire point is local dig +short ollama.com resolved, then nettop shows no connection to that address during one retain and one recall
F16 First-run embedding download pre-warmed P1 A cold start that stalls is an outage model present locally; a start with the network down still recalls
F17 Prompt growth measured, not guessed P1 41 declared tools sit in every prompt tool count and first-turn prompt tokens recorded; flag if above +2,000
F18 Per-profile config diff before and after P0 32 files change; one missed file is a seat on the wrong engine grep -c "provider: mnemosyne" equals 32 after rollout, 0 after rollback
F19 Bank mapping table, one row per profile P1 Three profiles today have no binding at all 31 rows; bank column never empty
F20 Export freshness check at cut-over P0 The archive is already 3,598 facts behind export timestamp later than the last write to every bank, printed
F21 Operation-failure review P1 84 failed operations and 2,825 failed consolidations exist today failures enumerated by bank; each classified lost or retryable

F23 Decommission only after the hold P0 Deleting the volume is the one irreversible act volume still listed on the day after cut-over; removal is a separate, logged action
F24 Consolidation plan stated, not implied P2 90,868 facts await consolidation and 30,734 observations exist follow-up document named, scope and owner stated
F25 Link-preservation decision recorded P0 6.6 M typed edges are not rebuilt by accident one written line: migrated as rows, or explicitly dropped

Architecture and API surface

The migration is a set of scripts beside the fleet, not a service.

AI Hub/memory-bakeoff/                    rehearsal kit (today)
  corpus.json  scorer.py                  the 24-question probe set and its scorer
  export_banks.py                         Phase 0: bank → JSONL + manifest
  import_facts.py                         Phase 3: JSONL → Mnemosyne (to be rewritten: I1–I4)
  rollout_profiles.sh / revert_mnemosyne.sh / cleanup_mnemosyne.sh
AI-Drive/Agent-Output/adrianna/memory-migration-2026-10-30/
  <bank>.facts.jsonl  <bank>.documents.jsonl  manifest.json
~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/hindsight/config.json    the bank binding, per profile
~/.hermes/config.yaml                                  memory.provider, the switch

One import rule that keeps it honest: the archive is read-only after hashing. Nothing rewrites a JSONL file; a re-export writes a new dated directory. A migration whose source changes while it runs cannot be reconciled.

Endpoints used, and by whom:

Endpoint Method What for Who may call
/v1/default/banks GET bank list and counts anyone, read-only
/v1/default/banks/{bank}/documents GET document ids, hashes, lengths migration operator
/v1/default/banks/{bank}/memories/list GET paged facts with timestamps and types migration operator
/v1/default/banks/{bank}/stats GET nodes, links, observations, operation status anyone, read-only
/v1/default/banks/{bank}/export GET server-side export alternative to paging migration operator
/memories (POST) POST write path — never used to re-import into the same bank nobody during this migration

Conventions the build must keep:

  • Pagination at 200 records, offset-stable within a run; a run records its start count so drift is visible.
  • Idempotency by hindsight_id, never by text.
  • Versioning: the archive directory is dated; the manifest is versioned by its own start timestamp.
  • No dual-write. A bank has one writer; the flip is atomic per profile.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Second import run duplicates everything a bank that doubles overnight I1 unique key on hindsight_id; F3 test runs the import twice migration operator
Facts silently dropped by the length filter a short preference the fleet no longer remembers F8 removes the filter; count reconciliation includes them reconcile step
Documents re-ingested under their old id Hindsight deleting and rebuilding units, links orphaned import facts, never documents; the write path is not used against a live bank migration operator
Export stale at cut-over imported bank missing the last day of work F20 freshness check; re-export with the fleet paused migration operator
Isolation left off every profile reads every other profile's memory F6 before the first write; F18 config diff proves the setting canary reviewer
Rollback untested discovering at 02:00 that the flip does not revert F13 drill executed and written before any deletion Ricardo's go-ahead gate
New writes lost on rollback a period of work vanishes when the provider flips back F14 exportable new writes; or accept and state the window in writing migration operator
Embedding model download stalls a cold start gateway hangs on first retain F16 pre-warm and an offline start test canary reviewer
Prompt growth from 41 tools slower, dearer turns on 31 profiles F17 measures on the canary; fallback is a smaller engine, not trimming canary reviewer
Operation backlog hides failed writes retries never surface as errors F21 enumerates 84 failed operations and 2,825 failed consolidations migration operator
Someone removes the volume early the archive is the only copy left F23: removal is separate, logged and gated Ricardo

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: Ricardo (authority), Bruce (agent, migration operator and verifier), each profile's agent (writer of its own bank), the Hindsight container (process), the Mnemosyne database (file).

Role Read banks Export Import Flip a profile Delete the volume
Ricardo yes yes no approves approves
Bruce (agent) yes yes yes, into a staging bank prepares, does not approve no
Profile agent own bank no no no no
Container / process all, at runtime no no no no

What is stored: conversation transcripts and extracted statements — names, preferences, prices, plans, and whatever staff and Ricardo have said in session. Some of it is personal. The archive holds the full original text of every document (43 MB in the current export), which is strictly more than the facts.

Anchored outside the system: the nightly volume tar (15 archives, newest 1.3 GB, 14-day retention) and the hashed JSONL archive. Both live on the Hiksemi volume, which is a single point of failure for both — worth stating plainly.

How strong the audit claim is: sha256 proves the archive has not changed since it was written. It proves nothing about who wrote it or whether it is complete. Completeness comes only from the count comparison at export time, and that comparison is against a live, drifting database. There is no signature, no notary, and no append-only log of the migration's own actions beyond the scripts themselves. The honest sentence is: the archive is verifiable, the migration is reproducible, and neither is attested.

Build order by dependency

Stage 0 — re-export at cut-over (Phase 0 exists and must be repeated). Work: run export_banks.py against all three banks with the fleet paused or accepting drift, write a fresh manifest, verify every hash, record the export time and each bank's last-write time. Exit test: the export time is later than the last write in every bank, all six hashes match, and each file's line count equals the count read at the same minute.

Stage 1 — isolation and the bank map. Runs in parallel with Stage 2. Work: one row per profile in a bank map; memory.mnemosyne.profile_isolation true and a bank name per profile before any write; the three profiles with no binding today get one. Exit test: 31 rows, no empty bank column, and a write in one profile is invisible to another.

Stage 2 — the importer, rewritten to the invariants. Work: hindsight_id unique key, timestamp, source, kind and state preserved, resumable, no length filter. Exit test: importing the same file twice leaves the bank's count unchanged, and a record whose mention date precedes the import by a month reads its own date back.

Stage 3 — canary on one profile. Work: install at the catalog sha, enable, flip the canary, restart, run the probe set and one real retain. Exit test: Provider: mnemosyne matches the regex, the 13 single-fact questions score 13/13 at rank one, and one new retain is recallable within the same session.

Stage 4 — import and reconcile every bank. Work: stage into a suffixed bank, import, reconcile on id, print missing/extra/duplicate lists. Exit test: for each bank, missing = 0, extra = 0, duplicated = 0, tolerance within 0.5%, and the link decision recorded.

Stage 5 — rollback drill, before rollout. Work: flip the canary back, recall a real id from maio-ops, flip forward again, export the new writes. Exit test: the rollback recall returns a real memory id, the forward flip re-passes the probe, and no duplicate appears after the round trip.

Stage 6 — wave rollout. Work: two lines per profile in waves — the writing seats, then the executive seats, then the rest; a config diff after each wave. Exit test: grep -c "provider: mnemosyne" equals the number of profiles in the wave, and the probe passes on a sampled profile from each wave.

Stage 7 — freeze, hold and decommission. Work: stop the container once no profile writes to it; hold the volume; remove it only on a separate go-ahead. Exit test: 14 consecutive days with zero writes to every Hindsight bank, then the volume listing is empty and the archive hashes still verify.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Migrate all three banks, or migrate two and start maio fresh? Migrate all three. maio holds 58,781 facts and the fleet's shared context; a fresh start throws away the fleet's memory of its own decisions A fresh maio is cleaner and cheaper to import, and loses every cross-company recollection on day one
2 Delete the Hindsight volume after the hold, or keep it forever? Keep it as a cold archive. 4.5 GB costs almost nothing; the irreversible act buys 4.5 GB Deleting reclaims 4.5 GB once and removes the only copy that is not a tar
3 Which profile is the canary? scribe — it writes constantly, so failures surface immediately, and it is not the seat that talks to you Batman as canary fails in the seat that runs the fleet, which is the wrong blast radius
4 Are the 6.6 M links migrated, or explicitly dropped? Dropped, in writing, with a rebuild plan. Migrating them is a second project Dropping them costs graph recall until the new engine learns its own edges; migrating them costs a build nobody has scoped
5 One bank for the fleet, or the current three? Keep three. Isolation already exists in the bindings and three banks match three audiences One bank is simpler to operate and mixes work memory with personal memory
6 Who signs the reconciliation pass? Bruce prepares, Ricardo reads the printed numbers. One human pair of eyes on an irreversible step An agent-only sign-off is faster and has nobody to answer to if a bank is short
7 Consolidation and "dreaming" now, or a follow-up? Follow-up. 90,868 facts are already unconsolidated in Hindsight; importing that backlog into a new engine is a separate problem Doing it now delays the migration behind the hardest part of the old system

The Local Farmer — one grow room sheet, and the arithmetic that has to hold

25 features 16 P0 3 implemented 1 partial 4 absent 0 decisions

One grow room sheet (v2)

What this version fixes

  • The evidence line claims 2,910 sourced facts. The file it cites holds 14,520 facts across 794 documents (wc -l AI-Drive/Agent-Output/batman/brand-knowledge/TLF/facts.all.jsonl). The wrong number was never load-bearing, but it is the number a reader uses to judge everything else.
  • The −67.1 kg variance is not reproducible. 17.3 × 4.33 − 17.04 = 57.9, and (67.1 + 17.04) ÷ 17.3 = 4.86 periods, so no printed input produces it. The dashboards' own variance cells sum to −41.93 kg and −48.21 kg.
  • The machine rule prices 10 kg per machine per month, which the same document's occupancy rule forbids: at 120 g and 95% the ceiling is 8.885 kg. 10 kg needs 20.3 harvestable trays a period against 18 available, or 131 g a tray. The 24-month payback test then never binds — it reads 2.95 months either way.
  • The two published grow-room files are one document in two themes, not two opinions: body identical after stripping <style> (14,332 characters each). They must not be revised separately.
  • "Built and live, nine tabs" is stated but not evidenced here: Zoho Sheet was not queried in this pass. It is unverified, not implemented, in the ledger below.

Product in five lines

  1. What it is: one operating sheet for the grow room — capacity, client demand, the machine order decision, harvested yield and invoiced rand on one surface, with the arithmetic visible.
  2. Who uses it: the TLF GM and the grower standing in the room; finance reads the invoice tab.
  3. Decision it enables: sow this much, quote this client this start date, order or hold the next machine — each answer derived, not argued.
  4. Deliberately not: an accounting system, a costing model, or a replacement for the harvest ledger, which stays the append-only log.
  5. The number that says it works: the machines figure the plan prints matches an independent recomputation to the same integer on every row, and no tab prints a capacity number that was typed rather than computed.

Status ledger

States used: implemented (proven by a command), partial (exists, incomplete), absent (proven missing), unverified (not checked in this pass).

Capability State Evidence Gap
Six source workbooks exist and disagree implemented 794 documents / 14,520 facts in the TLF sweep; every workbook the source PRD names is present, 19 to 1,985 facts each none — this is the problem being solved
Harvest ledger with 130 trays / 17.04 kg / 69.12 kg target implemented harvest_ledger_snapshot.pdf facts in the sweep; weekly rows 45/28/19/17/10/11 sum to 130 trays and 17.04 kg ledger's capacity target is 2× the room (69.12 = 8.64 × 8 sessions)
Weekly capacity arithmetic (spaces × 7 ÷ cycle × g × reliability) implemented recomputed: 4→8.21, 5→10.26, 7→14.36, 8→16.42, 10→20.52 kg correct, and contradicted by every other TLF document
Replacement sheet, nine tabs, filed in TLF_Reporting unverified UNVERIFIED — Zoho Sheet and WorkDrive were not queried in this pass must be inspected tab by tab before any tab is trusted

Domain model

Entity Purpose
Parameter one named fact the whole sheet reads: cycle days, spaces per machine, yield, reliability, price
Machine a physical unit with 36 spaces; capacity is a function of machine count, never a typed number
Batch one sowing on one date into one machine: the unit the yield log counts
Harvest trays taken from a batch on a date, with grams; the only source of actual yield
Client a buyer, with channel and price basis
Order a client's demand in one 7-day period, confirmed or prospect — the only place demand is entered
Invoice grams sold and rand received, pasted from Books
Plan period one 7-day row joining demand to capacity and naming the sowing and harvest
Machine decision a dated verdict with the parameters that produced it

Sheet tables that carry them, with the columns that decide correctness:

Tab Key columns (type) Notes
Settings key text PK · value decimal(12,4) · unit text · changed_by text · changed_at timestamp one row per parameter, no duplicates; the only typed numbers in the workbook
Capacity machines int PK · spaces int · trays_7d decimal(6,2) · kg_7d decimal(6,3) · kg_day decimal(6,3) · kg_month decimal(6,3) · rand_month decimal(10,2) every cell except machines is a formula over Settings
Yield Log date date · machine text · trays_sown int · trays_harvested int · kg_harvested decimal(7,3) · failed_trays int · g_per_tray decimal(6,2) append-only; g_per_tray = kg × 1000 ÷ trays_harvested
Demand period text (ISO 7-day key) · client text · channel text · kg decimal(7,2) · price_kg decimal(8,2) · status enum(Confirmed, Prospect) unique on (period,client,channel)
Plan period text PK · demand_kg · capacity_kg · headroom_kg · pct_used · trays_to_sow · kg_to_harvest · verdict enum 13 rows, all formulas except the period key
Prospects client · kg_day decimal(5,2) · days_7d int · price_kg · kg_7d · trays_7d · spaces · machines int · servable_today bool · first_period text days_7d is the delivery-days answer that moves the machine count by two
Invoices invoice_no text PK · date · client · grams int · rand decimal(10,2) · source text · pasted_by text unique on invoice_no; source names paste or CSV and the file
Machine decision decided_at timestamp · peak_demand_kg · machines_needed · machines_held · shortfall · capital_rand · contribution_rand · payback_months · verdict enum(ORDER, HOLD, NO) stamped with the Settings snapshot used

The three keys that decide whether the sheet holds together:

  1. Demand(period, client, channel) — unique. A second row for the same client in the same period double-counts demand and can trigger a machine that is not needed.
  2. Invoices(invoice_no) — unique. The paste lane is where duplicates enter, and a duplicated invoice inflates sell-through above 1.
  3. Yield Log(date, machine) — the sort key every variance cell reads; unsorted, g_per_tray silently points at the wrong batch.

Invariants:

  • I1 Space-time. For any 7-day window, trays_harvested ≤ spaces × 7 ÷ cycle_days. This is the invariant the 17.28 kg target breaks.
  • I2 One source. Capacity is computed only from Settings. No tab may hold a typed capacity, yield or price.
  • I3 One rounding rule. trays = CEILING(kg × 1000 ÷ (yield_g × reliability)); spaces = trays × cycle_days ÷ 7; machines = CEILING(spaces ÷ spaces_per_machine), each rounded at its own step and printed rounded. No row may ceil one column and floor the next.
  • I4 Sell-through ≤ 1. Invoiced grams in a period ≤ harvested grams in that period. A breach means harvest rows are missing, not that sales are extraordinary.
  • I5 Stamped decisions. A machine verdict carries the Settings snapshot it used; changing yield later never rewrites a past decision.
  • I6 No divide by zero. g_per_tray exists only where trays_harvested > 0.
  • I7 Append-only log. A correction is a new row flagged as a correction; no historical row is edited.
  • I8 Reliability once. 95% is applied at capacity. Actuals are actuals and carry no reliability factor.

The engine, as a state machine with arithmetic

Tray states and transitions:

SOWN ──► GROWING ──► HARVEST_READY ──► HARVESTED
          │                 │
          └────► FAILED ◄───┘        (contamination, mould, power loss)
  • SOWN → GROWING on the sowing date; occupancy begins.
  • GROWING → HARVEST_READY at cycle_days from sowing (10–14 days in practice).
  • HARVEST_READY → HARVESTED on the harvest date, which writes one Yield Log row.
  • Any state → FAILED writes a failed_trays count and no grams. A failed tray consumes its space for the full cycle and yields nothing — the reason reliability is 95% and not 100%.

Pure functions at the core, with inputs and units:

capacity(machines, spaces_per_machine, cycle_days, yield_g, reliability)
  trays_7d = machines × spaces_per_machine × 7 ÷ cycle_days      [tray-harvests per 7-day period]
  kg_7d    = trays_7d × yield_g × reliability ÷ 1000             [kg per 7-day period]

machines_required(kg_7d, yield_g, reliability, cycle_days, spaces_per_machine)
  trays    = CEILING(kg_7d × 1000 ÷ (yield_g × reliability))     [trays]
  spaces   = trays × cycle_days ÷ 7                              [spaces]
  machines = CEILING(spaces ÷ spaces_per_machine)                [machines]

Worked, at Settings defaults (36 spaces, 14-day cycle, 120 g, 95%, 4.33 periods a month):

Machines Spaces Trays/7d kg/7d kg/day kg/month R/month at R0.4504/g
4 144 72 8.21 1.17 35.54 16,008
5 180 90 10.26 1.47 44.43 20,009
7 252 126 14.36 2.05 62.20 28,013
8 288 144 16.42 2.35 71.08 32,015
10 360 180 20.52 2.93 88.85 40,019

Edge cases, each with its rule:

Case Rule
cycle_days ≠ 14 everything derives from the parameter; 7 days doubles capacity, 21 days cuts it by a third
cycle_days < 7 more than one cycle is in flight per space; trays_7d exceeds spaces, which is legal, and I1 is rewritten as trays_7d ≤ spaces × 7 ÷ cycle_days
yield_g = 0 or reliability = 0 capacity returns 0 and machines_required returns an error cell, never a divide-by-zero string
Empty demand Plan prints 0 and pct_used = 0, and the verdict is HOLD with the shortfall stated
Demand exactly at 90% the threshold is >, so 90.0% is HOLD. The sheet must print the gap to the threshold, not just the verdict
Duplicate (period, client, channel) unique key rejects the second row; the sheet shows the existing row's address
Order withdrawn after sowing the batch is not un-sown; the kg moves to the next period's headroom and the withdrawal is a note on the order row
Exhaustion (all spaces sown) trays_to_sow clips to spaces − occupied and prints 0, never negative
Stale Settings between plan and harvest I5 keeps the decision; the plan recomputes and prints the parameter version it used
Self-approval (grower writes yield and signs the machine decision) role matrix forbids it; the decision tab records decided_by and refuses the same name as logged_by
Duplicate invoice invoice_no unique; a repeated paste is rejected with the original row named
Harvest logged with 0 trays I6 blocks g_per_tray; the row is still written so the failure is visible

Feature table

ID Feature Priority Why it matters Acceptance test (expected value)
F1 Settings tab as the single input block P0 Every other number derives from it; one place to change a fact all 8 parameters present (cycle_days, spaces_per_machine, yield_g, reliability, price_kg, build_cost_rand, franchisee_price_rand, periods_per_month); changing yield_g from 120 to 150 moves the 4-machine kg/7d from 8.21 to 10.26
F2 Capacity table, machines 1–12 P0 The room's ceiling and the price of the next machine 12 rows; machines=4 reads 8.21 kg/7d; machines=7 reads 14.36
F3 Capacity in rand at Settings price P1 Turns kilograms into the number a bank asks about machines=4 at R0.4504/g reads R16,008/month (±R1)
F4 One rounding rule across the workbook P0 The source table's 246 trays / 491 spaces row is a contradiction for 4 kg/day × 7 days the sheet prints 246 trays, 492 spaces, 14 machines
F5 Space-time guard (I1) P0 Blocks the 17.28 kg target from ever being entered as capacity entering 17.28 kg/7d for 4 machines returns a violation naming 8.21
F6 Demand entry, one row per client per period P0 Duplicate rows invent demand and buy machines second row for the same client and period is rejected, first row address shown
F7 Confirmed vs prospect split P0 Machines bought on hope sit at 40% utilisation Plan counts only Confirmed; a 14 kg prospect week shows 0 kg demand and a separate prospect line
F8 13-period rolling plan P0 Sowing now harvests later; the plan is the only place that link exists 13 rows; period 1 trays_to_sow = CEILING(period 2 demand) with kg_to_harvest from the same batch
F9 Headroom and percent-used per period P1 Shows how close the room is before it is full 14 kg demand on 8.21 kg capacity prints headroom −5.79 and 170%
F10 Machine verdict per period P0 The order decision must be one word with a reason demand 8.0 kg against 8.21 capacity in two consecutive periods prints ORDER; 7.0 prints HOLD with gap 0.39 kg
F11 Payback arithmetic at the real yield P0 10 kg/month overstates contribution by 12% contribution uses 8.885 kg/machine → R4,001.88, payback 3.32 months at R13,294
F12 Two price bases kept apart P0 R13,294 build cost and R45,000 franchisee price are a 3.38× spread the tab prints both, labels which one the verdict used, and never mixes them in one cell
F13 Prospects calculator (kg/day and delivery days in, machines out) P0 Answers a client in the meeting, with a start date 2 kg/day 7 days → 7 machines; 2 kg/day weekdays → 5 machines
F14 First servable period per prospect P1 A quote without a start date is a wish prospect needing 7 machines against 4 held returns the period the 3rd machine's harvest lands
F15 Yield Log append-only with variance P0 Actuals against model is the only feedback loop a row with 18 trays and 2.2 kg reads 122.2 g/tray and variance +2.2 g against 120
F16 Failed-tray tracking P1 Reliability is a guess until failures are counted 2 failed of 18 shows 11.1% failure and recomputes the period's realised reliability
F17 Realised reliability feeding Settings P2 95% should be measured, not assumed after 4 periods the Settings suggestion reads the trailing average and never auto-applies
F18 Invoice paste with provenance P0 Sell-through is the only check on what is earned per kg pasted row records source, pasted_by, date; duplicate invoice_no rejected
F19 Sell-through per period P0 Grams invoiced ÷ grams harvested period with 1.8 kg invoiced and 2.2 kg harvested reads 81.8%
F20 Realised price per kg P0 Rand ÷ harvested kg is the price actually obtained 1.8 kg for R1,260 reads R700/kg
F21 Sell-through > 1 alert P1 Impossible sell-through means missing harvest rows 110% returns a flag naming the period and the likely missing harvest date
F22 Cost of the next machine P1 Capital decision needs the marginal kg, not the average machines 4→5 prints +2.05 kg/7d and +R4,001.88/month
F23 Decision stamp with parameter snapshot P0 Yield changes must not rewrite history decision row keeps the yield_g and price_kg used, readable after Settings change
F24 Monthly CSV snapshot with a hash P1 The sheet is not a database; the snapshot is the record CSV written to AI-Drive with a sha256 line; re-export of unchanged data matches the hash
F25 Retirement notice inside the sheet P2 Five surfaces disagree because nobody is told which one counts Start Here names the five superseded files, the archive folder, and the date of the move

Architecture and API surface

The system is one Zoho Sheet plus a monthly CSV snapshot. There is no service to build.

Package layout, and the one rule that keeps it honest:

TLF_Reporting/TLF Grow Room Master      (the workbook; 9 tabs, in this order)
  1 Start Here        what it is, the eight parameters named, who owns it, what it excludes
  2 Settings          the input block — the only typed numbers
  3 Capacity          machines 1–12, formulas only
  4 Demand            one row per client per period
  5 Plan              13 periods, formulas only
  6 Prospects         kg/day in, machines and start period out
  7 Machines          the decision rows, stamped
  8 Yield Log         append-only actuals
  9 Invoices          paste lane + sell-through + realised price
AI-Drive/Agent-Output/tlf/grow-room/<YYYY-MM>.csv    the immutable monthly snapshot

Import rule: tabs 1 and 2 may be typed into; tabs 3–7 are formulas over Settings and Demand; tab 8 is append-only; tab 9 is paste-only. A formula in tab 3–7 that does not trace to Settings is a defect, and the workbook is rejected by the reviewer who checks it.

Command and access table:

Action Who may do it How
Change a parameter GM only edit Settings; changed_by and changed_at are typed, not formulas
Add a demand row GM, grower one row per client per period; the unique key rejects a second
Add a harvest row grower append at the bottom of Yield Log; corrections are new flagged rows
Paste invoices finance paste into Invoices with source filled; never edit an existing invoice_no
Read everything any TLF staff member view-only link
Export the monthly CSV Bruce (agent) or the GM read-only export, hash recorded, filed in AI-Drive

Conventions the build must keep:

  • Units in every header (kg/7d, g/tray, R/kg, R/month). A header without a unit is a defect.
  • Named ranges for the eight parameters; no bare constants inside formulas.
  • CEILING at each step, per I3, and the printed value is the ceiling, never the unrounded number.
  • Period keys are ISO 7-day keys (2026-W44), sortable as text, never a date the sheet has to parse.
  • 4.33 is a display divisor for "per month" only. No decision is computed on a month; decisions are computed on 7-day periods.

Failure modes

Failure What the user sees Control Who checks it
Capacity typed instead of computed a number that survives a Settings change I2: reviewer edits yield_g and confirms every capacity cell moves GM on sign-off
Double-counted harvest (the 17.28 kg error) capacity 2× the room, promises missed by half F5 space-time guard rejects the entry and names the ceiling grower weekly
Duplicate demand row a machine ordered for a client counted twice Demand unique key on (period, client, channel) GM at entry
Duplicate invoice paste sell-through above 100% Invoices unique key on invoice_no; F21 flag finance monthly
Inconsistent rounding 246 trays beside 491 spaces F4 prints trays, spaces and machines from one rounding chain reviewer on the tab
Stale parameters mid-cycle a plan that no longer matches the room F23 stamp; the plan prints the parameter version used GM weekly
Invoice lane empty because Books refuses sell-through blank, price unknown paste lane with provenance; the blank is labelled as a missing feed, not zero finance monthly
Harvest not logged for a period capacity looks used, yield looks absent F21 cross-checks harvest rows against sown batches grower weekly
Failed trays unreported realised yield drifts below plan with no explanation F16 failed-tray count per row, F17 realised reliability grower weekly
Workbook edited by someone outside the role matrix history shows an edit with no owner WorkDrive sharing set to view-only for everyone but GM, grower, finance Bruce monthly
Five old sheets still circulating two answers to the same question F25 notice on Start Here, archive folder, one link GM on retirement

Security, permissions and the audit record

Principals: GM (owner), grower (operator), finance (invoice lane), Bruce (agent, read and export), TLF staff (read).

Role Settings Demand Yield Log Invoices Export
GM write write read read yes
Grower read write write read no
Finance read read read write yes
Bruce (agent) read read read read yes
TLF staff read read read read no

What is stored: client names, delivery volumes, price per kilogram, per-machine yield and failure rates — commercially sensitive, not personal data. No ID numbers, no bank details, no health data.

Anchored outside the system: the monthly CSV in AI-Drive with a sha256 of its content, and the WorkDrive version history. Together these give a defensible record of what the sheet said in a given month.

How strong the audit claim is, stated honestly: WorkDrive version history is evidence, not proof. The owner of the workbook can restore an older revision and the history shows a restore, not what was overwritten. The monthly CSV plus its hash makes quiet edits visible from the next snapshot onward, and does nothing about edits made between snapshots. No signature is claimed and none should be.

Build order by dependency

Stage A — skeleton and input block. Work: workbook in the TLF_Reporting folder, nine tabs, the eight parameters typed once in Settings, named ranges, headers carrying units. Exit test: every later tab reads cycle days, yield, reliability and price from Settings, and no constant appears in a formula outside the Settings tab.

Stage B — capacity and the guard. Work: machines 1–12 table, the rounding chain, the space-time guard. Runs in parallel with Stage C's data entry. Exit test: 4 machines reads 8.21 kg per 7-day period and 7 machines reads 14.36, matching an independent recomputation to two decimals; entering 17.28 kg is rejected by the guard.

Stage C — demand and plan. Work: Demand with its unique key, the 13-period plan, headroom, percent used, the verdict row. Exit test: entering 14 kg in a single period against today's 8.21 kg capacity prints headroom −5.79, 170% used, and names 7 machines as the covering count.

Stage D — prospects and the machine decision. Work: kg/day and delivery days in, machines and first servable period out; the stamped decision row; both price bases. Exit test: 2 kg/day for 7 days returns 7 machines and 2 kg/day for 5 days returns 5; a decision row still reads its original yield after Settings is changed.

Stage E — yield log, invoices and the snapshot. Work: append-only log with g/tray and variance, failed-tray counts, the paste lane with provenance, sell-through and realised price, the monthly CSV export with its hash. Exit test: a full month of harvest rows and pasted invoices produces g/tray, variance, sell-through and R/kg with no formula pointing at an empty cell, and the exported CSV's hash reproduces on a second export of unchanged data.

Stage F — retirement and signposting. Work: archive folder _Retired — superseded by TLF Grow Room Master under TLF_Reporting, the five model sheets moved and set read-only, the Start Here notice written. Exit test: the TLF GM opens one link, finds every number the five files used to answer, and no live folder still presents a superseded model sheet as current.

Decisions register

# Question Recommended answer Cost of each alternative
1 Retire the five model sheets into an archive folder, or leave them where they are? Retire, never delete. Read-only archive, one signposting line. Left in place, six surfaces keep disagreeing and the GM quotes whichever is open
2 Planning yield: 120 g, measured 131 g, or funding 140 g? 120 g until four consecutive periods beat it At 140 g every promise date is missed by a sixth; at 131 g the plan flatters itself on a good month
3 Machine price basis for payback: R13,294 build cost or R45,000 franchisee price? Build cost for our own capital; franchisee price in the franchise pack One basis for both under-prices the franchise or overstates our payback by 3.38×
4 Does a signed prospect count toward the machine trigger? No — confirmed orders only, prospects on their own line Machines bought on hope, sitting at 40% utilisation like the last four
5 Fix the Zoho Books seat so invoices can be read by API? Yes — sell-through is the only check on what we earn per kg The invoice tab stays hand-fed and sell-through is never reviewed
6 Who owns the workbook: the GM or the grower? GM owns it, grower writes the log. One name on the file. Shared ownership means neither changes a parameter when the room changes
7 Which figure is the room's official capacity: 8.21 kg or 8.64 kg per 7-day period? 8.21 kg — the 95% rule is a standing policy, and 8.64 assumes every tray succeeds Quoting 8.64 makes every failure look like a performance problem instead of a reliability number
